Topic mode · generated August 31, 2026 at 19:30 UTC · 18 items

Early Detection and Surveillance of the SARS-CoV-2 Variant BA.3.2 — Worldwide, November 2024–February 2026

Mila Shakya, Kevin C. Ma, Laura J. Hughes et al.. Early Detection and Surveillance of the SARS-CoV-2 Variant BA.3.2 — Worldwide, November 2024–February 2026. MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report. 2026

The paper examined the emergence and early spread of the SARS-CoV-2 variant BA.3.2, which was first identified in South Africa and subsequently detected across multiple countries. Researchers employed a multimodal genomic surveillance strategy incorporating traveler-based surveillance, clinical specimen testing, airplane wastewater sampling, and community wastewater monitoring to track the variant's introduction and circulation within the United States and internationally. BA.3.2 carries a large number of substitutions and deletions in the spike protein relative to the antigens used in current COVID-19 vaccines, raising concern that it may reduce protection conferred by prior infection or vaccination. Surveillance detected the variant in traveler swabs, clinical patient samples, and wastewater samples spanning numerous states, with international reporting from more than twenty countries. The authors conclude that continued genomic surveillance is essential to monitor the evolution of SARS-CoV-2 and assess the public health implications of this variant.

Metagenomic Sequencing for Wastewater-Based Surveillance: Modeling and Experimental Approaches for Determining Limit of Detection

Xiao A, Besse K, Connors D et al.. Metagenomic Sequencing for Wastewater-Based Surveillance: Modeling and Experimental Approaches for Determining Limit of Detection. 2026

This paper investigated the performance characteristics of metagenomic next-generation sequencing as a tool for wastewater-based surveillance, with a particular focus on establishing limits of detection. The researchers developed a computational model of a local wastewater system and conducted two types of experiments: laboratory-spiked experiments in which a test organism was introduced into real-world wastewater samples under controlled conditions, and system-spiked experiments in which the organism was introduced at an actual source location within a real wastewater system. The study found that detection performance varies depending on the sequencing method chosen and the data analysis pipeline applied. Importantly, the findings highlight that site-specific method characterization is necessary when deploying metagenomic sequencing for surveillance, because differences in wastewater system configuration, background microbial communities, and sequencing inhibitors can meaningfully affect results. The work addresses a key gap in understanding why metagenomic sequencing, despite its potential for broad pathogen detection beyond predefined targets, has not yet been widely adopted for routine wastewater surveillance.

Wastewater Metagenomics for Antimicrobial Resistance and Pathogen Surveillance: A Bibliometric Analysis.

Zheng Y, Ma N, Zhao B et al.. Wastewater Metagenomics for Antimicrobial Resistance and Pathogen Surveillance: A Bibliometric Analysis.. Microorganisms. 2026

This study examined the global research landscape at the intersection of wastewater metagenomics, antimicrobial resistance, and pathogen surveillance using a bibliometric approach. The researchers retrieved over a thousand publications from a major scientific database and applied network analysis software to map publication trends, country contributions, keyword evolution, and literature co-citation patterns across roughly fifteen years. Publication output grew dramatically over the study period, with the majority of output concentrated in the most recent years, reflecting accelerated interest following the COVID-19 pandemic and the rise of wastewater-based epidemiological surveillance. Thematic analysis revealed a progression from basic detection of antibiotic resistance genes in wastewater treatment systems toward broader concerns about dissemination mechanisms and One Health frameworks, while co-citation analysis indicated that integration between environmental monitoring and public health literature remains limited. The authors conclude that key priorities for the field include standardizing analytical methods, incorporating artificial intelligence for resistome analysis, improving participation from underrepresented regions, and operationalizing One Health surveillance to translate wastewater monitoring data into actionable public health interventions.

Clade-specific multiplex digital PCR assay for monkeypox virus detection in spike-in wastewater samples.

Pitton M, Gan C, Schmidhalter P et al.. Clade-specific multiplex digital PCR assay for monkeypox virus detection in spike-in wastewater samples.. Access microbiology. 2026

The paper developed and validated a multiplex digital PCR assay for detecting and quantifying monkeypox virus in wastewater samples, with the ability to distinguish between major viral clades and subclades. This capability is epidemiologically important because different clades vary in transmissibility and mortality, and wastewater-based surveillance offers population-level monitoring independent of individual clinical testing. The four-plex assay was evaluated for linearity, analytical sensitivity, and specificity using spike-in wastewater samples, demonstrating low detection limits across all targets. The authors conclude that the assay is suitable for both clinical and wastewater surveillance applications, offering a tool for tracking monkeypox virus dynamics at the clade level.

Validation of molecular detection of &lt;i&gt;Candida auris&lt;/i&gt; from wastewater.

Rodríguez Stewart RM, Litvintseva AP, Chavez J et al.. Validation of molecular detection of &lt;i&gt;Candida auris&lt;/i&gt; from wastewater.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

The paper studied whether an existing quantitative PCR assay developed for clinical *Candida auris* colonization screening could be adapted to detect this multidrug-resistant fungal pathogen in wastewater samples. The researchers validated the qPCR workflow using a wastewater matrix, assessing amplification efficiency, sensitivity, and reproducibility. The assay demonstrated high PCR efficiency, a very low limit of detection, and strong reproducibility across tests. These findings indicate that the clinical colonization assay can be reliably repurposed for wastewater surveillance, offering a more cost-effective approach to monitoring *C. auris* prevalence across entire healthcare systems without relying solely on individual patient screening.

Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data

Marlin D Figgins, Trevor Bedford. Inferring variant-specific effective reproduction numbers from combined case and sequencing data. eLife. 2026

This paper addresses the challenge of accurately estimating the transmission dynamics of co-circulating SARS-CoV-2 variants. The authors developed a method that jointly analyzes confirmed case counts alongside genetic sequencing data to estimate variant-specific effective reproduction numbers and variant frequencies simultaneously, rather than relying on sequence frequency data alone. The approach also allows for inferring structured relationships across time series, enabling estimation of fixed growth advantages for specific variants across different geographic locations. Applying this framework to data from United States states over more than a year of the pandemic, they estimated effective reproduction numbers for multiple variants of concern and interest and identified consistent growth advantages of particular variants across locations. The work demonstrates that combining case and sequencing data yields richer epidemiological insights than either data source alone.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global crisis of antimicrobial resistance as framed by the World Health Organization's priority lists of antibiotic-resistant bacteria, which were established to direct research and drug development efforts. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms by which these pathogens evade existing antibiotics, including reduced membrane permeability, efflux pump overexpression, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have largely failed and assesses emerging therapeutic strategies such as artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds, while addressing practical challenges including toxicity, delivery, dosing, resistance emergence, and regulatory barriers. The authors also emphasize the importance of antimicrobial stewardship, surveillance systems, and a One Health framework that integrates human, animal, and environmental health sectors. Their overarching conclusion is that overcoming resistance in priority pathogens will require coordinated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health policy.

Mobile Genetic Elements as Central Drivers of Antimicrobial Resistance: Molecular Mechanisms, Evolutionary Ecology, One Health Implications and Control Strategies

Hemayet Hossain, Md. Hasan Ali, Tanvir Ahmad et al.. Mobile Genetic Elements as Central Drivers of Antimicrobial Resistance: Molecular Mechanisms, Evolutionary Ecology, One Health Implications and Control Strategies. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines the role of mobile genetic elements in driving the global spread of antimicrobial resistance across bacterial species and ecosystems. The authors conducted a critical synthesis of recent genomic, epidemiological, and ecological studies, including case studies of clinically significant resistance outbreaks, to evaluate the types, mechanisms, and ecological drivers of mobile genetic element-mediated resistance gene dissemination. The review finds that plasmids, integrons, transposons, insertion sequences, integrative and conjugative elements, and prophages collectively act as hubs for the capture, recombination, and stabilization of antibiotic resistance genes, enabling the emergence of multidrug-resistant and extensively drug-resistant pathogens. Ecological pressures such as antibiotics, heavy metals, and biocides further promote mobile genetic element activity, and One Health transmission pathways connect human, animal, and environmental reservoirs of resistance. The authors argue that linking these molecular insights to applied strategies — including genomic surveillance, mobile genetic element-targeted inhibitors, phage therapy, and CRISPR-based interventions — is essential for designing effective responses to the antimicrobial resistance crisis.

Postbiotics and phage synergy in precision oral microbiome engineering: systems biology strategies targeting Streptococcus mutans in dental caries

Guhanraj Radhamanalan. Postbiotics and phage synergy in precision oral microbiome engineering: systems biology strategies targeting Streptococcus mutans in dental caries. Frontiers in Molecular Biosciences. 2026

This review examines the potential of postbiotics, defined as non-viable microbial products and metabolic derivatives, as targeted strategies for managing dental caries by modulating the oral microbiome. The authors focus on how postbiotic compounds such as organic acids, bacteriocins, and exopolysaccharides can reduce the acidogenic activity and biofilm integrity of Streptococcus mutans while preserving commensal microbial communities and influencing host mucosal immunity. The review synthesizes current knowledge on combining postbiotics with bacteriophage-based therapies, nanoformulation delivery systems, and bioadhesive matrices to improve site-specific efficacy against oral dysbiosis. It further highlights how multi-omics approaches, systems biology modeling, and artificial intelligence tools could enable personalized caries interventions tailored to individual microbial signatures, including modulation of viral–bacterial interactions and horizontal gene transfer that contribute to antimicrobial resistance. The authors conclude that realizing the clinical potential of these precision strategies will require resolving translational challenges related to formulation stability, safety, regulatory approval, and comprehensive profiling of the oral virome.

Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations

Ojonugwa Precious John, Kayode Olayinka Afolabi, Anayochukwu Chibuike Ngene et al.. Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations. Microorganisms. 2026

This paper examines the dual nature of *Enterococcus* species as both potentially beneficial microorganisms and safety concerns in human health and food systems. The authors conducted a comprehensive review of the existing literature on enterococci as commensal gut bacteria found across diverse hosts, environments, and food sources. The review covers their probiotic applications, including pathogen control, microbiome modulation, food fermentation enhancement, and production of bioactive compounds such as bacteriocins with antimicrobial properties. At the same time, the paper addresses serious safety concerns, particularly the association of certain enterococci with healthcare-associated infections and their role in spreading antimicrobial resistance. The authors conclude that while enterococci hold considerable promise as probiotics, antibiotic alternatives, and protective cultures, rigorous safety evaluations are essential before their broader deployment in agrifood and healthcare contexts.

Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective

Akshay Pal, Andrew Lucas, Umang Mehta. Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how electron-electron interactions modify density three-point correlation functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids, which are known to encode topological information about the Fermi sea geometry in the non-interacting case. The authors employ the coadjoint-orbit effective field theory framework, which reformulates fermionic loop calculations as tree-level diagrams in a bosonized language and allows systematic inclusion of generalized Landau interactions. By working to linear order in the generalized Landau interaction parameter, they demonstrate that a contribution proportional to the band curvature at the Fermi momentum emerges, altering the nonanalytic structure of the density three-point function beyond what is seen in either the free fermion case or the interacting Galilean-invariant system. This finding implies that interactions can, in principle, modify or obscure the topological information that the density three-point correlator was thought to reliably detect in non-interacting systems.

How Long-Range Tails Reshape Non-Hermitian Spectra

Ding Gu, Zhanpeng Fu, Yu-Min Hu et al.. How Long-Range Tails Reshape Non-Hermitian Spectra. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how exponentially decaying long-range hoppings, which are commonly truncated in tight-binding models, affect non-Hermitian systems under open boundary conditions. The authors demonstrate that even infinitesimally small long-range hopping tails can nonperturbatively reconstruct both the spectrum and eigenstates of short-range non-Hermitian systems, meaning the standard truncation approximation can fail catastrophically in this context. The underlying mechanism arises from a competition between the decay length of the long-range hoppings and the localization length of non-Hermitian skin modes, producing a sharp transition as the hopping decay rate is varied. To describe the reconstructed spectra, the authors introduce a squeezed generalized Brillouin zone framework in one dimension and a squeezed amoeba formulation in two or higher dimensions. They additionally show that long-range hoppings can qualitatively alter the Green's function in ways that are experimentally detectable.

Advancing Interaction-Sensitive Feature Selection: Novel Relief-Based Algorithms, Expanded Comparisons, and Recommendations for Biomedical Data Mining

Kia Kazemi-Nia, Harsh Bandhey, Philip J. Freda et al.. Advancing Interaction-Sensitive Feature Selection: Novel Relief-Based Algorithms, Expanded Comparisons, and Recommendations for Biomedical Data Mining. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examined Relief-based algorithms (RBAs), a class of filter-based feature selection methods that can detect feature interactions while remaining computationally efficient, with a focus on their application to high-dimensional biomedical and genomic data. The researchers refactored and expanded the scikit-rebate Python package to include existing and newly proposed RBA variants implementing alternative strategies for neighbor selection and feature scoring, then conducted systematic benchmark comparisons across simulated genomic datasets varying in sample size, feature count, heritability, and association type. The refactoring effort produced substantial reductions in algorithm runtimes, and the newly introduced variants were among the strongest performers overall. RBAs using "far" scoring excelled at detecting two-way interactions but were less sensitive to main effects, while several algorithms including the newly introduced MultiSWRFDB demonstrated strong performance across both main effects and higher-order epistatic interactions. The study concludes that robust RBAs capable of retaining both main effects and feature interactions are valuable tools for preserving predictive signals prior to downstream biomedical modeling.

ExoMOD II. A Statistical Model of Transit Timing Variations in Kepler Multi-Planet Systems

David Nesvorny, Daniel A. Yahalomi, David Kipping et al.. ExoMOD II. A Statistical Model of Transit Timing Variations in Kepler Multi-Planet Systems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper uses a forward-modeling population framework called ExoMOD, calibrated on Kepler photometric data, to statistically predict transit timing variations (TTVs) arising from gravitational interactions among planets in close-in multi-planet systems, then compares these predictions against TTVs actually measured in the Kepler dataset. The study finds that planet-planet interactions are insufficient to explain the observed short-period TTV signals, implying that most such signals must originate from some other physical mechanism. For long-period TTVs, which are more plausibly attributable to genuine gravitational perturbations between planets, the analysis reveals that singly-transiting planets harbor TTV-inducing companions nearly as frequently as doubly-transiting systems, arguing against orbital architectures dominated by intrinsic single-planet systems. Systems with higher observed multiplicities show elevated rates of long-period TTVs and low orbital gap complexity, suggesting their architectures preserve a record of formation conditions. The work positions this statistical TTV framework as a tool for creating a tighter feedback loop between observational data and theories of planetary system formation.

ExoMOD I. A Forward Model for the Orbital Architecture of Kepler Multi-Planet Systems

David Nesvorny, Daniel A. Yahalomi, David Kipping et al.. ExoMOD I. A Forward Model for the Orbital Architecture of Kepler Multi-Planet Systems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops a forward model to characterize the orbital architecture of close-in planetary systems observed by the Kepler telescope, addressing the inherent ambiguity introduced by the transit method's geometric bias toward edge-on orbits. The authors parameterize orbital excitation using Angular Momentum Deficit and model distributions of planet radii, masses, and orbital periods, fitting the model to Kepler's catalog using a nested sampling algorithm. They find that the orbital period distributions of systems with single detected planets differ statistically from those with multiple detected planets, potentially due to Kepler's observational baseline, and that high-multiplicity systems show correlated period ratios consistent with retained memory of formation conditions. The gap complexity metric proves useful for constraining orbital excitation distributions, and observed radius monotonicity trends are attributed largely to non-detections of smaller planets. The study concludes that a typical FGK dwarf in the Kepler field hosts on average roughly two to three planets within the studied radius and period ranges, with approximately half of detected planetary systems having at least one inner or intermediate-period planet missed by Kepler.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the computational complexity of a hypothesis testing problem: distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph. To tackle this, the authors analyze low-degree polynomial threshold functions on random regular graphs, providing the first such analysis for this detection problem. A key technical contribution along the way is a new characterization of the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic lengths, which generalizes several prior results on cycle count distributions in random regular graphs and random lifts. The findings contribute to understanding the statistical and computational limits of detecting structured random graph models within the framework of low-degree polynomial tests.

isothermal amplification LAMP diagnostics

Research progress on nucleic acid amplification–based detection technologies for phytopathogenic fungi

Baoyu Wang, Mei Liu, Hui Wang et al.. Research progress on nucleic acid amplification–based detection technologies for phytopathogenic fungi. Applied Microbiology and Biotechnology. 2026

This review examines the landscape of nucleic acid amplification-based technologies developed for detecting phytopathogenic fungi, which are responsible for the vast majority of plant disease infections and cause substantial annual losses in global crop yields. The authors surveyed both thermal cycling methods, including conventional PCR, real-time quantitative PCR, and droplet digital PCR, and isothermal amplification platforms such as loop-mediated isothermal amplification and recombinase polymerase amplification. The review also covers next-generation hybrid approaches that couple isothermal amplification with CRISPR/Cas systems, such as RPA-CRISPR and LAMP-CRISPR assays. By evaluating the principles, strengths, and limitations of each technology, the authors conclude that isothermal and CRISPR-assisted platforms are particularly well-suited for field-deployable diagnostics with minimal instrumentation requirements, and that integrated workflows using these methods can support early diagnosis and precision management of fungal plant diseases.

Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.

Li HM, Qin ZQ, Lv S et al.. Rapid and Sensitive Detection of &lt;i&gt;Schistosoma mansoni&lt;/i&gt; in the Intermediate Snail Hosts Using Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (LAMP) Diagnostics.. Tropical medicine and infectious disease. 2026

The paper developed and evaluated a loop-mediated isothermal amplification assay targeting the mitochondrial NADH dehydrogenase subunit 1 gene of *Schistosoma mansoni* to improve detection of the parasite in its intermediate snail hosts. The assay was optimized under straightforward isothermal conditions and tested against both laboratory-infected *Biomphalaria* snails and field-collected snails from two sub-Saharan African countries, with performance compared to microscopy, conventional PCR, and real-time PCR. In laboratory settings the assay achieved complete specificity and high sensitivity, outperforming traditional microscopy and performing comparably to real-time PCR, while in field surveys it detected a higher proportion of infected snails than conventional PCR and identified infections entirely missed by parasitological methods. The authors conclude that this LAMP assay is a sensitive, specific, and practically deployable diagnostic tool suitable for routine surveillance of schistosomiasis transmission in snail populations.

Development of a Novel &lt;i&gt;Pseudoperonospora cubensis&lt;/i&gt;-Specific LAMP Assay for Rapid Field Detection and Surveillance of Cucurbit Downy Mildew.

Waliullah S, Anwar MT, Ali E. Development of a Novel &lt;i&gt;Pseudoperonospora cubensis&lt;/i&gt;-Specific LAMP Assay for Rapid Field Detection and Surveillance of Cucurbit Downy Mildew.. Journal of fungi (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

Abstract unavailable. Open the link to read more.

Broad-Temperature Polymerase in Nucleic Acid Amplification-Based Diagnostics: From Thermal Precision to Dynamic Conditions.

Hamidizadeh M, Bier FF. Broad-Temperature Polymerase in Nucleic Acid Amplification-Based Diagnostics: From Thermal Precision to Dynamic Conditions.. ACS sensors. 2026

This paper investigated nucleic acid amplification across a wide temperature range as an alternative to conventional methods requiring precise thermal control. The researchers developed a LAMP-based amplification approach using a Bst polymerase with strong strand displacement activity and broad thermostability, enabling DNA synthesis across a temperature spectrum from approximately the lower end of isothermal LAMP conditions to temperatures typical of PCR thermocycling. They demonstrated that amplification could be initiated at both high and low starting temperatures and sustained even under dynamic, fluctuating temperature profiles. The system was validated for detecting SARS-CoV-2 and *M. tuberculosis* genomic material, confirming its diagnostic utility. The authors propose this broad-temperature amplification platform as a promising tool for point-of-care and field diagnostics where maintaining strict thermal conditions is impractical.

Quantitative Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (qLAMP) for the Rapid Discrimination of Normal and Cancerous Tissue Models: An Arduino-Based Portable Cancer Detection System Assisted by a pH Microelectrode.

Bravo-González S, Reyes-Cortés LM, Pérez-Alvarez KA et al.. Quantitative Loop-Mediated Isothermal Amplification (qLAMP) for the Rapid Discrimination of Normal and Cancerous Tissue Models: An Arduino-Based Portable Cancer Detection System Assisted by a pH Microelectrode.. Biosensors. 2026

This paper investigated whether a portable, low-cost point-of-care system based on loop-mediated isothermal amplification could distinguish cancerous from normal tissue models through quantitative monitoring of gene expression. The researchers developed an Arduino-based device that tracks the trajectory of the LAMP reaction during its early phase, deriving a rate-of-amplification metric called the m-P3 value, and applied it to measure expression of four cancer-relevant genes across three-dimensional tissue spheroids derived from breast cancer, prostate cancer, and normal fibroblast cell lines. Using principal component analysis and clustering of the resulting m-P3 value sets, they demonstrated that the system could statistically discriminate between the different cell line-derived tissue models, with findings corroborated by quantitative PCR and immunostaining assays. The study establishes a proof-of-concept for using quantitative LAMP in a portable diagnostic platform capable of identifying or differentiating cancer tissues based on their gene expression profiles. The authors suggest the approach could be extended to other diseases characterized by altered gene expression in tissues or bodily fluids.

Numerical study of the ITER divertor plasma with the B2-EIRENE code package

V. Kotov. Numerical study of the ITER divertor plasma with the B2-EIRENE code package. Dokumentenrepositorium der RUB (Ruhr University Bochum). 2026

The abstract is too thin to summarize, as it contains only bibliographic and administrative information about a report with no description of the study's methods, scope, or findings.

The analysis of natural gemstones and their synthetic counterparts using analytical spectroscopy methods

Raabell Shah. The analysis of natural gemstones and their synthetic counterparts using analytical spectroscopy methods. Edinburgh Napier Research Repository (Edinburgh Napier University). 2026

This study examined whether analytical spectroscopy methods, specifically Raman spectroscopy, could reliably distinguish natural gemstones from their laboratory-grown synthetic counterparts across four major commercial stones: ruby, sapphire, emerald, and spinel. The researchers evaluated both a laboratory Raman spectrometer equipped with two different laser wavelengths and a portable Raman system, comparing their effectiveness as identification tools for gemstones produced by various manufacturing methods. The laboratory instrument successfully differentiated natural from synthetic gemstones and could determine geographic origin for stones of the same color, while emerald analysis required the shorter wavelength laser specifically. The portable system could distinguish between different stone types but lacked the sensitivity needed to differentiate natural from synthetic specimens. Raman spectroscopy was found to be a reliable and practical approach overall, given its non-destructive nature, minimal sample requirements, and reproducible results consistent with reference literature values.

Porous material engineering through synthesis for smart sensor systems

Se Jin Choi, Sang Yoon Park, Kang Hyeon Kim et al.. Porous material engineering through synthesis for smart sensor systems. Microsystems & Nanoengineering. 2026

This review paper examines porous materials as a class of functional materials for next-generation sensor platforms, focusing on how their high surface areas, tunable pore structures, and chemical functionalization enable improved analyte detection. The authors systematically survey representative porous material categories, including metal oxides, polymers, and carbon-based systems, and analyze their synthesis strategies such as sol-gel processes, template-assisted methods, three-dimensional printing, and light-material interactions. Fundamental sensing mechanisms are discussed across electrical, electrochemical, and optical transduction pathways, connecting porous architecture design to enhanced sensitivity, selectivity, and lower detection limits. Applications reviewed span environmental monitoring, biomedical diagnostics, and smart packaging, where porous material-based sensors are reported to show faster response times, better analyte discrimination, and greater operational stability. The review synthesizes design principles and fabrication methodologies intended to guide future research and support practical deployment of advanced sensing technologies.

Viral metagenomics of synanthropic urban bats: A surveillance strategy for uncovering potentially zoonotic viruses

Juliana Amorim Conselheiro, Filipe Romero Rebello Moreira, Gisely Toledo Barone et al.. Viral metagenomics of synanthropic urban bats: A surveillance strategy for uncovering potentially zoonotic viruses. One Health. 2026

Bats harbor a wide range of viruses with zoonotic potential, yet systematic surveillance of bat populations remains limited, particularly in low- and middle-income countries. This study developed a framework that integrates viral metagenomics into an existing passive rabies surveillance program in Brazil, using an algorithm to select representative bat specimens from thousands collected across a large state and subjecting paired tissue samples to nanopore sequencing. The approach detected nearly a hundred viral contigs spanning multiple viral families of public health concern, including coronaviruses, arenaviruses, and paramyxoviruses. Strikingly, the framework uncovered a previously unknown filovirus in bats from the Americas, demonstrating its capacity to identify novel pathogens relevant to epidemic preparedness. The method was further validated by multisectoral One Health experts as a scalable and cost-effective model suitable for national expansion and adaptable to resource-limited settings.

Advances in low-cost electrochemical sensors for healthcare monitoring and diagnostics

B. Anuthra, Jasmine Ratan, Priyanka Gupta et al.. Advances in low-cost electrochemical sensors for healthcare monitoring and diagnostics. Advances in Industrial and Engineering Chemistry. 2026

This review examines recent progress in low-cost electrochemical sensors designed for healthcare diagnostics and point-of-care testing, particularly in resource-limited settings. The authors surveyed advances in sensor design, materials selection, and fabrication methods, focusing on strategies that reduce costs while maintaining analytical performance. Key approaches identified include the use of carbon-based materials, conductive polymers, and plant-derived bioactive molecules as electrode components, alongside scalable manufacturing techniques such as screen printing and three-dimensional printing to produce disposable, portable devices. The review also covers the integration of these sensors with digital platforms for real-time monitoring and their application in detecting biomarkers relevant to chronic and infectious diseases including diabetes and cardiovascular conditions. The authors conclude that while challenges around sensor stability, specificity, and commercialization remain, affordable electrochemical sensors hold strong potential to broaden diagnostic accessibility and improve patient outcomes.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a fundamental open question in quantum resource theory: how much "magic" (nonstabilizerness), the resource enabling universal quantum computation beyond Clifford circuits, is genuinely nonlocal — residing in the correlations between two parties rather than in their individual local degrees of freedom? The authors solve this problem exactly for the stabilizer fidelity by showing that the nonlocal magic of any pure multiqubit state equals the distance of its entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. They further establish an unexpected connection to entanglement embezzlement, proving that a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. A key finding is that the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum distributes its weight across factor-of-two windows of rank, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states — both exhibiting identical logarithmic entanglement scaling — carry unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The results make nonlocal magic an operationally meaningful and practically accessible quantity, computable via tensor-network methods and measurable experimentally through entanglement spectroscopy.

Hierarchical organization governs nonlinear mechanical reversibility in $ι$-carrageenan gels

Thomas Gibaud, Loïc Hilliou. Hierarchical organization governs nonlinear mechanical reversibility in $ι$-carrageenan gels. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated how the molecular and mesoscale organization of ι-carrageenan gels controls their mechanical behavior under deformation, a question that had remained poorly understood despite the widespread use of these thermoreversible polysaccharide networks. The researchers characterized gels formed in potassium chloride solutions across a range of temperatures using both linear and nonlinear oscillatory rheology alongside normal-force measurements. They found that deeper quenches produce stiffer gels exhibiting pronounced strain stiffening before yielding, accompanied by negative normal forces that reveal the buildup of internal stresses. Strikingly, large deformations irreversibly suppressed strain stiffening while leaving the small-strain elastic modulus intact, indicating a partial mechanical reversibility that distinguishes two structural levels. The authors interpret this behavior through a hierarchical network model in which a robust intermolecular network governs linear elasticity while a more fragile mesoscale organization enables cooperative chain alignment and stress amplification, underscoring that nonlinear mechanics in such gels arise from reversible hierarchical structures rather than molecular connectivity alone.

COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing

Raghul Sugumar, Amrit Gopinath. COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a methodological problem in evaluating multi-agent routing systems, where changing a team's composition confounds attempts to isolate the effect of routing decisions from downstream processing changes. The authors introduce an evaluation framework called COVER that establishes a fixed public information boundary, a frozen downstream processing stack, and a predeclared set of legal team compositions before any outcomes are generated, enabling identification of routing effects separate from other system changes. The approach distinguishes between prospective evaluations, which are binding and assumption-free for pairwise policy comparisons, and retrospective analyses, which lack the same inferential standing. Experiments across multiple benchmarks and task domains show that the framework can detect genuine routing improvements, but also exposes cases where apparent gains disappear or fail to meet predeclared performance criteria once the methodology is properly applied. The authors present COVER as an auditable measurement methodology that reveals selection headroom honestly rather than manufacturing spurious routing wins.

Acquire, Repair, Preserve: A Diagnosis-Guided Post-Training Recipe for Small-Model Dialogue Game Agents

Nan Li. Acquire, Repair, Preserve: A Diagnosis-Guided Post-Training Recipe for Small-Model Dialogue Game Agents. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how small language models can be improved as agents in interactive dialogue games, where success requires maintaining state across conversational turns, interpreting feedback, and selecting valid actions under dynamic constraints. The authors analyzed failure modes of a small open-weight model in a dialogue game challenge and found that errors fell into distinct categories, including repeated guesses, malformed actions, and violations of recently received feedback. To address these failures, they developed a three-stage post-training recipe: broad game participation through supervised fine-tuning, targeted repair of mechanically verifiable errors using turn-local preference pairs within a specific game family, and preservation of general capabilities outside the dialogue game domain. The approach substantially improved performance on both public and closed in-domain evaluations while approximately maintaining baseline aggregate performance on static benchmarks. The results indicate that supervised fine-tuning contributes the majority of capability gains, that turn-local preference learning transfers primarily within the targeted game family rather than broadly, and that out-of-domain dialogue game performance remains limited.

Follow-up of SN 2025wny V: Lens Modelling and Cosmography of a Strongly Lensed Superluminous Supernova at $z = 2.015$ using Space Data

Edvard Mörtsell, Joel Johansson, Ariel Goobar et al.. Follow-up of SN 2025wny V: Lens Modelling and Cosmography of a Strongly Lensed Superluminous Supernova at $z = 2.015$ using Space Data. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents a lensing and cosmographic analysis of a strongly lensed Type I superluminous supernova multiply imaged by two foreground galaxies, making it a rare and valuable system for cosmology. Using imaging from the Hubble Space Telescope and the James Webb Space Telescope, the researchers modeled the lens system with elliptical power-law mass distributions and an external shear component, incorporating both the supernova image positions and the surface-brightness distribution of the lensed host galaxy. The analysis accounted for microlensing by stars in the lens galaxies and found total magnifications spanning a broad range. By combining the lens models with spectroscopically and photometrically measured time delays, the study derived constraints on the Hubble constant that fall between approximately 67 and 71 kilometers per second per megaparsec depending on the assumed lens mass parameterization, though these values remain sensitive to the adopted priors, density slope assumptions, and potential contributions from the large-scale environment. The authors conclude that future stellar kinematic measurements and better characterization of the lens environment will enhance the system's power as an independent cosmological probe.

Kerr nonlinearity and three-wave mixing in superconducting resonators hosting Al-InAs weak links

Vittorio Buccheri, Ivo P. C. Cools, Nermin Trnjanin et al.. Kerr nonlinearity and three-wave mixing in superconducting resonators hosting Al-InAs weak links. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates superconducting microwave resonators that incorporate weak links made from aluminum-capped indium arsenide nanowires as a platform for engineered quantum nonlinearities. The researchers characterized how the Kerr (quartic) nonlinearity depends on magnetic flux and gate voltage, demonstrating that it can be tuned to zero using either control parameter. They also experimentally demonstrated three-wave mixing, confirming the presence of a cubic nonlinearity in this semiconductor-superconductor hybrid architecture. An effective theoretical model based on Andreev bound states qualitatively reproduced the observed behavior. The results establish these hybrid devices as a promising and tunable platform for parametric amplification, bosonic mode control, and engineered microwave mode interactions.

Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions

Haojie Dong, Minghui Song, Jingfang Sun et al.. Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions. iFungi. 2026

This review examines candidemia, a major cause of hospital-acquired bloodstream infections characterized by high mortality and significant healthcare burden. The authors synthesize current knowledge spanning epidemiology, species distribution, and infection origins, alongside an evaluation of existing diagnostic methods and their limitations. The review critically assesses emerging diagnostic technologies alongside therapeutic strategies, including empiric and targeted antifungal regimens, management of mixed infections, and the growing problem of antifungal resistance, with particular attention to the potential of combination therapy. Advances in antifungal drug development are highlighted, including agents in clinical trials and those targeting novel fungal pathways. Looking forward, the review identifies artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostics, vaccine-based prophylaxis, and synergistic treatment approaches as promising directions for improving candidemia management.

Bedside identification of subphenotypes in acute respiratory failure (PHIND): a multicentre, observational cohort study

Kiran Reddy, Pratik Sinha, David Antcliffe et al.. Bedside identification of subphenotypes in acute respiratory failure (PHIND): a multicentre, observational cohort study. The Lancet Respiratory Medicine. 2026

This prospective multicentre observational study investigated whether inflammatory subphenotypes of acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS) could be rapidly identified at the bedside and whether these subphenotypes would show meaningfully different clinical outcomes. Researchers enrolled adults with ARDS or acute hypoxaemic respiratory failure across intensive care units in the UK and Ireland, measuring plasma interleukin-6, soluble TNF receptor-1, and bicarbonate using a near-patient immunoanalyser with approximately one-hour turnaround, then classified patients into hyperinflammatory or hypoinflammatory subphenotypes using a validated logistic regression model. The hyperinflammatory subphenotype was identified in roughly one in five patients and carried substantially higher sixty-day mortality compared to the hypoinflammatory group, with the association remaining significant after adjustment for covariates. The study demonstrates that real-time bedside subphenotyping of ARDS is feasible and recapitulates the distinct outcomes previously described only through retrospective analyses, including differences in sepsis prevalence and metabolic acidosis. The authors argue these findings support the pursuit of precision medicine in ARDS and highlight the need for prospective interventional trials stratified by inflammatory subphenotype.

Mechanism-informed diagnostic accuracy of blood biomarkers for sepsis-associated encephalopathy: a systematic review and Bayesian diagnostic network meta-analysis.

Zhang Q, Zhu R, Li Y et al.. Mechanism-informed diagnostic accuracy of blood biomarkers for sepsis-associated encephalopathy: a systematic review and Bayesian diagnostic network meta-analysis.. Frontiers in molecular neuroscience. 2026

This systematic review investigated the diagnostic accuracy of blood biomarkers for sepsis-associated encephalopathy, a serious form of acute brain dysfunction occurring in sepsis patients. The authors conducted a PRISMA-compliant systematic review and Bayesian diagnostic network meta-analysis, searching multiple international databases and synthesizing evidence from studies with extractable diagnostic accuracy data, while stratifying findings by reference standard type and biomarker sampling timing relative to an anchor event. Blood-brain barrier and glial markers such as GFAP and S100β showed more balanced diagnostic discrimination than classic neuronal injury markers, while upstream immune and alarmin candidates ranked highest in Bayesian comparative analysis, suggesting a biologically coherent immune-to-neuronal cascade underlying the condition. However, several top-ranked biomarkers were supported by only single studies, thresholds and sampling contexts varied considerably across studies, and few studies examined whether biomarkers provide diagnostic value beyond established clinical predictors such as illness severity scores. The authors conclude that findings are hypothesis-generating rather than practice-changing, and call for prospective head-to-head validation studies using standardized phenotyping and anchor-based sampling to determine whether mechanism-staged biomarker panels offer incremental diagnostic utility.

Plasma and urinary sTREM-1 as biomarkers of sepsis-associated acute kidney injury in critically Ill children.

Magdy SM, El-Gawaad TA, Hussein FA et al.. Plasma and urinary sTREM-1 as biomarkers of sepsis-associated acute kidney injury in critically Ill children.. European journal of pediatrics. 2026

This prospective observational cohort study investigated whether plasma and urinary levels of soluble triggering receptor expressed on myeloid cells-1 (sTREM-1) could serve as early diagnostic biomarkers for sepsis-associated acute kidney injury in critically ill children admitted to a pediatric intensive care unit. Researchers measured sTREM-1 levels at the time of sepsis diagnosis and again at the time of acute kidney injury diagnosis in those who developed it, using established pediatric scoring criteria to define both conditions. Both plasma and urinary sTREM-1 levels were significantly elevated in children who subsequently developed sepsis-associated acute kidney injury compared to those who did not, with plasma sTREM-1 demonstrating better diagnostic accuracy than urinary sTREM-1 based on receiver operating characteristic analysis. Notably, both biomarkers rose further following acute kidney injury diagnosis, and urinary sTREM-1 also showed prognostic value for mortality in affected children. The authors conclude that sTREM-1 measurement at sepsis diagnosis may facilitate earlier risk stratification for acute kidney injury, potentially preceding detection by conventional creatinine- and urine output-based criteria.

Diagnostic value of cystatin C in acute kidney injury among patients with sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Cheng L, Zhang B, Hu X et al.. Diagnostic value of cystatin C in acute kidney injury among patients with sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.. Frontiers in medicine. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated the diagnostic utility of cystatin C for the early detection of acute kidney injury in patients with sepsis. The researchers systematically searched electronic databases, assessed study quality using the QUADAS-2 tool, and performed a random-effects meta-analysis across seventeen eligible studies, constructing a summary receiver operating characteristic curve to quantify overall diagnostic performance. Cystatin C demonstrated good diagnostic accuracy for sepsis-associated acute kidney injury, with an area under the curve, sensitivity, and specificity all indicating strong performance. Subgroup analyses revealed that studies employing Sepsis 3.0 diagnostic criteria showed higher specificity and diagnostic odds ratios with less heterogeneity, and combining cystatin C with other biomarkers further enhanced diagnostic accuracy. The authors conclude that cystatin C is a promising early biomarker for this condition and supports its potential use in clinical practice, despite acknowledging residual heterogeneity among included studies.

Pancreatic Stone Protein as an Emerging Biomarker in Paediatric Nosocomial and Postoperative Sepsis: An Integrative Review of Diagnostic and Prognostic Performance.

Ramírez Quintana A, Gaman EL, Badea M et al.. Pancreatic Stone Protein as an Emerging Biomarker in Paediatric Nosocomial and Postoperative Sepsis: An Integrative Review of Diagnostic and Prognostic Performance.. International journal of molecular sciences. 2026

This narrative review examined the diagnostic and prognostic value of pancreatic stone protein (PSP) as a biomarker for detecting sepsis and nosocomial infections in paediatric patients, a context where distinguishing true infection from postoperative sterile inflammation remains clinically difficult. The authors conducted a structured literature search across two major databases, screening studies published over a recent multi-year period and ultimately synthesising findings from over fifty studies meeting their inclusion criteria. The review found that PSP levels rise substantially in children with sepsis compared to those with non-infectious systemic inflammation, and that elevated PSP is associated with bacteraemia, organ dysfunction, and mortality. In multiple paediatric cohorts, PSP demonstrated diagnostic accuracy comparable to or superior to conventional markers such as C-reactive protein and procalcitonin, with particularly strong sensitivity and specificity reported in neonatal and other high-risk populations. The authors conclude that PSP is a promising early biomarker for paediatric sepsis but note that inter-study variability necessitates larger, prospective multicentre trials and integration of PSP into multimarker diagnostic frameworks before routine clinical adoption.

Early diagnostic accuracy of immature platelet fraction for sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.

Wiranata S, Manuaba IBAP, Supadmanaba IGP et al.. Early diagnostic accuracy of immature platelet fraction for sepsis: a systematic review and meta-analysis.. Hematology, transfusion and cell therapy. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated the diagnostic accuracy of the immature platelet fraction—a marker of bone marrow thrombopoietic activity—for the early identification of sepsis. The authors searched two major databases and identified a small number of studies meeting their inclusion criteria, performing pooled analyses of sensitivity, specificity, and diagnostic odds ratio, along with a subgroup analysis focused on adult populations. The meta-analysis found that the immature platelet fraction demonstrated only moderate diagnostic accuracy, with pooled sensitivity and specificity both around seventy percent and moderate heterogeneity across the included studies. The authors conclude that while the immature platelet fraction may serve as a complementary biomarker for sepsis, the limited number of available studies and methodological variability require cautious interpretation of these findings. They call for further standardized prospective research to clarify its clinical utility.

Diagnostic performance of interleukin-27 and C-reactive protein in neonatal sepsis: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis.

Liu W, Zhang Y, Liu W et al.. Diagnostic performance of interleukin-27 and C-reactive protein in neonatal sepsis: an updated systematic review and meta-analysis.. Frontiers in pediatrics. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated and compared the diagnostic accuracy of interleukin-27 (IL-27) and C-reactive protein (CRP) as biomarkers for neonatal sepsis, given that prior individual studies had produced inconsistent findings. The researchers searched major medical databases for controlled studies published over nearly three decades, following PRISMA guidelines, and pooled diagnostic performance data using standard meta-analytic tools including sensitivity, specificity, and summary receiver operating characteristic curves. The analysis, based on a set of controlled studies encompassing several hundred neonates, found that IL-27 achieved higher pooled sensitivity and specificity than CRP, with a notably greater area under the curve. These results indicate that IL-27 outperforms CRP in accurately identifying neonatal sepsis. The authors conclude that IL-27 may serve as a more reliable clinical biomarker for the early and accurate diagnosis of neonatal sepsis.

Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026

Hallie C. Prescott, Massimo Antonelli, Waleed Alhazzani et al.. Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026. Critical Care Medicine. 2026

This paper presents the 2026 Surviving Sepsis Campaign international clinical practice guidelines for the management of sepsis and septic shock in adults. The guideline development committee, representing broad geographic and professional diversity including representation from low- and middle-income countries, systematically reviewed evidence across six clinical domains encompassing screening and early management, infection control, hemodynamics, respiratory support, adjunctive therapies, and goals of care. Evidence synthesis followed the GRADE methodology, with recommendations classified as strong or conditional based on the balance of desirable and undesirable effects, certainty of evidence, patient values, resource considerations, and equity. The guidelines incorporate principles of antimicrobial stewardship alongside recommendations for early identification and treatment, and extend their scope to include prehospital and immediate post-hospital settings. The resulting recommendations are intended to provide actionable, evidence-based guidance to clinicians and health systems to improve outcomes for the tens of millions of patients affected by sepsis annually.

A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression

Kasper Favere, Matthias Bosman, Peter Delputte et al.. A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression. PubMed. 2026

This systematic review examined how different forms of exercise influence the expression of Toll-like receptors, a family of immune cell receptors central to inflammatory signalling that has been implicated in the link between physical inactivity, chronic low-grade inflammation, and non-communicable diseases. The authors conducted a comprehensive search of multiple databases, ultimately including studies that investigated exercise as an isolated intervention in humans, categorising findings by exercise modality and duration into acute and chronic resistance, aerobic, and combined exercise types. Acute resistance exercise generally appeared to upregulate TLR expression, whereas acute aerobic exercise tended toward neutral or downregulatory effects, particularly in healthy individuals. Chronic resistance and combined training programmes predominantly resulted in unchanged or decreased TLR levels, while chronic aerobic training produced mixed results with most measurements showing no significant change in expression. The authors conclude that exercise does interact with TLR signalling in a manner dependent on exercise characteristics, but highlight that substantial heterogeneity in study designs limits firm conclusions and that further research is needed to clarify the underlying mechanisms and pathophysiological implications.

Explainable AI in healthcare: a systematic review of XAI use cases in imaging, diagnostics, and rehabilitation

Apoorva Aravindkumar, Marimuthu Ramadoss, Saqhibuddeen Ahmed Fakhruddin Ahmed et al.. Explainable AI in healthcare: a systematic review of XAI use cases in imaging, diagnostics, and rehabilitation. Frontiers in Artificial Intelligence. 2026

This systematic review examined how explainable artificial intelligence methods are being applied across healthcare domains, specifically medical imaging, diagnostics, and rehabilitation. The authors searched major academic databases following established reporting guidelines, screening over a thousand records and ultimately synthesizing findings from several dozen empirical studies that employed XAI techniques alongside machine learning models. The review found a clear alignment between XAI methods and their application contexts, with gradient-based heatmap approaches such as Grad-CAM predominating in imaging tasks where spatial localization matters, while feature-attribution methods such as SHAP and LIME were favored for diagnostic and rehabilitation applications requiring case-level or global explanations. Many studies combined two or more XAI methods to cross-validate interpretations, reflecting a growing recognition that no single explainer is sufficient. The authors conclude that while current practice shows encouraging consistency in matching methods to modalities, broader adoption of standardized reporting, rigorous faithfulness assessments, and external validation will be necessary before these approaches can support trustworthy clinical deployment.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a lattice field generation algorithm that inverts a renormalization-group blocking transformation to efficiently produce large-volume field configurations. The blocking transformation is optimized using a "perfect blocking" condition so that the coarser lattice follows a simple effective action, and it is decomposed into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation. A conditional normalizing flow reconstructs the short-distance degrees of freedom lost during decimation, while a brief rethermalization corrects residual mismatches between the generated and target distributions. By applying this procedure recursively, the method enables a cascade upscaling strategy in which a small initial ensemble seeds progressively larger lattices without retraining. Testing in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality confirms stable upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume, with rethermalization tests showing that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while relevant thermal-direction mismatches relax more slowly, consistent with renormalization-group expectations.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the question of how much of a quantum state's "magic" (nonstabilizerness) arises from correlations between parties rather than from local degrees of freedom, a quantity termed nonlocal magic. The authors solve the previously intractable optimization problem of minimizing over all local bases by proving that, for the stabilizer fidelity, the nonlocal magic of any pure multiqubit state equals the distance of its entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. They further establish a surprising connection to entanglement embezzlement, showing that a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. Crucially, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum distributes weight across factor-of-two windows of rank, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states—despite sharing identical logarithmic entanglement scaling—carry unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The authors argue that nonlocal magic is an operationally meaningful measure of quantum correlations that is accessible via tensor-network methods and entanglement spectroscopy experiments.

QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions

Maria Vlachou, Anna Murphy Høgenhaug, Mohammad N. S. Jahromi et al.. QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the challenge of automatically identifying credibility-related factors in asylum application appeal decisions, which are long and legally complex documents where determining whether credibility influenced an original rejection is crucial for processing appeals. The authors developed QUEST, a system that frames this challenge as an information retrieval task, combining synthetic query generation, topic extraction, and relevance assessment to locate credibility indicators within two datasets of Danish asylum appeal materials. Beyond standard retrieval evaluation metrics, they introduced a novel domain-specific evaluation approach that assesses system performance specifically with respect to credibility factors rather than general topical relevance. Their findings show that evaluating system performance through this credibility-based lens reveals greater difficulty than standard metrics suggest, highlighting the inherent complexity of automatically extracting legally meaningful credibility assessments from asylum appeal documents.

Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach

Adil Alshammari, Hayretdin Bahsi. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of verifying accountability for cross-organization agent workflows when live systems are unavailable, untrusted, or controlled by a single party. The authors propose a preserved evidence-bundle model in which each workflow event carries policy-required evidence covering sender authentication, log commitments, witness-backed checkpoints, append-only continuity, delegation authorization, and optional receiver-signed receipts. A policy-controlled offline verifier then assesses whether a given bundle satisfies the selected evidence requirements without inferring delivery or receipt from transport behavior alone. Evaluation over a substantial set of complete workflows and preserved bundles showed that the verifier correctly rejected all corrupted or policy-insufficient bundles with no false acceptances, while checkpoint-context anchoring imposed the highest latency and delegation evidence required additional verification steps. The work advances evidence-based audit and dispute resolution by giving reviewers an offline, verifier-centered basis for assessing evidence sufficiency independent of any live platform.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates the computational problem of distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph, using polynomial threshold functions as the analytical tool. The authors develop the first rigorous analysis of low-degree polynomial threshold functions in this hypothesis testing setting. As part of this work, they derive new results characterizing the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic cycle lengths, which extends and generalizes several previously known results for random regular graphs and random lifts. The findings contribute to understanding the computational complexity of this detection problem by establishing what low-degree methods can and cannot achieve in this context.

Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective

Liheng Tan, Zhengkai Tu, Prasanna Karhade. Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examines how venture capital syndication networks form and evolve within United States information technology sectors, focusing on the mechanisms that drive co-investment relationships among investors. Using a comprehensive dyadic panel dataset spanning several decades across hardware, software, and hybrid subsectors, the researchers tested seven theorized mechanisms—including prior collaboration, shared partners, geographic proximity, and organizational similarity—using dyadic logit models and exponential random graph modeling. Prior collaboration emerged as the most consistent predictor of co-investment link formation across all subsectors, while shared partners, geographic proximity, and organizational-type similarity were also positively associated with new co-investment ties. Network closure and geographic homophily were further confirmed through whole-network modeling of a large software investor network. Together, the findings demonstrate that relational persistence, triadic closure, and homophily jointly shape the structural patterns of venture syndication networks in information technology finance.

Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.

Hammad M, Arif R, Fardoos S et al.. Integrated evaluation of rapid diagnostic testing, genotypic-phenotypic resistance profiling, and AI-Assisted prediction models for antimicrobial stewardship and clinical outcomes in a resource-limited setting.. PloS one. 2026

This prospective observational study investigated whether combining rapid diagnostic testing, molecular resistance gene detection, and an artificial intelligence prediction model could improve antibiotic prescribing and patient outcomes compared to conventional susceptibility testing alone. The study enrolled adult patients with suspected bacterial infections at a hospital in Pakistan, measuring outcomes including time to effective therapy, antibiotic appropriateness, hospital length of stay, and mortality. Rapid diagnostics dramatically shortened the time to pathogen identification and to initiation of effective therapy compared to conventional methods, while appropriate antibiotic use increased substantially and broad-spectrum antibiotic use declined. The integrated approach was associated with meaningfully shorter hospital stays and lower mortality, and the AI model showed strong discriminative ability for predicting antimicrobial resistance. The authors conclude that this combined diagnostic strategy holds promise as a scalable and cost-effective tool for combating antimicrobial resistance, particularly in resource-limited healthcare settings.

Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.

Goyal S, Sharma P, Aggarwal D et al.. Rapid diagnostics innovations for urinary tract infections using molecular biology, artificial intelligence and antimicrobial resistance surveillance: a comprehensive review.. Molecular biology reports. 2026

This review examines the current landscape and emerging innovations in diagnostic approaches for urinary tract infections, which present substantial clinical challenges due to high recurrence rates, microbial diversity, biofilm formation, and rapidly spreading antimicrobial resistance. The authors identify critical limitations of traditional culture-based diagnostics, particularly their slow turnaround times and inability to adequately resolve virulence and resistance factors, which drive inappropriate empirical antibiotic prescribing. The review surveys a range of advanced molecular diagnostic technologies, including nucleic acid amplification methods, biosensor-based platforms, microfluidic lab-on-chip systems, and omics-based approaches, with particular attention to their integration with artificial intelligence and machine learning for rapid, pathogen-specific detection and antibiotic susceptibility testing. The authors highlight how these combined technologies can improve diagnostic precision, guide targeted antimicrobial therapy, and support broader antimicrobial resistance surveillance and stewardship efforts. Challenges surrounding medical ethics, regulatory frameworks, and practical implementation are also addressed, with the authors arguing that next-generation AI-assisted diagnostics represent a transformative approach to managing antibiotic resistance in urinary tract infection care.

Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.

Liu Y, Xie N, Hu W et al.. Evaluation of an adapted relative growth method by MALDI-TOF MS for rapid determination of the susceptibility of &lt;i&gt;Escherichia coli&lt;/i&gt; to levofloxacin.. Microbiology spectrum. 2026

This study addressed the critical problem of slow antimicrobial susceptibility testing, which delays targeted treatment of bacterial infections, by developing a rapid diagnostic platform for determining *Escherichia coli* susceptibility to levofloxacin. The researchers created a framework called AI MedLab MS that combines dynamic MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry spectral analysis with a robust dynamic relative growth algorithm and machine learning to track how bacterial mass spectral profiles evolve over time during antibiotic exposure, rather than relying on single static measurements. A key innovation was analyzing the overall bacterial population rather than individual colonies, enabling detection of resistant subpopulations that could otherwise be missed. The platform was developed using clinical *E. coli* isolates and validated prospectively on an independent cohort, delivering susceptibility results within two hours. Validation demonstrated strong overall classification accuracy and particularly high precision for identifying resistant isolates, supporting the platform's potential clinical utility for rapid antimicrobial stewardship decision-making.

ERS/ESCMID state-of-the-science statement on the role of rapid diagnostic tests for the diagnosis and management of community-acquired lower respiratory tract infections.

This paper presents a state-of-the-science statement from two major European respiratory and infectious disease societies examining the role of rapid diagnostic tests in managing community-acquired lower respiratory tract infections. Developed within the framework of a European Union–funded initiative aimed at combatting antimicrobial resistance, the work systematically reviews existing literature and current clinical practice on how these tests can support antibiotic stewardship.
