It also addresses the downstream impact of incorporating rapid diagnostic tests into stewardship protocols on clinical outcomes. The overarching aim is to demonstrate how optimising antibiotic use through better diagnostics can reduce excess morbidity from respiratory infections and help curb the emergence of multidrug resistance.

Preliminary Single-Center Evaluation of the Alifax Molecular Mouse Sepsis Panel for Rapid Detection of Bloodstream Pathogens.

Han JH, Kim JK, Kim JO et al.. Preliminary Single-Center Evaluation of the Alifax Molecular Mouse Sepsis Panel for Rapid Detection of Bloodstream Pathogens.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of the Alifax Molecular Mouse sepsis panel for rapidly detecting bloodstream pathogens and antimicrobial resistance genes directly from positive blood culture broths. In a prospective single-center study, positive blood culture specimens were analyzed using the panel's cartridges targeting Gram-positive and Gram-negative bacteria and major resistance genes, with results compared against both a conventional microbiological workflow (including MALDI-TOF identification and automated susceptibility testing) and the FilmArray BCID2 panel. The Molecular Mouse panel demonstrated moderate-to-good agreement with the routine protocol for both bacterial identification and resistance gene detection, and showed comparable agreement with the FilmArray BCID2. Notably, the panel generated clinically actionable results within approximately one hour, representing a substantial reduction in turnaround time compared to the conventional workflow, which requires one to two days. The authors conclude that the panel shows promise as a rapid adjunct to routine microbiological workflows for managing bloodstream infections.

Antibiotic resistance in &lt;i&gt;Pseudomonas aeruginosa&lt;/i&gt;: mechanisms, diagnostic challenges, and omics-based diagnostic solutions.

Rahman MS, Wakeman CA. Antibiotic resistance in &lt;i&gt;Pseudomonas aeruginosa&lt;/i&gt;: mechanisms, diagnostic challenges, and omics-based diagnostic solutions.. Frontiers in microbiology. 2026

This paper reviews the antibiotic resistance landscape of *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*, a multidrug-resistant opportunistic pathogen that poses severe threats to immunocompromised patients and contributes substantially to the global antimicrobial resistance burden. The authors examine the diverse intrinsic and adaptive mechanisms by which the organism evades antibiotics, including efflux pump overexpression, porin modification, enzymatic inactivation, target site alterations, and phenotypic adaptations such as biofilm and persister cell formation, as well as its capacity for horizontal gene transfer. The paper also critically assesses the limitations of conventional culture-based diagnostics, particularly their inability to rapidly detect heteroresistance and biofilm-associated tolerance. In response, the authors survey recent advances in omics-based approaches—spanning genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, metabolomics, and others—alongside microfluidic platforms and machine learning-driven artificial intelligence as more rapid and precise diagnostic alternatives. The authors conclude that integrating multiple omics technologies with advanced diagnostic platforms represents a promising strategy for comprehensive resistance profiling, though standardized protocols, clinical validation, and cost-effective implementation remain essential prerequisites for translating these approaches into clinical practice.

Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026

Hallie C. Prescott, Massimo Antonelli, Waleed Alhazzani et al.. Executive Summary: Surviving Sepsis Campaign: International Guidelines for Management of Sepsis and Septic Shock 2026. Critical Care Medicine. 2026

The 2026 Surviving Sepsis Campaign guidelines represent an updated, comprehensive international guidance document for the identification and management of sepsis and septic shock in adult patients, developed using GRADE methodology with evidence synthesized through mid-2025. The guidelines encompass a total of one hundred twenty-nine statements spanning screening, infection management, hemodynamic support, respiratory care, adjunctive therapies, and long-term outcomes, with the majority being conditional recommendations. Notable new guidance includes suggestions for prehospital sepsis screening and antibiotic administration in patients with anticipated prolonged transport times, age-stratified mean arterial pressure targets favoring permissive hypotension in older patients, selective digestive tract decontamination in low-resistance settings, and active fluid removal following the acute resuscitation phase. Key changes from the prior iteration include a shift away from routine empiric antifungal therapy, updated flexibility permitting either invasive or noninvasive blood pressure monitoring, and a preference for crystalloids alone over supplemental albumin for fluid resuscitation in most patients. Several recommendations saw upgrades in strength or certainty of evidence, notably prolonged beta-lactam infusion and antimicrobial de-escalation, while others such as corticosteroid use and norepinephrine as first-line vasopressor were downgraded in strength or certainty based on updated evidence synthesis.

Accelerating scientific discovery with Co-Scientist

Juraj Gottweis, Wei‐Hung Weng, Alexander Daryin et al.. Accelerating scientific discovery with Co-Scientist. Nature. 2026

Co-Scientist is a multi-agent AI system built on Gemini that is designed to augment scientific discovery by generating, critiquing, and refining novel research hypotheses conditioned on user-defined research objectives and existing scientific evidence. The system features an asynchronous task execution framework for flexible compute scaling and a tournament evolution process through which hypotheses improve iteratively, with automated evaluations confirming that hypothesis quality increases as test-time compute scales. Although the system is general-purpose, its validation focused on three biomedical domains: drug repurposing, novel target discovery, and elucidating mechanisms of antimicrobial resistance. Notably, Co-Scientist identified new drug-repurposing candidates and synergistic combination therapies for acute myeloid leukaemia that were subsequently confirmed through in vitro experiments. These results are presented as evidence that such AI systems can meaningfully accelerate the hypothesis-generation stage of the scientific process.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

Thirumalaisamy Suryaprabha, Chunghyeon Choi, Yanfang Wu et al.. Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration. npj Flexible Electronics. 2026

This review examines the current landscape of smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time health monitoring in personalized and preventive medicine. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that allow these devices to integrate seamlessly with the body over extended periods, alongside multimodal sensing approaches that simultaneously track multiple biophysical and biochemical signals. The review also addresses how artificial intelligence is being incorporated to improve signal processing and generate predictive health insights from the data collected. Despite these advances, the authors identify persistent challenges including motion artifacts, limited energy autonomy, data privacy concerns, and difficulties in clinical interpretability. The paper synthesizes findings across materials science, device architecture, and AI-assisted strategies to provide a comprehensive overview of the field's progress and remaining obstacles.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state and future directions of biosensing technologies designed to detect priority foodborne pathogens, including *Escherichia coli*, *Salmonella*, and *Listeria monocytogenes*, which remain persistent threats to global public health. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, exploring how integrating nanoparticles with light-activated treatment modalities enhances therapeutic precision across a broad range of diseases. The authors survey the core components, classification schemes, and delivery platforms—including gold nanoparticles, liposomes, and dendrimers—that underpin photodynamic and photothermal therapies, with particular attention to how these constructs improve light absorption, targeting, and reactive oxygen species generation or localized heating. At the molecular level, the review dissects how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic cascades, and modulate immune responses, with relevance not only to cancer but also to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors also critically assess regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, identifying key barriers to clinical translation such as nanoparticle optimization, effective light delivery, and regulatory challenges. Overall, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative, noninvasive strategy with broad therapeutic potential, provided that molecular insights and clinical advancements can be successfully integrated.

A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion

Marcus DuPont. A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the mathematical structure underlying gas accretion onto a point mass embedded in atomic gas, demonstrating the existence of an exact similarity symmetry that holds even under time dependence, two-body atomic microphysics, and a broad class of radiation and feedback prescriptions. The symmetry is a joint rescaling of the black hole mass and ambient density that enlarges all spatial and temporal scales while leaving dimensionless flow profiles, optical depths, Eddington ratios, and variability patterns unchanged, and the authors prove this rescaling is unique within its class. A key dimensionless parameter is introduced, representing the fractional mass gained per Bondi time, which is shown to equal the expansion speed of the Bondi radius in units of the ambient sound speed, so that a value of unity corresponds to sonic dilation of the accretion radius. The paper demonstrates that any power-law retained accretion rate drives the system to this sonic threshold after a finite mass increase, after which the similarity-based description breaks down, and canonical hyper-Eddington accretion scenarios are found to have already crossed this boundary. The theorem unifies radiating Bondi and feedback-regulated accretion scalings into a common framework while precisely identifying the regime where a quasi-static, fixed-mass continuation of the solution loses validity.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the question of how much "magic" (nonstabilizerness) — the resource enabling universal quantum computation beyond Clifford circuits — resides specifically in the correlations between two parties sharing a quantum state, rather than in their local degrees of freedom. The authors solve the longstanding optimization problem of minimizing magic over all local bases by deriving an exact formula for the nonlocal magic of pure multiqubit states in terms of stabilizer fidelity: it equals the distance of the state's entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. They discover an unexpected connection to entanglement embezzlement, showing that a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. Strikingly, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum distributes weight across factor-of-two windows of rank, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states — both exhibiting identical logarithmic entanglement scaling — carry unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The authors argue that nonlocal magic is thus an operationally meaningful characteristic of quantum correlations, accessible via tensor-network simulations and entanglement spectroscopy experiments.

Automated Dislocation Detection in Electron Channelling Contrast Imaging: A Comparative Study of Rule-Based, Neural Network, and Deep Learning Approaches

A. Holmes, C. Trager-Cowan, J. Bruckbauer et al.. Automated Dislocation Detection in Electron Channelling Contrast Imaging: A Comparative Study of Rule-Based, Neural Network, and Deep Learning Approaches. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the automation of threading dislocation detection in semiconductor materials imaged via electron channelling contrast imaging (ECCI), a process that is traditionally slow due to reliance on manual analysis. The researchers benchmarked three automated detection pipelines — a classical rule-based computer vision approach, a convolutional neural network multi-stage classifier, and a single-stage YOLOv8 deep learning architecture — against a statistical ground truth derived from gallium nitride micrographs. The rule-based approach proved unreliable due to its need for extensive per-image tuning, and the CNN method achieved moderate accuracy but struggled with efficiency in high-density dislocation regions. YOLOv8 substantially outperformed the other methods, achieving near-complete counting accuracy, precision, and recall across thousands of dislocations with rapid inference times, after two domain-specific adaptations were implemented: an adaptive Gaussian tile-sizing algorithm to address scale sensitivity, and a substantially lowered confidence threshold that better reflected physical signal strength rather than classification ambiguity. The study demonstrates that a scale-adaptive, single-stage deep learning approach is practically viable for high-throughput quantitative defect characterisation in semiconductors.

Casimir-electrostatic pull-in in nanoelectromechanical actuators: Differentiable design sensitivities and the damping-dependent collapse boundary

N. S. Akintsov, A. P. Nevecheria, S. N. Andreev et al.. Casimir-electrostatic pull-in in nanoelectromechanical actuators: Differentiable design sensitivities and the damping-dependent collapse boundary. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the pull-in instability of nanoelectromechanical actuators operating at sub-100-nanometer gaps, where electrostatic and Casimir (quantum vacuum) forces compete to destabilize the device. The authors address a longstanding gap in understanding: while the quasi-static fold bounding safe operation has been known analytically for decades, the dynamical pull-in threshold reached from rest, its dependence on mechanical damping, and the design sensitivities of either boundary were not available in closed form. To overcome numerical difficulties near the collapse singularity, the authors trained a physics-informed neural network using a coordinate transformation that maps the pull-in pole to infinity, enabling accurate differentiation of the surrogate model to extract voltage sensitivities and solve inverse design problems. They analytically prove that the dynamic pull-in boundary is bracketed by two closed-form curves, is nondecreasing with the damping ratio, and merges with the quasi-static fold once the damping ratio exceeds a specific threshold, with the gap closure following a precise power-law scaling. They also find that thermal corrections to the Casimir force at these length scales are negligible, falling far below even the classical-limit bound.

COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing

Raghul Sugumar, Amrit Gopinath. COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a fundamental identification problem in evaluating multi-agent systems: when a team composition changes, it is impossible to attribute performance differences to routing decisions alone using end-to-end accuracy gaps. The authors introduce an evaluation framework called COVER that fixes a public information boundary, a downstream processing stack, and a finite legal team family before outcomes are generated, enabling identifiable measurement of routing effects separate from other system components. The framework distinguishes between pre-specified prospective controls and retrospective comparators, and defines oracle regret as the benchmark for assessing routing quality within a constrained setup. Empirical validation across multiple benchmarks and a tool-use environment demonstrates that the framework can reveal genuine selection headroom—cases where better routing would improve outcomes—while also exposing when apparent gains are artifacts of retrospective design choices rather than true routing improvements. The authors present COVER as an auditable measurement methodology that can expose routing shortcomings without falsely manufacturing routing wins.

Acquire, Repair, Preserve: A Diagnosis-Guided Post-Training Recipe for Small-Model Dialogue Game Agents

Nan Li. Acquire, Repair, Preserve: A Diagnosis-Guided Post-Training Recipe for Small-Model Dialogue Game Agents. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how small language models can be improved as agents in interactive dialogue games, where a model must maintain state across conversational turns, interpret feedback, and select valid actions under evolving constraints. The authors diagnosed failures of a small open-weight model in the LM Playschool Challenge, identifying not just broad knowledge gaps but also local decision errors such as repeated guesses, malformed actions, and violations of recently observed feedback. Based on these diagnostics, they developed a three-stage training recipe: supervised fine-tuning to acquire broad game participation, targeted preference learning using turn-local pairs to repair mechanically detectable failures within a specific dialogue-game family, and deliberate preservation of general capabilities outside the target domain. Their approach produced substantial improvements in both in-domain and public evaluation scores while approximately maintaining baseline performance on static benchmarks, though out-of-domain generalization remained limited. The results indicate that broad supervised fine-tuning contributes most of the capability gains, while targeted turn-level supervision offers additional benefit primarily when failure modes can be precisely identified and tend to transfer within rather than across game families.

Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology

Nam-Seog Kim, Jungkyu Kim. Advancing non-faradaic impedance biosensors: sensitivity enhancement strategies using microfluidics, multiscale labeling, and CMOS technology. Microsystems & Nanoengineering. 2026

This review examines non-Faradaic impedance biosensors, which detect biological targets by measuring changes in electrical properties without requiring electrochemical reactions, making them simpler to operate than Faradaic counterparts but historically limited in sensitivity. The authors analyze three complementary strategies for overcoming this sensitivity bottleneck: enhancement using micro- and nanoparticle labeling, microfluidic integration to improve mass transport of analytes to sensor surfaces, and on-chip integration using complementary metal-oxide-semiconductor technology for high spatiotemporal resolution. The review also evaluates the strengths and limitations of each strategy and discusses how their synergistic combination could be further developed. Fabrication of photoresist SU-8 pyrolyzed carbon electrodes and their integration in a microfluidic hematocrit detection system.

Lee HY, Rogers JA, Kendrick C et al.. Fabrication of photoresist SU-8 pyrolyzed carbon electrodes and their integration in a microfluidic hematocrit detection system.. Biomedical microdevices. 2026

This paper addressed limitations in fabricating pyrolyzed carbon microelectrodes from the photoresist SU-8 for use in microfluidic biosensing applications, particularly for hematocrit determination. The researchers developed a three-step pyrolysis protocol to overcome adhesion problems, geometric distortion, and limited surface area that plague conventional two-step approaches, and integrated the resulting low-aspect-ratio planar electrodes into a PDMS microfluidic device that measured current responses of red blood cells in saline under an applied voltage. Surface characterization revealed that the carbon electrodes exhibited substantially greater roughness and thickness compared to platinum electrodes, yielding a notable increase in electroactive surface area that reduced charge-transfer and concentration overpotentials. The device demonstrated precision and accuracy for hematocrit detection comparable to previously reported platinum-based systems, confirming functional equivalence without requiring noble metals or complex surface coatings. The work establishes a reproducible, scalable fabrication route for high-performance carbon microelectrodes suited to low-cost integrated diagnostic devices.

Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.

Bhuiyan SAM, Shumway R, Trinkle C et al.. Zinc finger protein arrays in an epoxy-functionalized, pump-free microfluidic device for amplification-free, colorimetric detection of double-stranded DNA.. The Analyst. 2026

This study developed a microfluidic biosensor platform for rapid, direct detection of pathogenic bacterial DNA without the need for amplification or DNA denaturation. The approach combines engineered zinc finger proteins, which bind specific double-stranded DNA sequences, with a sequence-enabled β-lactamase reassembly system that produces a colorimetric readout upon target recognition. The zinc finger proteins were covalently attached to epoxy-functionalized surfaces within a pump-free, capillary-driven microfluidic device, and were designed to target the *stx2* gene of *E. coli* O157. The device generated a visible color change from yellow to red upon target binding, achieved picomolar sensitivity, and demonstrated high specificity against non-cognate DNA sequences, with the entire assay completing in approximately ten minutes at ambient temperature. The authors conclude that this platform offers a practical, low-cost, instrument-free framework for translating programmable protein-DNA recognition into point-of-care pathogen detection.

Artificial Intelligence-Empowered Single-Cell Phenotyping for Rapid, Automated Pathogen Diagnostics.

Khadka S, Raahat B, Tragoudas S et al.. Artificial Intelligence-Empowered Single-Cell Phenotyping for Rapid, Automated Pathogen Diagnostics.. Advanced science (Weinheim, Baden-Wurttemberg, Germany). 2026

This paper developed an integrated diagnostic platform combining microfluidic technology with artificial intelligence to perform rapid bacterial identification and antimicrobial susceptibility testing at the single-cell level. The system uses a microfluidic device to expose individual bacterial cells to controlled antibiotic concentrations while capturing time-lapse phase-contrast microscopy images, which are then analyzed by a U-Net-based deep learning model that automatically segments and quantifies bacterial drug responses. The AI model demonstrated high accuracy in detecting individual Escherichia coli cells with negligible false-positive predictions, and the full platform determined dose-dependent growth inhibition profiles and minimum inhibitory concentrations for two antibiotics within two hours, compared to the one to two days required by conventional methods. Results were consistent with standard broth microdilution testing, validating the platform's accuracy. The system was also evaluated for automated species identification of both gram-negative and gram-positive bacteria in blood matrices, suggesting broader applicability for rapid clinical pathogen diagnostics.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how much of a quantum state's "magic" (nonstabilizerness, the resource enabling universal quantum computation beyond Clifford circuits) arises from correlations between parties rather than from local properties alone. The authors solve a longstanding open optimization problem by providing an exact characterization: for pure multiqubit states, the nonlocal magic quantified via stabilizer fidelity equals the distance of the state's entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. This result also reveals an unexpected connection to entanglement embezzlement, showing that a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if their nonlocal magic diverges. Strikingly, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum distributes weight across factor-of-two rank windows, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states can share identical logarithmic entanglement scaling yet possess unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The framework is directly applicable to tensor-network simulations and experimental entanglement spectroscopy.

Hardware-Efficient Error Mitigation and Shot-Efficient Sampling on IBM Quantum Hardware

Sumit Chongder. Hardware-Efficient Error Mitigation and Shot-Efficient Sampling on IBM Quantum Hardware. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper experimentally investigates error mitigation techniques and finite-shot sampling strategies on superconducting quantum hardware under realistic execution constraints. The study combines multiple methods — including calibration-aware qubit selection, zero-noise extrapolation, dynamical decoupling, readout-error mitigation, and optimized shot allocation — running experiments across ideal simulation, noise-model simulation, and physical device execution to isolate different sources of error. The researchers examined how mitigation performance varies with circuit depth, noise scale, qubit connectivity, and measurement budget, assessing accuracy through metrics such as expectation-value error, mean-squared error, and mitigation gain. A key finding is that the benefit of error mitigation is not universal: there are regimes where finite-shot fluctuations offset the advantages gained from additional mitigation overhead. The work provides a hardware-aware characterization of when error mitigation genuinely improves expectation-value estimation on current quantum processors, implemented using contemporary Qiskit and IBM Quantum Runtime workflows for reproducibility.

Targeted Power System Frequency Attack via the Selection of Maliciously Controlled Inverters

Xiangyu Zou, Betelihem Ashebo, Daniel K. Molzahn. Targeted Power System Frequency Attack via the Selection of Maliciously Controlled Inverters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how an adversary could strategically compromise a subset of inverter-based resources to destabilize targeted generators in a power system through a frequency attack. The attack is designed by engineering an unstable oscillatory mode whose eigenvector concentrates energy at the target generators while minimizing signatures at the compromised devices, making the attack both effective and harder to detect. The authors formalize this adversarial selection problem as a mixed-integer quadratically constrained program and develop two heuristic algorithms based on a ranking metric to make the otherwise combinatorially intractable problem computationally feasible. Validation on modified large-scale test systems through nonlinear dynamic simulations shows that the proposed heuristics recover optimal attacking device sets in most evaluated scenarios while substantially reducing computation time. The study also examines how the choice of eigenvalue targets, generator targets, and the number of compromised devices collectively influence the severity of the attack.

Casimir-electrostatic pull-in in nanoelectromechanical actuators: Differentiable design sensitivities and the damping-dependent collapse boundary

N. S. Akintsov, A. P. Nevecheria, S. N. Andreev et al.. Casimir-electrostatic pull-in in nanoelectromechanical actuators: Differentiable design sensitivities and the damping-dependent collapse boundary. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the pull-in instability of nanoelectromechanical actuators operating at sub-100-nanometer gaps, where competing electrostatic and Casimir (quantum vacuum) forces determine when the device collapses. The authors developed a physics-informed neural network trained using a rapidity coordinate transformation that maps the pull-in singularity to infinity, avoiding the numerical failures that occur when conventional integration methods cross the collapse threshold. By differentiating the trained surrogate, they obtained design sensitivities of the pull-in voltage that match known closed-form results with very high accuracy, and demonstrated the method's utility for inverting device specifications to target operating parameters. For the dynamically initiated pull-in boundary starting from rest—which has no closed-form solution when damping is finite—the authors proved analytical bounds on this boundary, established that it is nondecreasing in the damping ratio, and showed that it merges with the quasi-static fold above a critical damping ratio, with the gap closing according to a specific power-law scaling. They also characterize how the collapse timescale transitions from logarithmic to inverse square-root growth at the merger point, and find that thermal corrections to the Casimir force at these length scales are negligible relative to even the classical-limit bound.

COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing

Raghul Sugumar, Amrit Gopinath. COVER: Identifiable Evaluation of Coalition Routing. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a methodological problem in evaluating multi-agent systems: when a system changes its team composition, it also changes its outputs, making it impossible to attribute performance differences to routing alone using end-to-end accuracy gaps. The authors introduce an evaluation framework called COVER that fixes a public information boundary, a downstream processing stack, and a finite set of legal team configurations before outcomes are generated, enabling identifiable measurement of routing effects. The framework distinguishes between pre-specified prospective controls and retrospective comparators, and requires that the union of distinct teams selected by competing policies be executed to support fair pairwise policy contrasts. Controlled experiments on multi-hop question answering benchmarks and a tool-use sandbox demonstrate that the framework can expose genuine selection headroom—cases where better routing improves performance—while also revealing when apparent routing wins are artifacts of retrospective analysis or confounded stacks. The authors emphasize that COVER is an auditable measurement methodology rather than a claim of universal routing superiority, and that raw answer accuracy can be uninformative about routing effects when stack interactions are present.

Multirate State Space Models for End-to-End Processing of Pulse Density Modulated Speech Signals

Ludovic Boulanger, Sean U. N. Wood. Multirate State Space Models for End-to-End Processing of Pulse Density Modulated Speech Signals. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates processing pulse-density-modulated audio signals directly with deep neural networks based on state-space models, bypassing the conventional conversion to pulse-code-modulated audio that imposes computational overhead on resource-constrained edge devices. The authors identified two key properties of state-space models — their continuous-time parametrization and long-term memory — that make them well-suited for handling PDM signals across variable sampling rates without anti-aliasing. Building on these properties, they proposed a novel end-to-end architecture that encodes raw PDM input into a latent representation that is invariant to both modulation strategy and sampling rate. The architecture achieves competitive speech classification and enhancement performance compared to state-of-the-art methods operating on conventional PCM data at standard PDM sampling rates, while also performing robustly at low-power sampling rates. A particularly notable finding is that the state-space model's output can be downsampled by an extremely large factor, dramatically reducing the computational burden on subsequent processing layers.

Development of a NiCoFe-LDH/Ketjenblack-enhanced electrochemical antibacterial peptide biosensor for Aeromonas hydrophila detection in seawater samples.

Kızılkurtlu AA, Arancı K, Gülyüz S et al.. Development of a NiCoFe-LDH/Ketjenblack-enhanced electrochemical antibacterial peptide biosensor for Aeromonas hydrophila detection in seawater samples.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This paper describes the development of an electrochemical biosensor for the rapid detection of Aeromonas hydrophila, a bacterial pathogen significant in aquaculture settings. The biosensor was constructed by modifying a screen-printed carbon electrode with a nanocomposite of ternary nickel-cobalt-iron layered double hydroxide combined with Ketjenblack carbon, synthesized via a one-pot precipitation method, and functionalized with an antibacterial peptide as the biorecognition element. The nanocomposite material demonstrated a high-activity nanosheet architecture facilitating fast electron transfer, which was confirmed through multiple physicochemical and electrochemical characterization techniques. Under optimized conditions, the sensor achieved detection across a broad bacterial concentration range within thirty minutes at room temperature, with a very low limit of detection, high reproducibility, minimal cross-reactivity with other bacterial species, and strong performance when tested in spiked seawater samples. The authors claim this represents the first electrochemical antibacterial peptide-based biosensor for A. hydrophila validated in seawater, highlighting its potential utility for on-site aquaculture monitoring.

Self-Cleaning Electrochemical Biosensor Based on MCOF@Ag for Simultaneous Pathogen Detection and Photocatalytic Inactivation.

Bai Y, Xiao L, Cai L et al.. Self-Cleaning Electrochemical Biosensor Based on MCOF@Ag for Simultaneous Pathogen Detection and Photocatalytic Inactivation.. Analytical chemistry. 2026

This paper developed a dual-function electrochemical biosensor capable of both detecting and eliminating bacterial pathogens using a single electrode material. The researchers synthesized silver nanoparticle-decorated metal-covalent organic framework composites with a nanoflower-like structure, then functionalized the resulting electrode with aptamers specific to Staphylococcus aureus to enable selective bacterial capture and electrochemical quantification. The biosensor achieved highly sensitive detection of the target bacterium across a broad concentration range with a very low detection limit. Beyond detection, the material's photocatalytic activity under visible light allowed it to inactivate nearly all captured bacteria within minutes through reactive oxygen species generation, after which the electrode could be regeneratively cleaned and reused. The study demonstrates that a single metal-covalent organic framework-based electrode can integrate ultrasensitive pathogen detection with photocatalytic inactivation and self-cleaning regeneration, offering a promising platform for infectious disease monitoring and control.

Paper-Based Biosensor Using Dual-Recognition Molecules for Detection of &lt;i&gt;Staphylococcus aureus&lt;/i&gt; in Milk.

Hou W, Li X, Li J et al.. Paper-Based Biosensor Using Dual-Recognition Molecules for Detection of &lt;i&gt;Staphylococcus aureus&lt;/i&gt; in Milk.. Biosensors. 2026

This paper developed a paper-based biosensor for the rapid detection of *Staphylococcus aureus* in milk, addressing the need for on-site food safety monitoring of a common dairy contaminant. The biosensor employed a dual-recognition strategy combining a milk matrix-adapted aptamer, selected through a SELEX process, with polyclonal antibodies that capture the pathogen via multiple epitopes on the paper substrate. Colloidal gold nanoparticles conjugated to the aptamer served as visual signal reporters, enabling instrument-free detection. The system achieved a low detection limit within a short timeframe, and the combination of aptamer and antibody recognition was shown to synergistically improve both sensitivity and specificity compared to single-recognition approaches. The authors propose this cost-effective platform as a practical solution for dairy safety monitoring in complex food matrices.

A modular APEX biosensor enables sensitive and visualized monitoring of viral protease activity.

Li B, Dong B, Chen Y et al.. A modular APEX biosensor enables sensitive and visualized monitoring of viral protease activity.. Virologica Sinica. 2026

The paper developed modular biosensors to detect viral protease activity within living cells, aiming to overcome limitations of conventional fluorescence and FRET-based screening methods that suffer from non-specific conformational interference and poor reflection of intracellular inhibitory effects. The researchers engineered two biosensors based on a split ascorbate peroxidase system, where protease-specific cleavage of a recognition sequence triggers reassembly of enzyme fragments, restores catalytic activity, and produces amplified fluorescent signals. Using the coronavirus main protease as a model, one sensor achieved an exceptionally high signal-to-noise ratio for enzyme activity detection, while the other accurately identified potent inhibitors and avoided false positives that plague traditional approaches. By simply swapping the protease recognition sequence, the platform was adapted to detect proteases from multiple viruses including SARS-CoV-2, MERS-CoV, enterovirus 71, Epstein-Barr virus, and hepatitis A virus. The authors conclude that this modular, intracellular screening platform provides a reliable and broadly applicable tool for evaluating antiviral drug candidates against diverse viral protease targets.

Recent Progress in Nanoparticle-Based Biosensors for Monitoring &lt;i&gt;Shigella&lt;/i&gt; spp. in Food Safety: A Critical Review.

Savas S, Gharibzahedi SMT. Recent Progress in Nanoparticle-Based Biosensors for Monitoring &lt;i&gt;Shigella&lt;/i&gt; spp. in Food Safety: A Critical Review.. Biosensors. 2026

This review examined recent advances in nanoparticle-based biosensors for detecting *Shigella* species in food safety contexts, motivated by the pathogen's low infectious dose and the limitations of conventional culture-based and molecular diagnostic methods. The authors critically assessed biosensor designs across multiple signal transduction strategies—including optical approaches such as fluorescence, SERS, and LSPR, as well as electrochemical and lateral flow formats—evaluating how nanomaterials contribute to signal amplification and biorecognition. Detection was categorized by biological target, distinguishing between gene-level strategies using virulence markers such as *ipaH* for stable genus-level identification and whole-cell recognition approaches that enable faster detection with less sample preparation. Validation in real food matrices demonstrated acceptable recovery, minimal cross-reactivity, and agreement with reference methods across the biosensor types reviewed. The paper concludes by offering a design-oriented framework that integrates nanomaterial function, biosensor architecture, and performance characteristics to guide biosensor selection for food safety applications.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review examines the dual role of reactive oxygen species in biological systems, where they act as essential signaling molecules at physiological concentrations but drive disease pathology when produced in excess. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the major types, cellular sources, and molecular mechanisms underlying both beneficial redox signaling and oxidative stress-mediated damage across a range of major disorders, including cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegeneration, and inflammatory conditions. The review also critically evaluates contemporary and emerging methods for detecting and quantifying reactive oxygen species in clinical and preclinical settings, encompassing biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging approaches. On the basis of this integrated analysis, the authors argue for advancing precision redox medicine through targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies informed by improved detection technologies and a deeper mechanistic understanding of redox-regulated processes.

The extracellular matrix in inflammation and cancer

Wanting Zhang, Yuhang Xiang, Chen Lu et al.. The extracellular matrix in inflammation and cancer. Molecular Biomedicine. 2026

This review examines the role of the extracellular matrix in regulating immune cell behavior within inflammatory and tumor microenvironments. The authors systematically surveyed key ECM constituents—including fibrous proteins, glycosaminoglycans, proteoglycans, and glycoproteins—along with the molecular mediators, such as matrix metalloproteinases and inflammatory cytokines like TNF, interleukin, and TGF-β, that drive ECM remodeling in cancer. The review describes how dysregulated ECM components interact with immune cell receptors to suppress specific immune subsets, promote T cell exhaustion and macrophage polarization, and activate intracellular signaling pathways that cancer cells exploit to support progression and metastasis. The authors also explore how cytokine-driven ECM remodeling shapes a stromal environment conducive to tumor survival. Finally, the review highlights therapeutic strategies aimed at targeting ECM-mediated immunosuppression in solid tumors as a means of enhancing cancer immunotherapy efficacy.

Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective

Akshay Pal, Andrew Lucas, Umang Mehta. Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how electron-electron interactions modify density three-point correlation functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids, which are known to encode topological information about the Fermi sea in the noninteracting limit. The authors employ the coadjoint-orbit effective field theory framework, which reformulates fermionic loop calculations as tree-level diagrams in a bosonized language and allows systematic inclusion of generalized Landau interaction parameters. They find that even at linear order in the Landau interaction, a contribution proportional to the band curvature at the Fermi momentum arises, which alters the nonanalytic structure of the free density three-point function. This new nonanalytic structure is distinct from both the noninteracting case and the special case of a Galilean-invariant interacting system, demonstrating that interactions can in principle modify the topology-detecting character of the density three-point correlator. The results imply that the topological information encoded in these correlations is not interaction-independent for systems with general isotropic dispersions.

How Long-Range Tails Reshape Non-Hermitian Spectra

Ding Gu, Zhanpeng Fu, Yu-Min Hu et al.. How Long-Range Tails Reshape Non-Hermitian Spectra. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how exponentially decaying long-range hoppings—commonly truncated in tight-binding models—affect non-Hermitian systems under open boundary conditions. The authors demonstrate that even an infinitesimally small long-range hopping can nonperturbatively reconstruct both the spectrum and eigenstates of a short-range non-Hermitian system, a dramatic failure of the truncation approximation that has no counterpart in Hermitian physics. The underlying mechanism arises from a competition between the decay length of the long-range hoppings and the localization length of non-Hermitian skin modes, producing a sharp transition as the decay rate is varied. To describe the reconstructed spectra analytically, the authors introduce a squeezed generalized Brillouin zone framework in one dimension and a squeezed amoeba formulation in two or higher dimensions. They further show that long-range hoppings qualitatively reshape the Green's function in ways that are experimentally detectable, highlighting the physical importance of these otherwise seemingly negligible tails.

Advancing Interaction-Sensitive Feature Selection: Novel Relief-Based Algorithms, Expanded Comparisons, and Recommendations for Biomedical Data Mining

Kia Kazemi-Nia, Harsh Bandhey, Philip J. Freda et al.. Advancing Interaction-Sensitive Feature Selection: Novel Relief-Based Algorithms, Expanded Comparisons, and Recommendations for Biomedical Data Mining. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper examined Relief-based algorithms (RBAs), a class of filter-based feature selection methods capable of detecting feature interactions, in the context of high-dimensional biomedical data analysis. The researchers refactored and optimized the scikit-rebate Python package, introducing several existing and newly proposed RBA variants implementing alternative strategies for neighbor selection and feature scoring, then benchmarked all algorithms across diverse simulated genomic datasets varying in sample size, feature count, heritability, and association type. The refactoring alone yielded dramatic reductions in algorithm runtimes, making the methods substantially more practical. Most RBAs effectively detected two-way interactions in noisy data, with algorithms employing "far" scoring excelling at interaction detection though at the cost of sensitivity to main effects, while several algorithms including the newly introduced MultiSWRFDB showed strong performance across both main effects and multi-way epistatic interactions. The study concludes that the newly introduced RBA variants robustly preserve both main effect and epistatic predictive signals, making them well-suited as preprocessing steps for downstream biomedical modeling.

ExoMOD II. A Statistical Model of Transit Timing Variations in Kepler Multi-Planet Systems

David Nesvorny, Daniel A. Yahalomi, David Kipping et al.. ExoMOD II. A Statistical Model of Transit Timing Variations in Kepler Multi-Planet Systems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper uses a forward-modeling population framework called ExoMOD, calibrated on Kepler data, to statistically predict Transit Timing Variations (TTVs) in close-in multi-planet systems and compare those predictions against observed TTV signals. The study finds that gravitational interactions between planets are insufficient to explain the observed short-period TTV signals, implying those signals must arise from other, unidentified sources. For long-period TTVs, which are more plausibly caused by planet-planet interactions, the analysis reveals that single transiting planets have TTV-inducing companions nearly as frequently as systems with two transiting planets, ruling out orbital architectures that favor a large population of intrinsically single planets. Systems with higher observed multiplicities show elevated rates of long-period TTVs and low orbital gap complexities, suggesting they preserved a dynamical memory of their formation. The work is presented as a step toward building a more rigorous connection between observational statistics and theories of planetary system formation.

ExoMOD I. A Forward Model for the Orbital Architecture of Kepler Multi-Planet Systems

David Nesvorny, Daniel A. Yahalomi, David Kipping et al.. ExoMOD I. A Forward Model for the Orbital Architecture of Kepler Multi-Planet Systems. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops a forward model to interpret transit observations from the Kepler telescope and characterize the true orbital architecture of close-in multi-planet systems, accounting for the geometric bias that only planets with near edge-on orbits are detectable. The authors parameterize planetary system properties including distributions of planet radii, masses, orbital periods, and orbital excitation via the Angular Momentum Deficit, then fit the model to Kepler's planet catalog using a Bayesian inference framework. Key findings include that single- and multi-planet systems show statistically distinct period distributions, and that high-multiplicity systems appear to retain memory of their formation through correlated period ratios. The study estimates that a typical FGK dwarf hosts roughly two or more planets within the studied radius and period ranges, and concludes that for any detected planetary system, there is approximately an even chance that at least one inner or intermediate-period planet was missed by Kepler. The gap complexity metric proves useful for constraining both the period ratio structure and the orbital excitation distribution of these systems.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the computational problem of distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph, a natural hypothesis testing task in the theory of random graphs. To analyze this problem, the authors develop the first treatment of low-degree polynomial threshold functions in this setting, a framework commonly used to establish computational hardness or limitations of efficient algorithms. Along the way, they derive new results on the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic lengths, which generalize previously known results for random regular graphs and random lifts separately into a unified framework. Their findings collectively characterize the power and limitations of low-degree polynomial methods for detecting structured randomness in this graph model.

Twenty-Two Months of Syndromic Multiplex-PCR Testing for Acute Infections in a Southern Italian Hospital: Pathogen Epidemiology, Diagnostic Appropriateness and the Cost of Negative Results.

Chirizzi D, Spedicato A, Bianco G et al.. Twenty-Two Months of Syndromic Multiplex-PCR Testing for Acute Infections in a Southern Italian Hospital: Pathogen Epidemiology, Diagnostic Appropriateness and the Cost of Negative Results.. Microorganisms. 2026

This study examined the real-world use and diagnostic yield of syndromic multiplex-PCR panels across four clinical syndromes — meningitis/encephalitis, upper and lower respiratory infections, and gastrointestinal infections — at a southern Italian hospital over nearly two years. Researchers retrospectively analysed thousands of panel determinations, removing duplicate tests from the same patient to ensure each represented a distinct diagnostic episode, and evaluated pathogen distribution, result positivity rates, and the financial implications of negative findings. A striking majority of all determinations returned entirely negative results, with the meningitis/encephalitis and gastrointestinal panels showing the highest negativity rates, indicating substantial over-utilisation in those clinical streams. Key microbiological findings included viral dominance in central nervous system infections, rhinovirus/enterovirus prevalence in respiratory specimens, clustering of antimicrobial resistance markers in Enterobacterales co-infections on the pneumonia panel, and a predominance of diarrhoeagenic Escherichia coli pathotypes in gastrointestinal samples. The authors conclude that panel use should be restricted through clinical gating criteria, tiered testing algorithms, and mandatory reflex sequencing of unsubtypeable influenza A specimens to close surveillance gaps for novel or zoonotic strains.

320 Diagnostic stewardship of a multiplex pneumonia PCR panel leads to cost savings and more appropriate patient care

Season-matched comparison of pediatric respiratory pathogen detection patterns before and after the lifting of COVID-19 restrictions in Guangzhou.

Hua L, Liang W, Li W et al.. Season-matched comparison of pediatric respiratory pathogen detection patterns before and after the lifting of COVID-19 restrictions in Guangzhou.. Frontiers in pediatrics. 2026

This study examined how the lifting of COVID-19 non-pharmaceutical interventions affected the detection patterns of pediatric respiratory pathogens in subtropical southern China. Researchers retrospectively analyzed nasopharyngeal swab specimens from children under sixteen presenting with fever or respiratory symptoms at a Guangzhou pediatric medical center, using a consistent twelve-pathogen multiplex PCR panel across both a pre-lifting and a post-lifting period, with a season-matched comparison focused on the same months across two consecutive years to minimize seasonal bias. Following the lifting of restrictions, detection rates of Influenza A, RSV, and human rhinovirus increased substantially compared to the matched prior-year period, while Influenza B and human metapneumovirus declined markedly. The post-lifting cohort also contained a higher proportion of infants and showed greater rates of fever, wheezing, and lower respiratory tract diagnoses, though the authors note these clinical differences may partly reflect changes in healthcare-seeking behavior and admission thresholds rather than true shifts in disease burden. The findings highlight the value of continued multiplex surveillance and age-targeted prevention efforts, while cautioning against direct causal attribution of pathogen shifts to restriction lifting alone given residual confounding by seasonality, SARS-CoV-2 circulation, and behavioral factors.

Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.

Chen J, Wang H, Li Y et al.. Scenario-Driven Rapid Testing for Top Pathogens in Pediatric Respiratory Infections: Clinical and Economic Value from Emergency Triage to Precision Anti-Infective Management in the PICU.. Pathogens (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This narrative review examined how rapid diagnostic testing for respiratory pathogens in children can be optimized to improve both clinical and economic outcomes across different care settings. The authors identified a core problem: although a wide range of rapid tests now exist—from antigen assays to metagenomic sequencing—evidence from pediatric emergency trials shows that broad pathogen detection alone does not reliably reduce antibiotic use or healthcare costs. In response, they developed a scenario-driven framework in which "top pathogens" are defined contextually by age group, clinical syndrome, season, and care setting based on criteria such as prevalence, treatment implications, transmissibility, and antimicrobial resistance relevance. The review applies this framework across distinct clinical scenarios, from emergency triage and inpatient ward care to severe pneumonia, intensive care, and outbreak management, and analyzes the economic mechanisms through which appropriately targeted testing can generate value, including reduced unnecessary antimicrobials, timely antiviral initiation, optimized isolation, and shorter hospital stays. The authors conclude that embedding rapid diagnostics within predefined decision pathways guided by diagnostic and antimicrobial stewardship principles, rather than applying broad syndromic panels indiscriminately, offers a practical path toward precision infectious disease management in children.

Distribution Patterns of Non-SARS-CoV-2 Respiratory Pathogens Among Patients With Influenza-Like Illness During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Cross-Sectional Study From Bangladesh.

Sultana S, Taher A, Haque N et al.. Distribution Patterns of Non-SARS-CoV-2 Respiratory Pathogens Among Patients With Influenza-Like Illness During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Cross-Sectional Study From Bangladesh.. Cureus. 2026

This cross-sectional study investigated the respiratory pathogens responsible for influenza-like illness in patients who tested negative for SARS-CoV-2 during the COVID-19 pandemic at a tertiary care hospital in Bangladesh. Adults presenting with influenza-like symptoms between July and December 2022 were enrolled and their nasopharyngeal swabs were analyzed using multiplex real-time PCR to detect a broad range of viral and bacterial pathogens. Over a third of participants tested positive for at least one non-SARS-CoV-2 pathogen, with human rhinovirus emerging as the most prevalent viral agent and *Streptococcus pneumoniae* as the dominant bacterial pathogen. Co-infections were present in a minority of patients, with rhinovirus–influenza B and *S. pneumoniae*–*Haemophilus influenzae* pairings showing the strongest, though statistically non-significant after correction, co-detection trends. The authors conclude that broad-spectrum multiplex PCR testing should be incorporated into the clinical evaluation of SARS-CoV-2-negative patients with persistent respiratory symptoms to capture the full etiological burden of respiratory illness.

Etiology of severe acute respiratory infections among adults in northern Thailand using multiplex PCR: A post-COVID-19 surveillance study (2023-2024).

Kham-Kjing N, Kawila R, Tariyo P et al.. Etiology of severe acute respiratory infections among adults in northern Thailand using multiplex PCR: A post-COVID-19 surveillance study (2023-2024).. PloS one. 2026

This study investigated the causes of severe acute respiratory infections among hospitalized adults in northern Thailand during the post-COVID-19 pandemic period. Researchers retrospectively analyzed respiratory samples from adult patients admitted to a regional hospital in Chiang Mai with SARI symptoms, testing specimens using multiplex real-time PCR capable of detecting a broad range of respiratory pathogens. Nearly half of patients tested positive for at least one respiratory pathogen, with adenovirus and rhinovirus emerging as the most commonly identified viruses, and co-infections present in a notable proportion of cases. Seasonal patterns were observed, with influenza A and rhinovirus peaking in January while adenovirus and enterovirus circulated throughout the study period. The findings underscore the importance of continued pathogen-specific surveillance and broader rapid molecular diagnostics in the post-pandemic era, particularly given the high rate of empiric antibiotic use observed among patients whose infections may not have had a bacterial cause.

Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations

Ojonugwa Precious John, Kayode Olayinka Afolabi, Anayochukwu Chibuike Ngene et al.. Enterococcus Species: Multifaceted Probiotic Potential and Safety Considerations. Microorganisms. 2026

The paper examines the genus *Enterococcus*, commensal bacteria found in the gastrointestinal tracts of humans and animals as well as in food and environmental sources, focusing on their dual role as potentially beneficial microorganisms and sources of safety concern. The authors reviewed how select enterococcal strains are employed as probiotics, antibiotic alternatives, and protective cultures, where they help control pathogens, modulate the microbiome, support food fermentation, and produce bioactive compounds such as bacteriocins with pathogen-inhibitory activity. At the same time, the review highlights that certain species are associated with healthcare-acquired infections and the dissemination of antimicrobial resistance genes, complicating their application. The authors argue that rigorous safety evaluations are essential before enterococci can be broadly adopted in clinical or agrifood contexts. The paper is presented as a comprehensive reference intended to guide future research, regulatory frameworks, and practical applications in both healthcare and food systems.

Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America

Maria Ogrzewalska, Ralph Eric Thijl Vanstreels, Elisa Cavalcante Pereira et al.. Genomic analysis of high pathogenicity avian influenza viruses from Antarctica reveals multiple introductions from South America. Nature Communications. 2026

This study investigated the introduction and spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza virus H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b into Antarctica, where it poses a significant threat to polar wildlife. Researchers detected the virus in carcasses of eight species across the South Shetland Islands during two consecutive austral summers, then applied whole-genome sequencing, mutational profiling, and phylogenetic reconstruction to characterize the viral strains. The analyses revealed that the detected viruses descended from multiple distinct introduction events rather than a single source, with one group of strains clustering with H5N1 previously found in marine mammals in South America and another group clustering with strains from South Georgia and the Antarctic Peninsula. These findings demonstrate extensive epidemiological connectivity between South America and Antarctica, with South Georgia appearing to function as an intermediary stepping stone facilitating viral spread into the Antarctic region.

Serologic Evidence of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Infection in a Veterinary Professional Exposed to an Infected Domestic Cat — Los Angeles County, California, December 2024–January 2025

Aisling Vaughan, Allison Joyce, Elizabeth Traub et al.. Serologic Evidence of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza A(H5N1) Virus Infection in a Veterinary Professional Exposed to an Infected Domestic Cat — Los Angeles County, California, December 2024–January 2025. MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report. 2026

This report investigated potential human exposure to highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) virus following an outbreak among domestic cats in Los Angeles County linked to consumption of raw animal products. Health authorities monitored all persons exposed to the infected cats and later conducted a serological survey to detect infections that may have been missed by standard diagnostic testing, including among those without symptoms. Although none of the exposed individuals tested positive by PCR, antibody testing revealed serological evidence of H5N1 infection in one asymptomatic veterinary professional who had not used respiratory or eye protection during animal contact and had no other known risk factors. These findings represent evidence of possible cat-to-human transmission of H5N1 virus, a route of spillover that warrants heightened concern. The report emphasizes the importance of consistent use of appropriate personal protective equipment in veterinary settings where exposure to infected animals may occur.
