Metagenomic and Targeted Next-Generation Sequencing in Infectious Disease Diagnostics: Current Applications, Challenges, and Future Perspectives

Rong Rong, Yuni Long, Yujing Li et al.. Metagenomic and Targeted Next-Generation Sequencing in Infectious Disease Diagnostics: Current Applications, Challenges, and Future Perspectives. Diagnostics. 2026

This review examines how metagenomic and targeted next-generation sequencing technologies are reshaping the diagnosis and management of infectious diseases in clinical settings. The authors surveyed current applications of both approaches across clinical microbiology, focusing on pathogen detection, antimicrobial resistance profiling, virulence characterization, and outbreak investigation in conditions such as pneumonia, critical illness, and pediatric respiratory infections. The review evaluates the diagnostic performance, strengths, and limitations of these sequencing methods, including obstacles related to sensitivity, specificity, standardization, bioinformatic complexity, and cost. The authors also explore how integrating sequencing-based surveillance with public health tools such as wastewater epidemiology could enable population-level monitoring of healthcare-associated infections and antimicrobial resistance. Finally, the review outlines the steps required to move these genomic approaches from research environments into routine clinical and public health practice.

Expert Consensus on the Prevention of Secondary Mucormycosis Following Respiratory Viral Infections

Junyan Qu, Xinyao Liu, Min Quan et al.. Expert Consensus on the Prevention of Secondary Mucormycosis Following Respiratory Viral Infections. iFungi. 2026

This paper addresses the clinical challenge of secondary mucormycosis arising after respiratory viral infections, particularly those caused by SARS-CoV-2 and influenza viruses. A multidisciplinary group of experts was convened to develop a formal consensus document aimed at systematically guiding prevention efforts. The consensus identifies high-risk patient populations and puts forward preventive recommendations organized across four domains: education and training, personal protection, early diagnosis and treatment, and infection control. The document also acknowledges areas where evidence remains insufficient or contested, and it proposes directions for future research to strengthen the evidence base. Overall, the consensus seeks to optimize prevention strategies and raise awareness of mucormycosis as a serious complication following respiratory viral disease.

Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective

Liheng Tan, Zhengkai Tu, Prasanna Karhade. Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how venture capital co-investment networks form and evolve within U.S. information technology sectors by examining the relational mechanisms that drive syndication decisions. Using a comprehensive dyadic dataset spanning nearly six decades across hardware, software, and hybrid subsectors, the researchers tested seven theoretical mechanisms—including prior collaboration, shared partners, geographic proximity, and organizational similarity—through logistic regression models applied to all possible investor pairs. They also estimated an exponential random graph model of the contemporary software investment network among a large set of active investors. The findings consistently show that prior collaboration is the strongest predictor of new co-investment ties, while network closure, geographic homophily, and organizational-type similarity also positively structure link formation, with other factors such as domain overlap and prominence varying by subsector. Together, the results demonstrate that IT venture syndication networks are jointly shaped by relational persistence, social closure, and homophilous preferences rather than any single mechanism.

PanelShield: Verifiable Closed-Loop Safe Planning for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation

Guipeng Xin, Jiahe Xu, Chenhui Wan et al.. PanelShield: Verifiable Closed-Loop Safe Planning for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of safe, manual-guided robotic operation of industrial control panels, which requires not only recognizing controls and generating actions but also strictly adhering to constraints specified in operation manuals and safety regulations. The authors developed PanelShield, a closed-loop planning framework that generates parameterized action sequences grounded in task-relevant manual evidence and enforces safety through dual formal verification using Linear Temporal Logic and a Safety Finite State Machine, catching both cross-step temporal violations and local transition illegality. When a violation is detected, the system produces a structured counterexample identifying the earliest offending step and its cause, enabling targeted repair and re-verification rather than wholesale replanning. The framework was evaluated on a custom multi-level long-horizon benchmark spanning three representative industrial panel types, as well as in real-world robotic experiments, where it substantially outperformed foundation-model-only baselines on safety-constrained tasks while achieving a very low violation rate and modest end-to-end latency. The results demonstrate that formal verification can be practically integrated with foundation-model-based planners to achieve flexible yet auditable and safety-assured robotic manipulation in industrial settings.

MaCoPlanner: LLM-Assisted Manual-Compiled Task Planning with Proactive Safety Verification for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation

Guipeng Xin, Jiahe Xua, Mohammad Deghat et al.. MaCoPlanner: LLM-Assisted Manual-Compiled Task Planning with Proactive Safety Verification for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

MaCoPlanner is a task-planning framework designed to enable robots to operate industrial control panels by reasoning over heterogeneous equipment manuals containing operating procedures, safety rules, and device-state constraints. The system converts these manuals into a typed intermediate representation, retrieves relevant evidence for plan generation, and symbolically simulates candidate plans before execution to check for procedural and state-transition violations, with detected errors returned for targeted repair and unresolvable plans rejected outright. A separate execution interface then grounds verified symbolic actions to physical controls and tracks device state. Evaluation using an independent oracle showed a low final violation rate, with meaningful improvements in task success on both moderately and highly complex tasks compared to a baseline that uses raw manuals directly. Additional experiments on a controller-panel simulator confirmed execution feasibility under representative conditions, though the authors explicitly note the system is not claimed to be ready for industrial deployment.

Spectral Features Dominate BCG Respiratory-Event Detection: A Large-Scale Patient-Independent Comparison of Feature Groups in Sleep Apnea Patients

Israel Campero Jurado, Zoe Bousraou, Lara Benning et al.. Spectral Features Dominate BCG Respiratory-Event Detection: A Large-Scale Patient-Independent Comparison of Feature Groups in Sleep Apnea Patients. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This study investigated which signal features extracted from ballistocardiographic recordings are most useful for detecting respiratory events associated with sleep apnea, a question previously unresolved in the literature. Researchers recorded data from a large capacitive pressure mat simultaneously with standard respiratory polygraphy in a substantial cohort of patients undergoing in-hospital sleep apnea evaluation, extracting a broad set of features spanning statistical, time-domain, frequency-domain, wavelet, frame-energy, and nonlinear complexity descriptors across multiple spatial channels. Using strict leave-one-patient-out cross-validation to ensure patient-independent generalization, machine learning classifiers achieved very high discriminative performance for distinguishing respiratory-event windows from event-free windows. Feature importance analysis revealed that frequency-domain features, particularly breathing-band spectral power and spectral shape descriptors, dominated discrimination, while time-domain features such as curve-length and area-under-the-curve measures provided the main complementary contribution, and wavelet or nonlinear features played only secondary roles. The findings establish an empirical basis for prioritizing a compact, interpretable subset of features in future unobtrusive BCG-based sleep apnea monitoring systems.

CP-Aware OFDM-Based OOK Signaling

Badr Eddine Ouakouak, Salah Eddine Zegrar, Hüseyin Arslan. CP-Aware OFDM-Based OOK Signaling. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses a fundamental incompatibility between cyclic prefix structures and on-off keying signal generation in OFDM-based low-power IoT systems. The researchers proposed a CP-aware waveform design that extends OOK signal generation across the entire CP-OFDM symbol duration, which risks breaking subcarrier orthogonality when the cyclic prefix length is mismatched at the receiver. To manage this, the design requires independent OOK generation with modifications to the existing signal generation block, and introduces a block-wise time shift strategy to reduce CP mismatch effects. Analytical and simulation results indicate that interference imposed on legacy OFDM subcarriers remains controlled under this scheme, while OOK detection performance improves substantially, though practical deployment trade-offs must be weighed.

Under-Mattress Temporal Sensing for Next-Day Agitation Risk Scoring in Dementia Wards

Zhen Liu, Marta Bono, Robbe Decloedt et al.. Under-Mattress Temporal Sensing for Next-Day Agitation Risk Scoring in Dementia Wards. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated whether contactless under-mattress physiological signals recorded during the night could predict the risk of agitation in dementia patients on the following day, and whether retaining fine-grained temporal structure improves prediction compared to simple nightly summaries. Using data from a specialized hospital dementia unit, the researchers benchmarked four analytical approaches ranging from conventional handcrafted nightly summary features to full-night sequence modeling and sliding-window multiple-instance learning, evaluated with patient-grouped cross-validation. Full-night sequence modeling achieved the best discrimination performance, and both minute-level temporal pipelines outperformed nightly summaries, though differences relative to three-period handcrafted features were uncertain. Attribution analyses across temporal models consistently highlighted activity, heart rate, and respiratory rate during the core overnight period as the most informative signals. The authors conclude that overnight contactless sensing offers modest but promising next-day agitation risk discrimination, while noting that calibration limitations and the need for external validation preclude immediate use in individual clinical decisions.

lateral flow immunoassay diagnostics

Topic mode · generated August 31, 2026 at 19:16 UTC · 18 items

Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.

Li L, Liu F, He X et al.. Recent advances in lateral flow immunoassay for virus detection: current achievements, challenges and future prospects.. Talanta. 2026

This review examines lateral flow immunoassay (LFIA) technology and its role in detecting viral pathogens, reconceptualizing it not merely as a point-of-care tool but as a point-of-need self-testing platform capable of supporting large-scale epidemic surveillance and pandemic preparedness. The authors systematically survey recent advances across three domains: signal generation and transduction strategies employing diverse nanomaterials such as colorimetric, fluorescent, magnetic, surface-enhanced Raman scattering, and photothermal systems; multimodal and multiplex detection approaches aimed at improving sensitivity and throughput; and the integration of machine learning for signal interpretation and system optimization. The review also comprehensively covers LFIA applications in detecting viral antigens, antibodies, and nucleic acids. Current limitations—including sensitivity constraints, target specificity issues, and practical challenges related to cost, usability, and stability—are critically assessed, with future directions proposed around advanced nanomaterials, intelligent data processing, and multi-target detection. Overall, the authors argue that LFIA, particularly colorimetric formats, holds transformative potential as an emergency screening tool and for strengthening decentralized diagnostic capacity in global public health systems.

Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.

Yin J, Qiu Y, Nie Y et al.. Polydopamine nanoparticle-based colorimetric-photothermal dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay for thromboembolism diagnosis.. RSC advances. 2026

This paper investigated whether polydopamine nanoparticles could serve as superior signal labels in lateral flow immunoassays compared to conventional gold nanoparticles, which are constrained by single-mode colorimetric detection and limited sensitivity. The researchers constructed a dual-mode colorimetric and photothermal lateral flow immunoassay platform exploiting the high molar extinction coefficient and broadband ultraviolet-to-near-infrared absorption of polydopamine nanoparticles, targeting two thromboembolism-related biomarkers, thrombomodulin and α2-plasmin inhibitor-plasmin complex, in clinical serum samples. The photothermal readout mode substantially amplified detection sensitivity beyond what colorimetric reading alone could achieve, and clinical validation using serum samples showed strong agreement with established clinical methods. The authors conclude that polydopamine nanoparticle-based dual-mode lateral flow immunoassay represents a feasible and sensitive strategy for point-of-care thromboembolism diagnosis.

A lateral flow microarray immunoassay to detect USUV NS1-specific antibodies in tissues of deceased blackbirds in the Netherlands.

Godarzi B, Holicki CM, Chandler FD et al.. A lateral flow microarray immunoassay to detect USUV NS1-specific antibodies in tissues of deceased blackbirds in the Netherlands.. Virus research. 2026

This study investigated whether a lateral flow microarray immunoassay (LMIA) could be used to detect Usutu virus (USUV)-specific antibodies in tissues collected from deceased Eurasian blackbirds, addressing a gap in current surveillance systems that typically rely on serum from live birds. The researchers tested the LMIA against blood clots and extracts from lung and liver tissues taken from blackbird carcasses found in the Netherlands. Detection of USUV non-structural protein 1 (NS1)-specific antibodies was more likely in lung tissue extracts from RT-PCR-confirmed USUV-positive birds, and the assay showed minimal cross-reactivity with the related West Nile virus NS1 antigen. The findings demonstrate that the LMIA can specifically detect USUV NS1 antibodies in deceased bird specimens, opening the possibility of extending serological surveillance to carcasses found in the field. The authors suggest this approach could enable rapid, on-site analysis of both live and deceased blackbirds, improving monitoring of USUV circulation.

Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.

Yang Z, Yan X, Yang Z et al.. Boronate-mediated antibody orientation: a molecular simulation-guided strategy for ultra-sensitive lateral flow immunoassays.. Mikrochimica acta. 2026

This study addressed the problem of random antibody orientation on lateral flow immunoassay strips, which limits detection sensitivity and reproducibility. The researchers developed a boronate affinity-based strategy that exploits covalent reactions between boronic acid and cis-diol groups on antibody Fc-region glycans to achieve site-specific, oriented immobilization, using molecular dynamics simulations to characterize the binding mechanism and energetics. Simulations revealed that the boronate-antibody configuration achieved the most favorable binding energy and rapid conformational stabilization through a sequential process of electrostatic guidance followed by hydrogen-bond and covalent bond formation. When applied experimentally, the oriented immobilization yielded near-complete antibody conjugation efficiency, greater antigen-binding fragment exposure, and substantially improved affinity compared to conventional passive adsorption. The resulting lateral flow assay for detecting zearalenone in corn achieved a dramatically lower detection limit than traditional colloidal gold assays, with strong agreement to liquid chromatography–tandem mass spectrometry reference measurements, demonstrating a broadly applicable, chemistry-driven framework for next-generation rapid diagnostics.

On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.

Godarzi B, Holicki CM, Chandler FD et al.. On-site serological screening of Usutu virus in wild birds: Field evaluation of a lateral flow microarray immunoassay using the WHO REASSURED framework.. Journal of virological methods. 2026

This study evaluated a novel field-deployable serological assay for detecting Usutu virus (USUV) and West Nile virus (WNV) antibodies in wild birds at capture sites in the Netherlands, where both arboviruses pose increasing public health and ecological concern. The researchers used a species-independent Lateral Flow Microarray ImmunoAssay (LMIA) targeting non-structural protein 1 antibodies for both viruses, testing blood samples from wild birds captured at bird-ringing sites and comparing results against the gold-standard Focus Reduction Neutralization Test. The LMIA demonstrated high sensitivity and strong specificity for USUV detection relative to the gold-standard method, and successfully identified seroconversion in a Eurasian blackbird, which was independently confirmed. Performance of the assay was also evaluated using the WHO REASSURED framework, scoring well against criteria designed to assess diagnostics for point-of-care use in resource-limited settings. The authors conclude that the LMIA is a feasible, rapid, multi-analyte, and equipment-light tool for on-site arbovirus surveillance in wild birds, though broader validation is still recommended before widespread deployment.

Large-scale synthesis of phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles enables an oriented antibody interface for sensitive point-of-care diagnostics.

Xu J, Li Q, Lu B et al.. Large-scale synthesis of phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles enables an oriented antibody interface for sensitive point-of-care diagnostics.. Colloids and surfaces. B, Biointerfaces. 2026

This paper investigated how antibody orientation on gold nanoparticles affects the sensitivity of lateral flow immunoassays and sought to overcome the limitations of conventional citrate-capped nanoparticles, which rely on random antibody adsorption that can obscure antigen-binding sites. The researchers developed a one-pot, room-temperature synthesis method for phenylboronic acid-functionalized gold nanoparticles that could be produced at large scale in a single batch within minutes, without the high-temperature reflux conditions typically required. These nanoparticles enabled oriented antibody immobilization by exploiting boronate-affinity interactions with carbohydrate moieties on antibody Fc fragments, thereby directing antigen-binding sites away from the nanoparticle surface. When tested in a lateral flow assay for fecal occult blood detection, the phenylboronic acid-functionalized nanoparticles achieved dramatically improved sensitivity compared to conventional citrate-capped nanoparticle probes, demonstrating strong potential for clinical point-of-care diagnostic applications.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state and future direction of biosensing technologies designed to detect priority foodborne pathogens, including *Escherichia coli*, *Salmonella*, and *Listeria monocytogenes*, which continue to threaten global public health. The authors survey electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing platforms, evaluating how advances in functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering have improved sensor sensitivity, specificity, and performance in complex food matrices. A central theme is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into these platforms, enabling real-time analytics, intelligent pattern recognition, and high-throughput multiplexing that transform conventional detection systems into smart diagnostic devices. The review critically assesses practical challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, scalability limitations, and the absence of regulatory standardization, while proposing mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, explainable AI, and printable electronics. Looking ahead, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen panel hubs, blockchain integration, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward robust, regulation-ready platforms for next-generation food safety monitoring.

Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives

Shehwaz Anwar, Hajed Obaid A. Alharbi, Ali Yousif Babiker et al.. Oxidative Stress in Health and Disease: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Perspectives. International Journal of Molecular Sciences. 2026

This review examines the dual role of reactive oxygen species in biological systems, where they act as essential signaling molecules at physiological concentrations but drive disease when produced in excess or dysregulated. The paper synthesizes current knowledge on the major types, sources, and physiological functions of reactive oxygen species, as well as the molecular pathways through which oxidative stress causes cellular damage and signaling dysregulation. Methods for detecting and quantifying oxidative stress in clinical and preclinical settings are critically evaluated, including biochemical assays, fluorescent probes, biosensors, and advanced imaging approaches. The review also surveys how oxidative stress contributes to the pathophysiology of cancer, diabetes, cardiovascular disease, neurodegenerative conditions, and inflammatory disorders. The authors conclude by outlining future directions in precision redox medicine, emphasizing targeted antioxidant-based diagnostic and therapeutic strategies informed by improved detection technologies and deeper mechanistic understanding of redox biology.

Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling

Wojciech Chrzanowski, Joy Wolfram. Rethinking Extracellular Vesicle Signaling. Advanced Materials. 2026

This paper examines how extracellular vesicles communicate between cells, challenging the prevailing view that their signaling primarily depends on cellular uptake and release of internal cargo into recipient cells. The authors propose two alternative signaling modes based on surface binding alone: a "bind-and-stay" mechanism, in which vesicles remain attached to a cell and signal from the surface, and a "bind-and-leave" mechanism, in which vesicles transiently bind one cell and then move on to interact with additional cells. This latter mode directly challenges the conventional assumption that a single vesicle signals to only a single recipient cell, suggesting instead that one vesicle can sequentially trigger downstream responses in multiple cells. The paper also outlines experimental approaches that could be used to investigate these surface-binding interactions more rigorously. The authors argue that a more complete understanding of these signaling modes has implications for both fundamental cell biology and the development of extracellular vesicle-based therapeutics.

Mechanistic advances in microbial nanobiotechnology and their applications in sustainable agriculture, environment and biomedicine

Mir Sajad Rabani, Meenakshi Shrivastav, Mudasir Fayaz et al.. Mechanistic advances in microbial nanobiotechnology and their applications in sustainable agriculture, environment and biomedicine. Discover Nano. 2026

This review examines the field of microbial nanobiotechnology, which combines microbiology and nanotechnology to develop sustainable solutions across medicine, agriculture, and environmental management. The authors survey how microorganisms such as bacteria, fungi, and algae can biosynthesize nanoparticles through biochemical pathways that are environmentally benign and cost-effective, yielding particles with controllable size, shape, and functional properties. These biogenic nanoparticles show promise in biomedical contexts including targeted drug delivery, imaging, and antimicrobial treatments, as well as in environmental remediation such as wastewater treatment and pollution control. The review also highlights how tools like genetic engineering and synthetic biology are advancing nanoparticle synthesis and characterization, while noting that scalability, biosafety, regulatory concerns, and ethical implications remain substantial barriers to commercial translation. The authors conclude that addressing these challenges through multidisciplinary collaboration is essential for the field to fulfill its potential in tackling global health, environmental, and industrial problems.

Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads

Karan Malhotra, Louise L. Hansen, Samuel Han et al.. Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads. Journal of the American Chemical Society. 2026

This paper studied the development of an enzyme-free platform for ultrasensitive protein biomarker detection aimed at improving accessibility in resource-limited settings. The researchers created a system called efMOSAIC that uses hybridization chain reaction-based signal amplifiers on magnetic beads, eliminating the need for enzymes that typically require cold chain infrastructure and specialized laboratory conditions. The platform achieves detection limits comparable to established single-molecule digital assays while supporting multiplexed biomarker detection from human plasma samples, including cytokines relevant to clinical workflows. A key finding was that the signal amplifier reagents remain stable when stored at room temperature for up to six months, removing a major logistical barrier for deployment in low- and middle-income countries or point-of-care settings. The authors argue that this combination of ultrasensitive performance, simplified workflows, and temperature-stable reagents positions efMOSAIC as a transformative tool for expanding access to advanced protein diagnostics globally.

Bounds for inertialess dynamo

Ali Arslan, Hezekiah Grayer. Bounds for inertialess dynamo. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper derives rigorous necessary conditions for dynamo action in rotating convection within a magnetohydrodynamic framework, examining both a rapidly rotating inviscid plane layer and a viscous system at arbitrary rotation rates. Unlike classical kinematic dynamo bounds, the analysis couples magnetic field evolution to an inertialess force balance, meaning the flow and magnetic field are not treated independently. The authors establish that the buoyancy-driven component of the flow must satisfy certain norm inequalities involving the magnetic Reynolds number, with a refined anisotropic constraint obtained through a poloidal-toroidal decomposition. For the viscous case, additional necessary conditions are derived for the growth of magnetic enstrophy and combined thermo-magnetic energy. A key result is that a specific scaling relationship between the Rayleigh number and Ekman number is necessary for dynamo action to occur, providing new constraints linking convective forcing to rotational effects in planetary and astrophysical dynamo contexts.

Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a machine-learning algorithm for generating lattice field configurations that is guided by renormalization-group principles. The method works by approximately inverting a renormalization-group transformation: long-distance modes are supplied through a perfect blocking construction, while a conditional normalizing flow reconstructs the missing short-distance details, and brief rethermalization corrects residual errors. The approach is tested on two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, where the algorithm is applied recursively in cascades to reach lattice sizes far larger than those used during training. A key finding is that a normalizing flow trained on relatively small lattices can be reused at each scale in the cascade to generate physically correct configurations at much larger lattice sizes, demonstrating strong generalization across length scales.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes and validates a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The method optimizes the blocking using a "perfect blocking" condition so that the coarse-grained lattice distribution is captured by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step and a decimation step, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom lost during decimation. A brief rethermalization step corrects residual mismatches, and because long-distance modes are supplied by the coarse ensemble, the procedure can be applied recursively to scale up to much larger lattices from a small initial ensemble. Testing in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, the authors demonstrate stable cascade upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume on local computational resources. Rethermalization tests confirm that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while mismatches in the relevant thermal direction relax much more slowly, and the authors argue the approach has natural extensions toward higher-dimensional, gauge, and fermionic theories.

Quantum Fourier transform for the symmetric group

Carli Bruinsma, Dmitry Grinko, Maris Ozols. Quantum Fourier transform for the symmetric group. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper revisits the quantum Fourier transform (QFT) for the symmetric group, a canonical example of a non-abelian QFT that is fundamental to quantum computing. Building on earlier constructions, the authors carefully reanalyze the gate complexity and circuit depth of the existing algorithm. They find that the previously reported complexities require revision, correcting them to specific polynomial bounds in terms of the group size parameter. Additionally, the authors identify a suboptimality in the choice of transversal elements used in the construction and propose a simpler, improved realization of the symmetric group QFT.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses a fundamental open question about quantum resources: how much of the "magic" (nonstabilizerness) in a shared quantum state arises from correlations between parties rather than from local properties alone. The authors solved this optimization problem exactly for the stabilizer fidelity, showing that the nonlocal magic of any pure multiqubit state is determined by how far its entanglement spectrum lies from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. This framework reveals an unexpected connection to entanglement embezzlement: a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. Crucially, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but rather how the entanglement spectrum distributes its weight across factor-of-two windows of rank, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states can share identical logarithmic entanglement scaling yet possess unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The results make nonlocal magic an operationally meaningful measure of quantum correlations that is accessible through tensor-network simulations and entanglement spectroscopy experiments.

Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach

Adil Alshammari, Hayretdin Bahsi. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of verifying accountability in cross-organization agent-to-agent messaging workflows when live systems are unavailable, controlled by a single party, or not trusted by all participants. The authors developed a preserved evidence-bundle model in which each workflow event carries policy-required cryptographic evidence — including sender authentication, log commitments, witness-backed checkpoints, append-only continuity proofs, delegation authorization, and receiver-signed receipts — alongside a policy-controlled offline verifier that evaluates whether a bundle meets explicitly stated evidentiary requirements. The verifier deliberately avoids inferring delivery or receipt from transport behavior or log inclusion alone, requiring instead that all accepted claims be directly supported by preserved evidence. In a prototype evaluation spanning hundreds of complete workflows and valid preserved bundles, the system rejected all corrupted or policy-insufficient bundles with no false acceptances, while checkpoint-context anchoring was identified as the highest-latency component. The work positions itself as providing an auditor-centered, offline basis for dispute review that does not depend on platform-specific logs or cooperative live services.

Feasibility, Acceptability, and Test Performance of Point-of-Care Nucleic Acid Tests for HIV Testing and Viral Load Monitoring in the United States: Prospective Longitudinal Mixed-Methods Study.

Niemann LA, Violette LR, Cornelius-Hudson A et al.. Feasibility, Acceptability, and Test Performance of Point-of-Care Nucleic Acid Tests for HIV Testing and Viral Load Monitoring in the United States: Prospective Longitudinal Mixed-Methods Study.. JMIR research protocols. 2026

This study examined the feasibility, acceptability, and diagnostic performance of point-of-care nucleic acid tests for HIV detection and viral load monitoring in clinical and community settings in the United States. The researchers enrolled participants across two sites in Seattle, Washington, including individuals seeking HIV testing, pre-exposure prophylaxis, or post-exposure prophylaxis, as well as people already living with HIV, using qualitative and semiquantitative point-of-care nucleic acid test devices alongside standard laboratory testing for comparison. The study employed a mixed-methods prospective design that included a randomized clinical trial arm, acceptability surveys, in-depth interviews, and time-and-motion observations to comprehensively assess implementation outcomes. Enrollment was completed with several hundred participants across multiple study groups, though the primary analyses evaluating test performance, impact on time to viral suppression, and acceptability are not yet complete and are anticipated in forthcoming publications. The abstract is essentially a protocol and feasibility report, and no performance or outcome results are yet available to summarize.

Brief Report: Association Between Pharmacologic Tenofovir Adherence Measures and Subsequent 24-Week Viral Load Outcomes for People With HIV in South Africa.

Bodley N, Govender K, Moodley P et al.. Brief Report: Association Between Pharmacologic Tenofovir Adherence Measures and Subsequent 24-Week Viral Load Outcomes for People With HIV in South Africa.. Journal of acquired immune deficiency syndromes (1999). 2026

This study evaluated whether pharmacologic measures of tenofovir adherence could predict future HIV viremia among people with HIV receiving antiretroviral therapy in South Africa. Researchers compared three adherence measures — a point-of-care urine tenofovir assay, quantitative urine tenofovir by liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry, and tenofovir-diphosphate concentrations in dried blood spots — and examined their associations with current and subsequent viral load outcomes at twenty-four weeks using logistic regression. Tenofovir-diphosphate in dried blood spots was the only measure significantly associated with viremia at twenty-four weeks, suggesting it reflects longer-term adherence patterns most relevant to future viral suppression. An undetectable point-of-care urine tenofovir result was associated with current viremia, lower CD4 counts, and likely recent treatment initiation, but did not predict future viremia. The findings suggest that dried blood spot tenofovir-diphosphate measurement is the most useful pharmacologic tool for identifying patients at risk of subsequent viremia and guiding targeted adherence interventions.

Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?

Richard J. Simpson, Richard J. Simpson, Maree Gleeson et al.. Can exercise affect immune function to increase susceptibility to infection?. PubMed. 2026

This debate article examines a central and contested question in exercise immunology: whether exercise itself can suppress immune function and increase susceptibility to infection. The authors structured the discussion around opposing arguments concerning whether high-performance athletes face greater infection risk than the general population, whether exercise independently alters immunity beyond confounding factors such as sleep disruption, psychological stress, travel, and nutritional deficits, and whether the biomarkers and study designs used to assess immune health in this context are adequate. There is broad agreement that regular, moderate-intensity exercise of shorter duration benefits immune defense, particularly in older or chronically ill individuals, whereas arduous exercise characteristic of elite athletes and military personnel has long been thought to suppress immunity. However, the extent to which exercise per se, rather than the constellation of stressors accompanying intense training and competition, causally drives increased infection risk remains unresolved. Both sides agree that infection susceptibility is multifactorial, and the article calls for more rigorous empirical research to disentangle the specific contribution of exercise from other shared immune-activating factors in these populations.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review paper examines how the integration of multiomics technologies with artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and clinical translation. The authors analyze multiomics approaches spanning genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics alongside advanced AI methodologies including deep learning, exploring their combined application across the drug development pipeline. The paper covers specific applications such as target deconvolution, drug repositioning, de novo compound discovery, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment, with case examples drawn from oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions. The authors find that this convergence holds substantial promise for accelerating target identification, optimizing lead compounds, and improving clinical trial design, while acknowledging persistent challenges including data harmonization, reproducibility, and algorithmic bias. The review concludes that the fusion of multiomics and AI represents a foundational shift toward more precise, data-driven therapeutic innovation in bench-to-clinic translational medicine.

Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients

Christopher C. Butler, Andrew Pinto, Victoria Harris et al.. Oral Nirmatrelvir–Ritonavir for Covid-19 in Higher-Risk Outpatients. New England Journal of Medicine. 2026

These two open-label platform trials examined whether nirmatrelvir–ritonavir reduces hospitalization or death in vaccinated or previously infected higher-risk adults with COVID-19, a population distinct from the unvaccinated high-risk outpatients in whom the drug originally demonstrated benefit. Conducted in the United Kingdom and Canada, the trials randomly assigned community-dwelling adults who tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 and had been symptomatic for five days or fewer to receive either nirmatrelvir–ritonavir plus usual care or usual care alone, with the primary outcome being hospitalization or death within 28 days. Rates of hospitalization or death were very low and nearly identical between treatment and control groups in both trials, and neither trial demonstrated a statistically meaningful probability of superiority for the drug. Although a substudy confirmed that nirmatrelvir–ritonavir reduced viral load by the end of treatment, this virological effect did not translate into a clinical benefit. The authors conclude that nirmatrelvir–ritonavir does not reduce hospitalization or death among vaccinated higher-risk outpatients with SARS-CoV-2 infection.

CRISPR-Cas systems as next-generation antimicrobials: a systemic review of mechanisms, delivery strategies, and translational challenges

Maha S.I. Wizrah. CRISPR-Cas systems as next-generation antimicrobials: a systemic review of mechanisms, delivery strategies, and translational challenges. Frontiers in Microbiology. 2026

This systematic review examined the potential of CRISPR-Cas systems as precision antimicrobials against multidrug-resistant bacteria, which have increasingly rendered conventional antibiotics ineffective. The authors synthesized preclinical and experimental evidence on different CRISPR effectors, including Cas9, Cas12, and Cas13, evaluating their mechanisms of action, delivery platforms, and therapeutic outcomes against resistant bacterial targets. The reviewed studies demonstrated that CRISPR-Cas tools can kill or resensitize resistant bacteria through mechanisms such as chromosomal double-strand break induction, resistance plasmid elimination, and RNA-targeted cleavage, with phage-mediated delivery showing the most consistent efficacy across complex environments and animal models. One CRISPR-enhanced engineered bacteriophage cocktail has progressed to clinical evaluation, representing a meaningful translational milestone. Nevertheless, the review identifies substantial remaining barriers including narrow host range, physiological instability, emergence of escape mutations, and insufficient long-term safety data, concluding that optimized delivery strategies, multiplex targeting, and standardized regulatory frameworks will be necessary for clinical translation.

Chitosan Derivatives: Challenges and Opportunities in the Green and Sustainable Transition Era

Ana Morais, Rita Lima, Madalena Pinto et al.. Chitosan Derivatives: Challenges and Opportunities in the Green and Sustainable Transition Era. Molecules. 2026

This review examines the production and chemical modification of chitosan derivatives through the lens of green and sustainable chemistry principles. The authors surveyed the full production pipeline, from chitin isolation across diverse natural sources and deacetylation through to downstream chemical modification of chitosan. The review highlights sustainable methodologies including eco-efficient isolation and purification strategies, continuous-flow processes, and greener synthetic approaches such as click chemistry. The authors argue that interdisciplinary collaboration and a holistic approach to process design are essential for advancing cost-effective, safe, and high-performing chitosan derivatives while minimizing environmental impact.

A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion

Marcus DuPont. A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper establishes and analyzes an exact scaling symmetry in the problem of atomic gas accreting onto a point mass, such as a black hole embedded in an ambient medium. The authors prove that simultaneously rescaling the black hole mass and ambient number density by inverse factors leaves all dimensionless physical quantities—including optical depths, Eddington ratios, and variability patterns—unchanged, and demonstrate this rescaling is unique within its class. They introduce a dimensionless growth parameter that measures how much mass is gained per Bondi time, showing it is equivalent to the expansion speed of the Bondi radius in units of the ambient sound speed, so that a value of unity corresponds to sonic dilation of the accretion sphere. Using this parameter, they identify a finite mass threshold beyond which rapidly accreting systems—including canonical hyper-Eddington accretion scenarios—break the similarity, and show that no nontrivial stationary growing solution can simultaneously preserve the atomic scaling symmetry and self-consistent radiative flux. The theorem thus provides a unified framework connecting radiating Bondi accretion and feedback-regulated scalings while delineating the regime where quasi-static, fixed-mass approximations lose validity.

PULSAR: Pooled Unified Late-Interaction Search and Retrieval for Enterprise Visual Document RAG

Benjamin Constable, Anup Roy, Vishal Sharma et al.. PULSAR: Pooled Unified Late-Interaction Search and Retrieval for Enterprise Visual Document RAG. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper presents PULSAR, a vision-first retrieval system designed for searching visually dense financial documents such as pitch decks and board packs in an enterprise investment context. The system indexes document page images directly using a frozen ColPali-style backbone with a two-stage late-interaction approach, where compact page summaries enable initial retrieval and a finer pooled representation handles exact rescoring, eliminating the need for costly optical character recognition and figure verbalization pipelines. The pooled architecture dramatically reduces vector-search latency and supports far higher query throughput under concurrent load compared to unpooled configurations, while maintaining nearly equivalent retrieval quality as measured by standard ranking metrics. Ingestion costs are substantially lower than the OCR-based baseline the system replaced, and in production deployment the system more than doubles answer-fact recall over that baseline. The work demonstrates that a vision-native, pooled late-interaction design can meet both the latency and freshness demands of high-volume, time-sensitive financial document retrieval at scale.

Quadratic Probing Insertions Are $ε^{-(1+o(1))}$

Yang Hu, William Kuszmaul, Jingxun Liang et al.. Quadratic Probing Insertions Are $ε^{-(1+o(1))}$. arXiv. 2026 preprint

Quadratic probing is a classical open-addressing hash table scheme in which collisions are resolved by probing positions at quadratically increasing offsets, yet its theoretical performance has remained poorly understood despite decades of use. The central conjecture holds that at load factor one minus epsilon, the expected insertion time should be on the order of the reciprocal of epsilon, but even establishing any bound expressible purely as a function of that reciprocal had been an open problem since the scheme was introduced. This paper proves that the expected insertion time is epsilon to the power of negative one plus a sub-polynomial correction, thereby settling the complexity of quadratic probing up to sub-polynomial factors and coming within a sub-polynomial gap of the conjectured optimal bound.

A Single Spin Switches the Steady-State Phase of an Open Quantum System

Jianwen Jie. A Single Spin Switches the Steady-State Phase of an Open Quantum System. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how adding a single spin to a dissipative many-body quantum system can dramatically alter its macroscopic steady-state behavior, challenging the conventional expectation that one-constituent changes produce negligible effects on intensive observables. The authors study a collective spin model with dissipation imbalance and show that the parity of the total spin number determines whether the Dicke ladder samples a zero of the nonlinear-loss amplitude in the jump operator, a structural difference that controls whether certain quantum states are transient or form a recurrent class. Through exact finite-size calculations, they demonstrate that exponential competition between stationary weights amplifies this microscopic connectivity difference, producing a first-order dissipative phase transition that switches the steady state between a phase-averaged ring and a south-polar fixed point, but only along the odd-spin-number sequence. Single-spin loading confirms that rare quantum fluctuations are sufficient to induce this phase switch without any postselection, making the effect physically accessible. The findings identify zeros in jump operators as a general mechanism by which single-constituent changes can exert control over the phase of an open quantum system.

MaCoPlanner: LLM-Assisted Manual-Compiled Task Planning with Proactive Safety Verification for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation

Guipeng Xin, Jiahe Xua, Mohammad Deghat et al.. MaCoPlanner: LLM-Assisted Manual-Compiled Task Planning with Proactive Safety Verification for Robotic Industrial Panel Operation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the challenge of robotic operation of industrial control panels, which requires robots to follow complex operating procedures, safety rules, and device-state constraints scattered across heterogeneous equipment manuals. The authors developed MaCoPlanner, a task-planning framework that compiles equipment manuals into a typed intermediate representation, retrieves relevant evidence for plan generation, and symbolically verifies candidate plans against procedural and state-transition constraints before any physical action is taken. Detected constraint violations are localized and fed back for targeted repair, while plans that cannot be resolved within a refinement budget are rejected outright, and a separate execution interface grounds verified symbolic actions to physical controls. The system achieved a notably low final violation rate and substantially improved task success rates compared to a baseline that used raw manuals directly, with gains observed across both moderately and highly complex task levels. Experiments on a controller-panel simulator further demonstrated the feasibility of integrated execution under representative conditions, though the authors explicitly note the system is not claimed to be ready for real industrial deployment.

Unsteady airfoil aerodynamics in attached flow: From unsteady thin airfoil theory to wind turbine application

Ang Li, Mac Gaunaa, Georg Raimund Pirrung. Unsteady airfoil aerodynamics in attached flow: From unsteady thin airfoil theory to wind turbine application. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the formulation and implementation of attached-flow unsteady aerodynamics for wind turbine solvers, focusing on the region of the blade where flow remains predominantly attached and which dominates power production and loading. Starting from classical unsteady thin-airfoil theory, the authors derived circulatory and non-circulatory loads expressed as lift, drag, and moment coefficients compatible with blade-element momentum, lifting-line, and actuator-line methods, using shed-wake memory cast in terms of downwash velocity rather than angle of attack. The work identifies key modeling choices and clarifies how airfoil polar lookup, shed-wake memory, non-circulatory loads, consistent force definitions, and separated-flow dynamics combine into a complete engineering model. Verification through a coned straight-blade benchmark revealed that omitting certain contributions introduces meaningful errors in thrust and power, with lift-direction projection having the largest influence on power and the omission of a mid-chord heaving-acceleration term disrupting non-circulatory normal-force cancellation. A vertical-axis wind turbine case in the zero-onset-flow, ideal thin-airfoil limit confirmed that all circulatory and non-circulatory contributions cancel exactly, yielding zero net rotor torque and validating the theoretical consistency of the formulation.

Brief Report: Sustainability of the Effects of Linkage Case Management on Posthospitalization HIV Outcomes: The Daraja Randomized Clinical Trial.

Willkens M, Issarow B, Kisigo GA et al.. Brief Report: Sustainability of the Effects of Linkage Case Management on Posthospitalization HIV Outcomes: The Daraja Randomized Clinical Trial.. Journal of acquired immune deficiency syndromes (1999). 2026

The Daraja randomized clinical trial examined whether the benefits of a linkage case management intervention for hospitalized people with HIV would persist beyond its primary endpoint. Researchers followed participants for an additional year after the initial twelve-month assessment, evaluating continuity in HIV care, antiretroviral therapy adherence, and viral load suppression at twenty-four months post-hospital discharge. Intervention participants demonstrated significantly higher rates of all three outcomes compared to those receiving standard care, with differences exceeding ten percentage points across each measure. The intervention's effect on time to HIV clinic linkage and antiretroviral therapy initiation also remained significant at two years, with a notable proportion of control participants still untreated at that point. These findings indicate that linkage case management initiated at hospitalization produces durable improvements in HIV care engagement and viral suppression well beyond the active intervention period.

New to cohort, not new to care: leveraging historical data in HIV clinical cohorts in the United States.

Haw NJL, Hogan B, Humes E et al.. New to cohort, not new to care: leveraging historical data in HIV clinical cohorts in the United States.. American journal of epidemiology. 2026

This paper examined how to distinguish patients newly entering HIV care from those transferring from prior care within United States clinical cohorts, a distinction important for accurate research on care outcomes. Using data from a large multi-cohort collaboration spanning over two decades, the researchers assessed the availability of five types of historical HIV data at enrollment, including antiretroviral therapy prescriptions, CD4 counts, viral loads, suppressed viral load at enrollment, and AIDS-defining conditions. They developed an algorithm to classify participants as new to care, transfers, re-engaging after a gap, or previously in care with unknown transfer status. The analysis revealed a substantial shift over time, with transfers becoming the dominant enrollment category while those truly new to care declined, and the gap between earliest known HIV history and cohort enrollment grew considerably. The authors recommend that HIV cohorts systematically collect all five data types and apply the proposed algorithm, tailored to their specific context, to improve the accuracy of identifying participants who are genuinely new to HIV care.

Diagnostic accuracy of point-of-care urine tenofovir test, and associations between metrics of tenofovir use and treatment outcomes in a community ART programme

Naidu L, Tlhaku K, Govender K et al.. Diagnostic accuracy of point-of-care urine tenofovir test, and associations between metrics of tenofovir use and treatment outcomes in a community ART programme. 2026

This study evaluated two methods of measuring tenofovir adherence—a point-of-care urine tenofovir assay and dried blood spot tenofovir diphosphate concentrations—in a South African community antiretroviral therapy programme, examining both the diagnostic accuracy of the point-of-care test and the relationship between these adherence metrics and treatment outcomes. Researchers measured tenofovir concentrations using liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry as the reference standard and assessed associations between the adherence measures, concurrent viraemia, and retention in care over sixteen weeks. The point-of-care urine tenofovir assay demonstrated high sensitivity but only moderate specificity compared to the reference method. The vast majority of participants without viraemia had detectable urine tenofovir or high dried blood spot tenofovir diphosphate levels, consistent with good short- and medium-term adherence, though neither adherence measure was associated with retention in care at sixteen weeks.

Diagnostic performance of a field-deployable iiPCR platform and viral load dynamics of MPXV Clade IIb in a cohort with high prevalence of HIV.

Liu LT, Chen CJ, Lin PC et al.. Diagnostic performance of a field-deployable iiPCR platform and viral load dynamics of MPXV Clade IIb in a cohort with high prevalence of HIV.. Annals of medicine. 2026

This study evaluated a field-deployable insulated isothermal PCR platform as an alternative to standard quantitative real-time PCR for diagnosing Mpox virus Clade IIb infection, with particular attention to diagnostic performance across specimen types and in a cohort with high HIV prevalence. Researchers prospectively enrolled confirmed Mpox patients and tested multiple specimen types using both the portable iiPCR system and reference qPCR, while also tracking viral load dynamics over the course of illness and performing phylogenetic characterization. The iiPCR platform achieved analytical sensitivity and qualitative concordance identical to qPCR, with lesion-derived specimens consistently yielding the highest detection rates and lowest cycle threshold values compared to non-lesion specimens. Among people living with HIV who were receiving antiretroviral therapy and had preserved immune function, no differences in diagnostic performance or viral load dynamics were observed relative to HIV-negative individuals. These findings support the potential of the portable iiPCR system as a decentralized diagnostic tool for Mpox, especially when lesion specimens are available.

malaria rapid diagnostic test

Topic mode · generated August 31, 2026 at 19:12 UTC · 14 items

Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.

Bogale A, Mitiku A, Tefera S et al.. Comparative evaluation of a multi-antigen malaria rapid diagnostic test against qPCR and microscopy in a co-endemic area of Southern Ethiopia.. Scientific reports. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a multi-antigen rapid diagnostic test for malaria in a region of Southern Ethiopia where both *Plasmodium falciparum* and *Plasmodium vivax* co-circulate, a setting complicated by low-density infections and gene deletions that can undermine conventional testing. Researchers enrolled febrile patients from health facilities during peak transmission season and compared the Bioline Malaria multi-Ag RDT, which detects HRP2 and dual pLDH antigens, against expert microscopy and quantitative PCR. The multi-antigen RDT performed well relative to microscopy, showing high sensitivity and specificity with near-perfect agreement, but its sensitivity dropped meaningfully when qPCR served as the reference standard, revealing that submicroscopic infections were frequently missed. Overall, qPCR detected the highest proportion of malaria cases, including more species-specific and mixed infections than either RDT or microscopy. The authors conclude that while the multi-antigen RDT is suitable for routine clinical diagnosis, more sensitive tools are needed for surveillance and elimination efforts in co-endemic settings.

Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.

Matulis GA, Smith HP, Hall G et al.. Characterization of arbovirus infections in patients within Haiti by screening discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes.. PLoS neglected tropical diseases. 2026

This study investigated circulating arboviruses in Haiti during a period when traditional surveillance was disrupted by political instability, using an innovative approach of extracting nucleic acids from discarded malaria rapid diagnostic test cassettes collected from febrile patients. Samples were screened using a series of real-time PCR assays targeting flaviviruses, alphaviruses, and Oropouche virus, followed by next-generation sequencing and phylogenetic analysis on positive samples. The predominant finding was dengue virus serotype three, detected exclusively in samples from a single year, with phylogenetic analysis placing these sequences in close relationship to strains circulating across the Caribbean region during the same period. No evidence of alphavirus or Oropouche virus infections was detected in the subset of samples tested for those pathogens. The study demonstrates that discarded malaria rapid diagnostic tests can serve as a practical and resource-efficient surveillance tool for arboviral diseases in resource-limited or insecure settings where conventional public health infrastructure is compromised.

ZigBee Based Low Latency IoT and AI Integrated Framework for Real Time Telehealth Monitoring

B. V. Ravi Kumar, Mohammad Shahnawaz Shaikh. ZigBee Based Low Latency IoT and AI Integrated Framework for Real Time Telehealth Monitoring. Emerging Science Journal. 2026

This paper presents a wireless telehealth monitoring framework that integrates IoT sensors with cloud-based artificial intelligence to enable continuous, real-time health surveillance for elderly and chronically ill patients. The system uses the ZigBee communication protocol to transmit physiological data—including heart rate, body temperature, and blood oxygen levels—from wearable sensors to a centralized processing unit with minimal power consumption and low latency. Multiple machine learning algorithms, including random forest, support vector machine, and logistic regression, were applied to detect health anomalies, with the random forest model achieving the highest performance in terms of accuracy and recall. The framework was validated in a real-world care facility setting, demonstrating suitability for long-term deployment through its low delay and energy efficiency. The authors claim the system addresses key limitations of existing telehealth solutions—such as high latency and excessive false alarms—while offering a scalable, modular architecture that can accommodate additional sensor types to meet diverse patient needs.
