Biosynthesis of cinchona alkaloids

Blaise Kimbadi Lombe, Tingan Zhou, Gyumin Kang et al.. Biosynthesis of cinchona alkaloids. Nature. 2026

This paper investigated the biosynthetic pathway by which cinchona plants produce their characteristic alkaloids, including the antimalarial drug quinine and the chiral catalyst cinchonidine, whose biosynthetic origins have remained largely unknown. Using a combination of isotopic labelling, gene silencing, single-nucleus RNA sequencing, and comparative transcriptomics, the researchers identified the genes responsible for constructing the distinctive quinoline-quinuclidine scaffold of these compounds, revealing several unexpected enzymatic transformations including an unusual cyclization that produces a previously unreported quaternary amine intermediate. Heterologous expression of the discovered genes in *Nicotiana benthamiana* enabled production of multiple cinchona alkaloid intermediates, and the same system could accept non-native fluorinated and chlorinated tryptamine substrates to generate halogenated analogues. These findings resolve a longstanding mystery in plant biochemistry and open avenues for producing cinchona alkaloids and novel derivatives through metabolic engineering.

Safety of a large language model-based clinical decision support system in African primary healthcare

Ambrose Agweyu, Paul Mwaniki, Wilkister Musau et al.. Safety of a large language model-based clinical decision support system in African primary healthcare. Nature Health. 2026

This study evaluated the safety and clinical utility of a large language model embedded within electronic medical records to support clinical decision-making at primary care clinics in Kenya. Trained physicians retrospectively reviewed over a thousand patient encounter records generated with the system's assistance, assessing for hallucinations, guideline alignment, and the presence of harmful or beneficial recommendations. The system rarely hallucinated and almost universally aligned with local clinical guidelines, yet clinicians frequently left documentation unmodified, meaning both harmful and helpful suggestions were often carried forward unchanged. Critically, actively harmful recommendations from the model appeared in a meaningful proportion of final clinical documents, while the system's capacity to mitigate pre-existing clinical risk in initial notes was also demonstrated but less frequently realised. The authors conclude that although the tool shows promise for quality improvement in African primary healthcare, the tendency for clinicians to adopt harmful outputs more readily than beneficial ones highlights urgent need for improved usability, locally tailored safety guardrails, and prospective trials to establish patient-level benefit.

Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies

Carsyn R. Travis, Jared McMaster, Fatima Rivas. Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies. Molecules. 2026

This review examines recent advances in the extraction and isolation of natural products, motivated by the renewed centrality of nature-derived compounds in modern drug discovery. The authors survey a broad range of established and emerging methodologies, including green and designer solvent systems, pressurized and ultrasound-assisted extraction, supercritical fluid techniques, and integrated purification workflows. A particular focus is placed on the growing role of artificial intelligence and machine learning in optimizing solvent selection, extraction conditions, and metabolite dereplication within complex biological matrices. Together, these developments have improved compound recovery, selectivity, and analytical throughput while reducing processing time. The review concludes that combinatorial extraction strategies and AI-driven approaches have collectively expanded the scope of detectable and characterizable metabolites over the recent period surveyed.

Macrophage metabolic exhaustion and PANoptotic cell death drive chronic tissue inflammation in rheumatoid arthritis

Tao Huang, Yoshinori Takashima, Jitendra Kumar et al.. Macrophage metabolic exhaustion and PANoptotic cell death drive chronic tissue inflammation in rheumatoid arthritis. Immunity. 2026

This study investigated why chronic joint inflammation persists in rheumatoid arthritis despite the presence of macrophages normally associated with tissue repair. The researchers found that patient-derived synovial repair-type macrophages produce the complement protein C1q and simultaneously express its receptor, creating an autocrine signaling loop that drives disease. C1q stimulation induced a state of metabolic exhaustion in these macrophages, characterized by impaired energy production and NAD cleavage by the mitochondrial enzyme SARM1, which converted NAD into cyclic ADP ribose and triggered a form of inflammatory cell death called PANoptosis. In animal experiments, C1q administration worsened synovitis while inhibiting SARM1 provided therapeutic benefit. The findings establish SARM1 as both a marker of metabolically stressed macrophages and a key executor of the pro-inflammatory cell death that sustains autoimmune tissue destruction in rheumatoid arthritis.

A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion

Marcus DuPont. A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper studies the mathematical structure of gas accretion onto a point mass in an atomic medium, asking whether and when the governing physics admits an exact scaling symmetry. The authors prove that simultaneously rescaling the black hole mass and ambient density by inverse factors leaves all dimensionless physical quantities—including optical depths, Eddington ratios, and variability—unchanged, and demonstrate this rescaling is unique within the class of two-body atomic microphysics and a specified family of radiation and feedback laws. They introduce a dimensionless growth parameter defined as the fractional mass gained per Bondi time, showing it equals the Bondi radius expansion speed in units of the ambient sound speed, so that a value of unity corresponds to sonic dilation of the accretion radius. When the retained accretion rate follows a power-law in black hole mass, the system reaches this sonic boundary after a finite mass increase, and at most a finite number of additional Bondi times remain before the similarity framework loses control; the authors note that canonical hyper-Eddington accretion scenarios have already crossed this threshold. Additionally, they prove that no nontrivial stationary growing solution can simultaneously preserve the atomic similarity and self-consistent radiative flux, thereby unifying radiating Bondi and feedback-regulated accretion scalings within a single theoretical framework while delineating its limits.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The approach optimizes the blocking using a "perfect blocking" condition to ensure the coarse-lattice distribution is well described by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom that decimation removes. A brief rethermalization step corrects residual mismatches, and because the coarse ensemble provides long-distance modes, the same blocking and flow can be applied recursively to generate configurations on progressively larger lattices from a single small-volume ensemble. The method is validated in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, demonstrating stable cascade upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume on local computational resources, with rethermalization tests confirming that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while infrared mismatches relax slowly as expected. The authors argue that the constituent components of the construction extend naturally toward higher-dimensional theories, including those with gauge and fermionic degrees of freedom.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the fundamental question of how much "magic" (nonstabilizerness), the resource enabling universal quantum computation beyond Clifford circuits, resides specifically in the correlations between spatially separated parties rather than in their local degrees of freedom. The authors solve an open optimization problem by deriving an exact formula for the nonlocal magic of pure multiqubit states in terms of stabilizer fidelity, showing it equals the distance of a state's entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. They further establish a surprising connection to entanglement embezzlement, demonstrating that a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. Notably, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum is distributed across rank windows, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet phases can have identical logarithmic entanglement scaling yet carry unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The authors argue that nonlocal magic is an operationally meaningful characterization of quantum correlations that is accessible both to tensor-network methods and to experimental entanglement spectroscopy.

QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions

Maria Vlachou, Anna Murphy Høgenhaug, Mohammad N. S. Jahromi et al.. QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper addresses the challenge of automatically identifying credibility-related factors in legal decisions on asylum applications, which are lengthy, complex documents combining applicant narratives, original decisions, and supporting materials. The authors developed QUEST, an information retrieval system that combines synthetic query generation, topic extraction, and relevance assessment to locate information pertaining to credibility indicators within Danish asylum appeal board materials. Beyond standard retrieval evaluation metrics, they introduced a novel domain-specific evaluation framework that assesses system performance according to credibility-based relevance rather than traditional topical relevance. Their findings show that performance estimation becomes substantially more difficult when using credibility-based assessments, highlighting the particular challenge of automatically extracting credibility factors from asylum legal documents.

Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach

Adil Alshammari, Hayretdin Bahsi. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of verifying accountability for cross-organization agent-to-agent messaging workflows when live systems are unavailable, controlled by a single party, or not trusted by all participants. The authors propose a preserved evidence-bundle model in which each workflow event carries policy-required evidence components, including sender authentication, authenticated log commitments, witness-backed checkpoints, append-only continuity proofs, delegation-aware authorization evidence, and receiver-signed receipts where mandated. A policy-controlled offline verifier evaluates these bundles against explicit policy profiles rather than inferring delivery or receipt from transport behavior alone, ensuring that only claims supported by sufficient evidence are accepted. A prototype evaluation across hundreds of complete workflows and preserved bundles measured offline verification latency across different policy profiles and found that checkpoint-context anchoring imposed the greatest latency, while delegation and workflow-prerequisite checks required additional steps. Notably, all corrupted or policy-insufficient bundles were rejected with no false acceptances, supporting the approach's suitability for evidence-based audit and dispute resolution without dependence on live infrastructure.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the computational complexity of a hypothesis testing problem: distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph. To address this, the authors develop the first analysis of low-degree polynomial threshold functions in this setting, a framework commonly used to establish computational lower bounds for statistical problems. A key technical contribution along the way is a new characterization of the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic lengths, which generalizes several prior results on random regular graphs and random lifts. The findings extend the reach of polynomial threshold function methods to this graph-theoretic detection problem and provide foundational distributional results that unify and broaden earlier work in the area.

tuberculosis rapid diagnostic test

Topic mode · generated August 31, 2026 at 19:10 UTC · 18 items

Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.

Walzl G, McHugh TD, Chakaya J et al.. Advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarkers, 2020-26: scientific evidence, recommendations, gaps, and developmental priorities.. The Lancet. Microbe. 2027

This paper reviews major advances in tuberculosis diagnostics and biomarker research over a five-year period, culminating in updated WHO policy guidance that consolidates recommendations for detecting tuberculosis disease and drug resistance into a single framework. Key developments include the expanded role of digital chest radiography with computer-aided detection as a screening and triage tool, and the broadened deployment of decentralised molecular diagnostics, including low-complexity automated and manual nucleic acid amplification tests, near point-of-care platforms, and novel specimen types such as tongue swabs and pooled sputum. Progress in non-sputum specimen testing has improved bacteriological confirmation in children and sputum-scarce populations, while advances in rapid molecular drug-resistance testing and next-generation sequencing have strengthened comprehensive susceptibility testing at reference laboratory level. Urine-based lipoarabinomannan assays offer diagnostic benefit in people with HIV and advanced disease, and host blood transcriptomic signatures show promise for prognosis and triage, alongside emerging proteomic, metabolomic, and other biomarker platforms. Despite these advances, the paper concludes that significant performance, implementation, and equity gaps persist, and no biomarker has yet met WHO target criteria for a point-of-care test capable of reliably distinguishing infection from disease, including subclinical tuberculosis.

The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.

Maartens G. The changing landscape of diagnosing HIV-associated tuberculosis.. South African medical journal = Suid-Afrikaanse tydskrif vir geneeskunde. 2026

This narrative review examines how the diagnosis of tuberculosis in people with HIV has evolved from 1990 to the present day. The authors highlight the particular diagnostic challenges posed by this co-infection, including lower bacillary burden in sputum, frequent extrapulmonary or disseminated disease, and nonspecific clinical features that overlap with other opportunistic infections, all of which demand rapid diagnostic approaches. The review traces the limitations of early reliance on smear microscopy, which had reduced sensitivity in people with HIV, and describes how diagnostic algorithms for smear-negative cases led to both under- and overdiagnosis with serious consequences for patient outcomes. The introduction of nucleic acid amplification tests and urine lipoarabinomannan lateral flow assays, alongside the widespread use of antiretroviral therapy, has substantially transformed the diagnostic landscape and contributed to reductions in tuberculosis incidence and mortality. Nevertheless, the authors conclude that existing rapid tests remain imperfect in sensitivity and that only the urine lipoarabinomannan assay meets criteria for affordability and point-of-care suitability, underscoring the continued need for novel, accurate, and accessible diagnostic tools.

Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.

Silva DHMD, Valença ABM, Ferreira MRL et al.. Operationalization of rapid molecular test coverage for tuberculosis in different international contexts: a scoping review.. Revista da Escola de Enfermagem da U S P. 2026

This scoping review examined how coverage of rapid molecular testing for tuberculosis has been defined, measured, and operationalized across different international settings. The authors searched multiple major databases spanning over a decade and synthesized findings from studies meeting their inclusion criteria. They found substantial conceptual and methodological heterogeneity in how coverage was defined, ranging from proportions of tested populations to measures of equipment availability and geographic accessibility, with no standardization across countries. Reported coverage values varied enormously, from negligible levels to roughly four-fifths of target populations, with socioeconomic, structural, and financial barriers consistently impeding access. The authors conclude that meaningful global monitoring of rapid molecular test coverage will require governmental commitment, decentralization of testing services, and adoption of uniform measurement criteria aligned with World Health Organization guidance.

Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Urine-Based Molecular Testing for Urogenital Tuberculosis.

Fang L, Chen X, Chen J et al.. Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Urine-Based Molecular Testing for Urogenital Tuberculosis.. Archivos espanoles de urologia. 2026

This systematic review and meta-analysis evaluated the diagnostic performance of urine-based molecular testing methods — including Xpert Mycobacterium tuberculosis/rifampicin, multiplex polymerase chain reaction, and loop-mediated isothermal amplification — for detecting urogenital tuberculosis, a condition that is difficult to confirm rapidly using conventional culture. The authors pooled data from diagnostic accuracy studies involving patients with high clinical suspicion of urogenital tuberculosis, assessing sensitivity, specificity, likelihood ratios, and diagnostic odds ratios using a bivariate random-effects model. Urine-based molecular testing demonstrated high specificity and a strong area under the summary receiver operating characteristic curve, indicating good overall discriminative ability, though sensitivity was more modest. A positive test result substantially increased post-test probability of disease, while a negative result meaningfully reduced it, supporting the clinical utility of these assays. The authors conclude that urine-based molecular testing is a valuable adjunctive tool for rapid confirmation of urogenital tuberculosis, best interpreted in conjunction with clinical, imaging, and culture findings.

Emerging blood-based diagnostic strategies for tuberculosis.

Yue C, Geng X, Sun X et al.. Emerging blood-based diagnostic strategies for tuberculosis.. Clinical chemistry and laboratory medicine. 2026

This review examines blood-based diagnostic approaches for tuberculosis, a disease caused by Mycobacterium tuberculosis that continues to pose a major global public health burden. The authors focus on serological methods, which have gained increasing attention due to their simplicity, rapid turnaround time, and cost-effectiveness compared to conventional diagnostics such as sputum culture and rapid molecular assays. The review summarizes current biomarkers employed in TB serodiagnosis, discusses how advanced diagnostic technologies can be integrated with these approaches, and highlights their particular utility for diagnosing extrapulmonary TB and for patients who test negative using standard methods. The authors argue that these emerging blood-based strategies hold significant potential to improve TB diagnosis across a range of clinical settings.

Diagnostic accuracy, operational feasibility, and cost considerations of Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra for pulmonary tuberculosis.

Sidiq Z, Anand A, Dwivedi KK et al.. Diagnostic accuracy, operational feasibility, and cost considerations of Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra for pulmonary tuberculosis.. PloS one. 2026

This study compared two WHO-endorsed nucleic acid amplification tests — Truenat MTB Plus and Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra — for detecting pulmonary tuberculosis and rifampicin resistance among presumptive TB patients in India. Using mycobacterial culture and phenotypic drug susceptibility testing as reference standards, the researchers prospectively tested paired sputum specimens from several hundred patients at a tuberculosis centre in New Delhi. Xpert MTB/RIF Ultra demonstrated higher sensitivity and specificity for both TB detection and rifampicin resistance identification, and produced substantially lower rates of invalid and indeterminate results compared to Truenat MTB Plus. Truenat, however, was considerably less expensive per test and offers practical advantages for decentralized settings, though its higher rates of invalid and indeterminate results increase the operational burden through the need for repeat testing. The authors conclude that a tiered diagnostic approach utilizing both platforms according to their respective strengths could help expand molecular testing access and advance tuberculosis elimination efforts.

Osteoarthritis: molecular pathogenesis and potential therapeutic options

Yi Zhang, Yanqi Han, Ying Sun et al.. Osteoarthritis: molecular pathogenesis and potential therapeutic options. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review paper examines the molecular mechanisms underlying osteoarthritis, a chronic joint disorder characterized by pain, inflammation, and bone deterioration, with a focus on identifying new therapeutic targets. The authors comprehensively analyzed nociceptive pathways connecting the knee joint to the central nervous system, pathological changes in bone and cartilage, and the roles of inflammatory cytokines alongside organelle-specific dysfunctions including mitochondrial impairment, lysosomal destabilization, and unresolved endoplasmic reticulum stress. Based on these mechanistic insights, the review delineates emerging pharmacological strategies aimed at simultaneously addressing inflammatory cascades, restoring organelle homeostasis through approaches such as mitophagy enhancement and lysosomal integrity preservation, and reducing pain signaling. The authors also highlight advanced organelle-targeted drug delivery systems intended to improve therapeutic efficacy and stability. Together, these findings are proposed to establish a multimodal therapeutic framework capable of addressing both the structural and clinical manifestations of osteoarthritis.

Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years

Richard W. Lee, Arjun Nair, Haval Balata et al.. Implementation of the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme over 5 years. Nature Medicine. 2026

The paper presents a progress report on the NHS England Lung Cancer Screening Programme, a national initiative that offers low-dose computed tomography to ever-smokers aged 55–74 who meet risk thresholds determined by multivariable models. The programme operates through regionally federated clinical infrastructure guided by national strategic, clinical, and economic frameworks, and was launched in 2019 with the goal of achieving full national coverage by 2030. Over five years, more than two million people were invited and thousands of lung cancers were diagnosed, with nearly two-thirds detected at the earliest tumour stage, representing a substantial increase in early-stage diagnoses particularly in socioeconomically deprived regions. The authors conclude that the programme demonstrates feasibility and scalability in reaching high-risk and underserved populations, though inequalities in participation remain a challenge to be addressed. These findings are presented as evidence supporting broader adoption of lung cancer screening across the UK and internationally, along with practical frameworks for adaptation elsewhere.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, focusing on how integrating nanoparticles with light-activated treatment modalities enhances therapeutic precision across a range of diseases. The authors survey the core components, classification schemes, and delivery platforms—including gold nanoparticles, liposomes, and dendrimers—that improve light absorption, tumor targeting, reactive oxygen species generation in photodynamic therapy, and localized heating in photothermal therapy. At the molecular level, the review details how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic cascades, and modulate immune responses through altered cytokine profiles and T-cell activity, with relevance not only to cancer but also to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors critically assess regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, identifying key obstacles to broader clinical translation such as nanoparticle optimization, efficient light delivery, and regulatory barriers. Collectively, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative, noninvasive therapeutic strategy with broad applicability across chronic and oncological diseases.

EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update

Gulen Hatemi, Sofia Ramiro, Yeşim Özgüler et al.. EULAR recommendations for the management of Behçet’s syndrome: 2025 update. Annals of the Rheumatic Diseases. 2026

This paper presents the updated clinical practice recommendations from a major European rheumatology alliance for managing Behçet's syndrome, a complex multisystem inflammatory disease. A large, multinational task force representing multiple medical specialties, along with patient partners, conducted a systematic literature review and used a Delphi survey process to formulate and vote on updated guidance. The resulting framework comprises five overarching principles and twelve recommendations organized by organ involvement, including one entirely new recommendation and several modified ones. Key therapeutic guidance emphasizes colchicine as first-line treatment for mucocutaneous and joint manifestations, with apremilast or TNFα inhibitors reserved for refractory cases, and more aggressive immunosuppressive regimens for organ-threatening disease. The overarching principles stress the relapsing-remitting nature of the condition, the need for individualized treatment based on disease activity and prognostic factors, and the importance of multidisciplinary care, patient education, and shared decision-making.

A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression

Kasper Favere, Matthias Bosman, Peter Delputte et al.. A systematic literature review on the effects of exercise on human Toll-like receptor expression. PubMed. 2026

This systematic review examined how different forms of exercise affect the expression of Toll-like receptors (TLRs), a family of immune pattern recognition receptors implicated in inflammatory signalling and linked to chronic low-grade inflammation associated with sedentary behaviour and non-communicable diseases. The authors searched multiple databases for studies in which exercise served as an isolated intervention in human subjects, ultimately including several dozen articles spanning acute and chronic resistance, aerobic, and combined exercise modalities. Acute resistance exercise tended to upregulate TLR expression, whereas acute aerobic exercise more often produced neutral or downregulatory effects, particularly in healthy individuals. Chronic resistance and combined training programs predominantly left TLR levels unchanged or reduced them, while chronic aerobic training yielded mixed results with most measurements showing no significant change. The authors conclude that exercise does interact with TLR signalling in a modality- and duration-dependent manner, but substantial heterogeneity across study designs limits firm conclusions and underscores the need for more standardised research to clarify the underlying mechanisms and clinical implications.

A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion

Marcus DuPont. A Similarity Theorem and Its Breakdown in Atomic Black Hole Accretion. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates the mathematical symmetry properties of accreting atomic gas onto a central point mass, such as a black hole, and determines where that symmetry breaks down during rapid growth. The authors prove that a specific rescaling of black hole mass and ambient gas density constitutes an exact similarity transformation that preserves all dimensionless physical quantities—including optical depths, Eddington ratios, and variability—across a broad class of physically motivated radiation and feedback laws, and demonstrate this rescaling is unique within that class. They introduce a dimensionless parameter characterizing the fractional mass gained per Bondi time, showing it equals the expansion speed of the Bondi radius in units of the ambient sound speed, so that a value of unity corresponds to sonic dilation of the accretion radius. For power-law mass retention rates, this sonic threshold is reached after a finite mass increase, and the available remaining time within the similarity regime is strictly bounded; canonical hyper-Eddington accretion scenarios are found to have already crossed this boundary. The authors conclude that the similarity theorem unifies previously separate radiating Bondi and feedback-regulated scaling relations while identifying the precise conditions under which a fixed-mass quasi-steady approximation loses validity.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes and validates a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The method optimizes a blocking transformation under a "perfect blocking" condition so that the coarse-grained lattice distribution is well described by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom lost in decimation. A brief rethermalization step corrects residual mismatches, and because the coarse ensemble provides the long-distance modes, the same learned transformation can be applied recursively to generate configurations on successively larger lattices from a single small-volume ensemble. The method is tested in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, demonstrating stable cascade upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume using only local computational resources. Rethermalization tests confirm that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while mismatches in the relevant thermal direction relax slowly, and the authors argue the approach naturally extends to higher-dimensional, gauge, and fermionic systems.

Exact quantification of nonlocal magic

Piotr Sierant. Exact quantification of nonlocal magic. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the question of how much quantum "magic" (nonstabilizerness, the resource enabling universal quantum computation beyond Clifford circuits) is genuinely nonlocal—residing in correlations between parties rather than in their individual local descriptions. The authors solve the previously open optimization problem of minimizing over local bases for the stabilizer fidelity, proving that the nonlocal magic of any pure multiqubit state is determined by the distance of its entanglement spectrum from the closest spectrum of Bell pairs. They also establish an unexpected connection to entanglement embezzlement: a family of states can universally embezzle entanglement under local operations and classical communication if and only if its nonlocal magic diverges. Crucially, the relevant property is not the total amount of entanglement but how the entanglement spectrum distributes weight across factor-of-two windows of rank, which explains why critical chains and random-singlet states—despite sharing the same logarithmic entanglement scaling—carry unbounded and vanishing nonlocal magic, respectively. The results make nonlocal magic an operationally meaningful characteristic of quantum correlations that is accessible through tensor-network methods and entanglement spectroscopy experiments.

QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions

Maria Vlachou, Anna Murphy Høgenhaug, Mohammad N. S. Jahromi et al.. QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper investigates how to automatically identify and extract information related to credibility assessments within asylum application appeal decisions, which are lengthy and complex legal documents. The researchers developed QUEST, a system that combines synthetic query generation, topic extraction, and relevance assessment to frame this identification problem as an information retrieval task, applied to two datasets of Danish asylum appeals. Beyond standard retrieval evaluation metrics, they introduced a novel domain-specific evaluation framework based on credibility-relevant factors rather than traditional notions of document relevance. Their findings reveal that performance estimation becomes substantially more challenging when using credibility-based relevance assessments compared to conventional metrics, highlighting the inherent difficulty of this specialized legal information retrieval task.

Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach

Adil Alshammari, Hayretdin Bahsi. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of verifying accountability in cross-organization agent workflows when live systems are unavailable, untrusted, or controlled by a single party. The authors propose a preserved evidence-bundle model in which each workflow event carries policy-required cryptographic evidence, including sender authentication, log commitments, witness-backed checkpoints, delegation authorization, and receiver-signed receipts. A policy-controlled offline verifier then assesses whether a given bundle satisfies the chosen evidence policy without inferring delivery or receipt from transport behavior alone. Evaluation using a prototype over hundreds of complete workflows and preserved bundles showed that all corrupted or policy-insufficient bundles were correctly rejected with no false acceptances, while latency measurements revealed that checkpoint-context anchoring was the costliest verification step. The work claims to provide an auditable, dispute-ready foundation for cross-organization agent messaging that does not depend on live infrastructure or platform-specific logging.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the computational difficulty of a hypothesis testing problem: distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph of the same degree. To analyze this problem, the authors develop the first treatment of low-degree polynomial threshold functions in this setting, a standard framework for understanding the limitations of efficient algorithms. A key technical contribution is a new characterization of the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic lengths, which extends prior results on cycle statistics in random regular graphs and random lifts to this more general noisy setting. The findings collectively shed light on the computational complexity of the detection problem by establishing regimes where low-degree methods provably fail to distinguish the two distributions.

sexually transmitted infection point-of-care testing

Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).

Abou Shahla W, Rossoni I, Padovese V. Community peer-led STI/HIV point-of-care testing and knowledge, attitude and practice (KAP) survey in men who have sex with men (MSM) attending an NGO-led clinic in the outskirts of Beirut (Lebanon).. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This pilot study examined the utility of point-of-care testing for HIV, syphilis, and hepatitis among men who have sex with men attending an NGO-led clinic in Beirut, Lebanon, while also evaluating their sexual health knowledge, attitudes, and practices. Using a cross-sectional design over several months, researchers conducted sexual health consultations alongside an anonymous questionnaire covering demographics, STI knowledge, condom use, chemsex, sexual violence, and perceived HIV-related stigma. The cohort was predominantly young, educated Lebanese men, with a notable proportion living with HIV, and findings revealed that high levels of STI and HIV awareness coexisted with condomless sex and recreational drug use. Perceived community stigma emerged as a significant barrier to testing-seeking behavior. The study concludes that integrating point-of-care testing into sexual health services enables same-day diagnosis, counselling, and linkage to care, representing a valuable approach for reaching MSM populations in contexts of high stigma.

Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.

Martin K, Bath D, Nyamwanza R et al.. Point-of-care screening for curable sexually transmitted infections in antenatal care in Zimbabwe: a costing study.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study investigated the cost of integrating point-of-care screening for three curable sexually transmitted infections into antenatal care in Harare, Zimbabwe, compared to the standard syndromic management approach. Researchers implemented screening at primary healthcare clinics and collected detailed economic cost data using staff interviews, time-and-motion studies, and other sources, then modelled implementation in a low-volume routine care setting over a twelve-month period. The prevalence of at least one of the three infections was substantial among pregnant women screened. The largest cost drivers were test kits and personnel, which together accounted for the majority of total intervention costs. The findings highlight that while point-of-care antenatal STI screening is feasible, its incremental cost over syndromic management is considerable, underscoring the need for less expensive point-of-care diagnostic tools to make such programmes more affordable for health systems.

Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.

Watts CG, Causer LM, Matthews SJ et al.. Cost-effectiveness of a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections in remote primary care health services in Australia.. Sexually transmitted infections. 2026

This study examined whether a decentralised molecular point-of-care testing programme for sexually transmitted infections is cost-effective compared with standard laboratory-based testing in remote Australian First Nations communities, where the burden of chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and trichomonas is disproportionately high. Using a decision analytic model with a government payer perspective and a ten-year time horizon, the researchers modelled costs and health outcomes for young women and men seeking care at remote primary health services. The primary measure was cost per quality-adjusted life year gained, with sensitivity analyses used to assess uncertainty. The point-of-care programme was found to be cost-effective relative to the accepted willingness-to-pay threshold, and was predicted to substantially reduce rates of pelvic inflammatory disease and preterm or low birthweight births among those with infections. The findings support sustained investment in scaled point-of-care testing in remote primary care settings as a means of improving reproductive health outcomes for First Nations communities.

Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.

Latt PM, Kong FYS, Khosropour CM et al.. Mechanisms, management and prevention of anorectal sexually transmitted infections.. Nature reviews. Gastroenterology & hepatology. 2026

This review examines the epidemiology, pathogenic mechanisms, diagnosis, and prevention of anorectal bacterial sexually transmitted infections, focusing primarily on chlamydia, gonorrhoea, and syphilis. The authors synthesize current understanding of why these infections are frequently asymptomatic, attributing this to bacterial adaptation to rectal tissue and attenuated local immune responses that do not reach symptom thresholds, a phenomenon particularly pronounced among men who have sex with men where anorectal infections often exceed urogenital prevalence. Molecular diagnostics have substantially improved detection, yet challenges remain in distinguishing viable from non-viable organisms, expanding point-of-care testing, and balancing screening with antimicrobial stewardship, especially as gonorrhoea continues to develop antimicrobial resistance. The review highlights doxycycline post-exposure prophylaxis and artificial intelligence applications as promising emerging prevention strategies, while noting that real-world implementation across diverse clinical settings remains limited. The authors identify key knowledge gaps and propose research priorities to strengthen control of these infections globally.

Cost-Effectiveness of Testing and Screening Strategies for Trichomoniasis among Asymptomatic and Symptomatic Women in U.S. Outpatient Clinical Settings.

Rong R, Stoecker C, Kissinger PJ et al.. Cost-Effectiveness of Testing and Screening Strategies for Trichomoniasis among Asymptomatic and Symptomatic Women in U.S. Outpatient Clinical Settings.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This study evaluated the cost-effectiveness of various testing and screening strategies for trichomoniasis among both symptomatic and asymptomatic women in U.S. primary care settings. Using a decision tree model applied to a hypothetical cohort of women aged 15–59, researchers examined combinations of wet mount microscopy, rapid antigen testing, and nucleic acid amplification testing, estimating outcomes including quality-adjusted life years and societal economic burden across sequelae such as preterm birth, HIV infection, and cervical cancer. The analysis found that adding nucleic acid amplification testing to wet mount microscopy for symptomatic women improved health outcomes and fell within a commonly accepted cost-effectiveness threshold, whereas adding it to rapid antigen testing for symptomatic women was not cost-effective at that same threshold. Universal nucleic acid amplification testing screening of asymptomatic women was substantially more costly per quality-adjusted life year gained and was not considered cost-effective under standard willingness-to-pay assumptions. The findings suggest that while enhanced testing for symptomatic women can be economically justified, broader screening of asymptomatic populations would require considerably higher willingness-to-pay thresholds to be warranted.

Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions

Campbell Ince, Stephanie Antons, Sarah Ashton et al.. Compulsive sexual behavior disorder (CSBD) and problematic pornography use (PPU): A comprehensive, interdisciplinary, and expert-informed narrative review with suggested future directions. Journal of Behavioral Addictions. 2026

This narrative review examined the current state of scientific and clinical knowledge on Compulsive Sexual Behavior Disorder and Problematic Pornography Use, two widely debated constructs concerning dysregulated sexual behavior. Drawing on contributions from experts across psychology, psychiatry, sexology, addiction science, and neuroscience, the authors synthesized empirical and theoretical literature spanning eleven key thematic areas, including historical context, conceptualization, assessment, epidemiology, co-occurring disorders, etiology, consequences, and treatment. The review also addressed underrepresented groups such as adolescents, women, and LGBTQ+ individuals, as well as the role of cultural factors in shaping these phenomena. The authors identify persistent methodological challenges and classificatory controversies that continue to complicate the field, and they argue that progress depends on greater interdisciplinary collaboration and more rigorous research designs. The work is intended to advance scientific discourse, guide clinical practice, and inform public health policy related to these conditions.

An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening

Kristina Ramstedt. An epidemiological approach to sexually transmitted diseases – with special reference to contact tracing and screening. Acta Dermato Venereologica. 2026

This thesis examined epidemiological approaches to controlling sexually transmitted diseases, with particular focus on contact tracing and screening as tools for identifying asymptomatic infected individuals. The research evaluated contact tracing routines, characterized chlamydia-infected populations and their partners, and investigated whether contact tracing data could inform mathematical models of partner choice and disease spread. Key findings included that a gonorrhoea microepidemic was successfully contained through coordinated contact tracing, and that integrating contact tracing into HIV care identified large numbers of previously unknown cases. Chlamydia screening in family planning clinics revealed meaningful prevalence among asymptomatic patients, with infection risk associated with younger age, shorter relationship duration, lack of condom use, and no prior genital infection history, while uptake of testing was lowest in low socioeconomic areas. The study also found that although patients identified through screening differed somewhat from those attending dedicated clinics, the differences were insufficient to warrant distinct partner management strategies, and that partner selection data did not support a purely random mixing model for disease transmission.

Multifactorial determinants of health status: insights from the MEDIET4ALL large-scale survey on eco-sociodemographic, psychological, and lifestyle (diet, physical activity, and sleep) factors

Achraf Ammar, Atef Salem, Mohamed Ali Boujelbane et al.. Multifactorial determinants of health status: insights from the MEDIET4ALL large-scale survey on eco-sociodemographic, psychological, and lifestyle (diet, physical activity, and sleep) factors. Frontiers in Public Health. 2026

This large-scale survey study investigated the multifactorial determinants of individual health status, with particular focus on how geo-demographic, socioeconomic, psychological, and lifestyle factors collectively shape outcomes related to non-communicable diseases. Researchers administered the MEDIET4ALL e-survey across ten Mediterranean and neighboring countries, recruiting thousands of participants and categorizing their self-reported health as healthy, at-risk, or having known disease, before applying multinomial logistic regression and other statistical models. Body mass index emerged as the strongest negative predictor of health status, with both obesity and underweight substantially elevating the odds of being at risk or having disease, while psychological distress, insomnia, alcohol consumption, and prolonged sitting also worsened outcomes. Conversely, life satisfaction was the strongest protective factor, followed by higher education, better sleep quality, and adherence to the Mediterranean diet and lifestyle, and residing in Mediterranean regions, being female, employed, or in a rural environment was also associated with more favorable health outcomes. The findings underscore the complex interplay among mental health, lifestyle behaviors, and socioeconomic context, and call for multi-level public health interventions addressing physical activity, healthy eating, psychological well-being, and equitable healthcare access.

A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks

Kathleen Kristensen, Leslie D. Williams, Charlie Kaplan et al.. A Novel Index Measure of Housing-Related Risk as a Predictor of Overdose Among Young People Who Inject Drugs and Injection Networks. Journal of Urban Health. 2026

This study examined the relationship between housing instability and overdose risk among young people who inject drugs, addressing limitations of prior research that typically reduced housing instability to a single dimension. Using baseline data from a network-based longitudinal study of young people who inject drugs and their social networks in metropolitan Chicago, the researchers constructed a multi-dimensional housing instability risk index composed of five dichotomous variables and analyzed its association with lifetime overdose count via negative binomial regression. After adjusting for multiple covariates, a significant positive association was found between the housing instability risk score and lifetime overdose count. The findings support the value of a holistic, multi-dimensional approach to measuring housing instability, suggesting that such an index better captures overdose risk than single-dimension measures and should inform the design of interventions targeting this vulnerable population.

Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads

Karan Malhotra, Louise L. Hansen, Samuel Han et al.. Ultrasensitive Enzyme-Free Detection of Proteins on Magnetic Beads. Journal of the American Chemical Society. 2026

This paper studied the development of an enzyme-free platform for ultrasensitive protein detection on magnetic beads, aimed at making advanced diagnostic assays more accessible in resource-limited settings. The researchers created a system called efMOSAIC, which uses hybridization chain reaction-based signal amplifiers instead of enzymes to achieve digital protein quantification with simple workflows. The platform achieved detection limits comparable to established single-molecule detection technologies such as Simoa, while the reagents remained stable when stored at room temperature for up to six months. The team validated efMOSAIC for detecting cytokines from human plasma and demonstrated its capability for multiplexed biomarker detection. By eliminating the need for enzymes, specialized instrumentation, and cold-chain supply logistics, the authors argue this approach could expand access to ultrasensitive protein diagnostics in low- and middle-income countries and point-of-care settings.

Malaria in the 21st Century: Global Disease Burden, Epidemiological Insights, and Strategic Control Approaches

Basmah F. Alharbi, Mawahib A. Ahmed. Malaria in the 21st Century: Global Disease Burden, Epidemiological Insights, and Strategic Control Approaches. Biology. 2026

This review paper examines the current global burden of malaria, synthesizing epidemiological data and evidence on the disease's distribution, determinants, and control strategies in the twenty-first century. The authors drew primarily on the World Health Organization's World Malaria Report and Global Burden of Disease estimates to characterize regional trends and identify key drivers of ongoing transmission, including socio-economic factors and emerging challenges such as drug resistance, climate change, and funding constraints. The paper covers the full spectrum of malaria transmission routes — including vector-borne, transfusion-related, transplant-related, and congenital pathways — and evaluates intervention approaches across primary, secondary, and tertiary prevention. The authors argue that effective disease control requires multifactorial, locally adapted strategies and that strengthening health education with contextually relevant knowledge is essential to improving outcomes. The review concludes by outlining policy priorities necessary to advance toward the WHO's goal of malaria elimination by the year two thousand and thirty.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes and validates a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The approach optimizes the blocking using a "perfect blocking" condition so that the coarser lattice distribution is well described by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom that decimation removes. A brief rethermalization step corrects residual mismatches, and because the coarse ensemble provides the long-distance modes, the same trained flow can be applied recursively in a cascade to generate configurations on progressively larger lattices from a small initial ensemble. QUEST: A Query and Extraction System for Topics in Asylum Law Application Decisions

The paper addresses the challenge of automatically identifying credibility-related factors in legal decisions on asylum applications, which are lengthy and complex documents combining applicant interviews, original rulings, and supporting materials. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach

Adil Alshammari, Hayretdin Bahsi. Offline-Verifiable Accountability for Cross-Organization Agent Messaging: A Preserved Evidence-Bundle Approach. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper addresses the challenge of verifying accountability in cross-organization agent workflows when live systems are unavailable, controlled by a single party, or not trusted by all participants. The authors propose a preserved evidence-bundle model in which each workflow event carries a self-contained collection of policy-required evidence—including sender authentication, log commitments, witness-backed checkpoints, delegation authorization, and receiver-signed receipts—alongside a policy-controlled offline verifier that accepts only claims supported by that evidence. The approach is distinguished by its verifier-centered design, which refuses to infer delivery or receipt from transport behavior or log inclusion alone, instead requiring explicit evidence for each claim under a chosen policy profile. A prototype was evaluated over hundreds of complete workflows and preserved bundles, measuring verifier latency across policy configurations and demonstrating that all corrupted or policy-insufficient bundles were correctly rejected with no false acceptances observed. The results support evidence-based audit and dispute resolution without dependence on live infrastructure or platform-specific logging systems.

Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift

Xifan Yu. Analysis of Polynomial Threshold Functions on Random Regular Graphs: Computational Complexity of Detecting Noisy Random Lift. arXiv. 2026 preprint

The paper studies the computational complexity of a hypothesis testing problem: distinguishing a noisy random lift of a base regular graph from a uniformly random regular graph. To analyze this problem, the authors examine the power of low-degree polynomial threshold functions, a standard framework for understanding algorithmic hardness, applied in the context of random regular graphs for the first time in this setting. A key technical contribution is a new characterization of the distribution of short cycle counts in noisy random lifts up to logarithmic lengths, which generalizes previously known results for both random regular graphs and random lifts. The findings collectively suggest fundamental limitations on low-degree polynomial methods for solving this detection problem, contributing to the broader understanding of when graph inference tasks are computationally tractable.

Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective

Liheng Tan, Zhengkai Tu, Prasanna Karhade. Understanding Venture Capital Syndication in Information Technology Sectors: A Network Formation Perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how venture capital co-investment relationships form and evolve within U.S. information technology sectors, focusing on the structural mechanisms that drive syndication network development. Using comprehensive dyadic panel data spanning several decades across hardware, software, and hybrid subsectors, the researchers tested multiple relational mechanisms—including prior collaboration, shared partners, geographic proximity, and organizational similarity—using dyadic logit models and exponential random graph modeling. Prior collaboration emerged as the most consistent predictor of new co-investment links across all subsectors, with shared partners, geographic proximity, and organizational-type similarity also positively associated with link formation, while the influence of domain overlap, prominence, and experience varied by subsector. A whole-network model of the software sector confirmed that triadic closure and geographic homophily reinforce network structure, alongside a weaker effect of investor-type homophily. Together, the findings demonstrate that IT venture syndication networks are jointly shaped by relational persistence, network closure, and homophily rather than any single mechanism.

Distinct Modes of Pulsar Glitch Activity Revealed by the Waiting-Time--Amplitude Morphology

Wen-Tong Liu, Wei-hua Wang, Xia Zhou et al.. Distinct Modes of Pulsar Glitch Activity Revealed by the Waiting-Time--Amplitude Morphology. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated the diversity of pulsar glitch behavior by developing an objective, quantitative classification scheme to replace the traditional qualitative "Vela-like" and "Crab-like" labels. The authors compiled a sample of glitches from dozens of pulsars by cross-matching two major glitch databases, then characterized each pulsar's glitch activity using the joint distribution of backward waiting time and fractional glitch amplitude, analyzed through two-dimensional kernel density estimation and highest-density region descriptors. Hierarchical clustering of these geometric descriptors revealed two distinct morphological classes—a Compact class characterized by small, anisotropic distributions consistent with near-complete superfluid reservoir depletion, and an Extended class with broader, more diffuse distributions suggestive of partial, avalanche-like angular momentum releases—with most sources clustering robustly into one group, though two prolific pulsars occupied intermediate positions. Logistic regression identified mean backward waiting time as the strongest predictor distinguishing the two classes, and a parallel analysis using forward waiting times recovered the same two-class structure with somewhat less stability. The authors conclude that this morphological distinction has physical implications, particularly that short-term glitch activity measurements in Extended-class pulsars likely underestimate the true fraction of crustal superfluid involved in glitching.

CRISPR based diagnostics infectious disease detection
