CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.

Liao J, Su Y, Jiang F. CRISPR-Cas12a assay for rapid and specific detection of &lt;i&gt;Shigella flexneri 2a&lt;/i&gt; in clinical samples.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

The paper studied the development and validation of a rapid diagnostic assay for *Shigella flexneri 2a*, the most prevalent serotype causing shigellosis and a significant public health burden in developing countries. The researchers designed a CRISPR-Cas12a-based detection system using two guide RNAs targeting serotype-specific genes, coupled with recombinase polymerase amplification to enable isothermal signal amplification readable by either fluorescence or lateral flow. The assay was analytically and clinically validated against conventional PCR using both purified DNA and a large cohort of clinical stool specimens. The assay achieved a detection limit of ten copies per microliter with complete analytical specificity and no cross-reactivity, while clinical testing demonstrated diagnostic sensitivity and specificity comparable to PCR. The authors conclude that the assay's speed, simplicity, and dual readout options make it a practical tool for point-of-care diagnostics and field-based surveillance in resource-limited settings.

An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.

Li L, Wei H, Wei M et al.. An integrated centrifugal microfluidic CRISPR-based diagnostics platform for multiplexed point-of-care testing of respiratory pathogens.. Biosensors & bioelectronics. 2026

The paper developed and validated an integrated microfluidic diagnostic platform called iCARD for rapid, multiplexed detection of respiratory pathogens at the point of care. The system combines centrifugal microfluidics with CRISPR/Cas13a chemistry on a rotating disc pre-loaded with lyophilized reagents, paired with a portable fluorescence analyzer, and uses a novel resin-based method to purify and concentrate nucleic acids directly from throat swabs. The platform enables simultaneous screening of multiple pathogens across several independent samples in a single automated workflow within approximately forty minutes. Analytical testing confirmed single-copy-level sensitivity with no cross-reactivity, and clinical validation against retrospective patient samples demonstrated very high diagnostic sensitivity and perfect specificity. The authors conclude that iCARD represents a robust, accurate, and accessible tool suitable for decentralized molecular diagnostics and epidemiological surveillance.

CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.

Sun T, Yuan A, Xie W et al.. CRISPR RNA Engineering Enables Single Nucleotide Polymorphism Discrimination in Nucleic Acid Detection.. Angewandte Chemie (International ed. in English). 2026

This review examines how CRISPR-based diagnostic platforms can be engineered to achieve single nucleotide polymorphism discrimination in nucleic acid detection, addressing the persistent problem of off-target activation that limits clinical reliability. The authors synthesize advances in CRISPR RNA engineering strategies, detailing how structural features such as spacer length, intentional mismatch design, secondary-structure modulation, chemical modification, and strand-displacement gating collectively govern target recognition and cleavage specificity. Computational prediction, high-throughput screening, and hybrid guide architectures are systematically evaluated for their ability to enhance single-nucleotide discrimination, stabilize reaction performance, and enable multiplexed detection for pathogen profiling and mutation identification. The review identifies remaining obstacles including interference from complex clinical matrices, absence of unified evaluation standards, and scalability barriers that impede translation to clinical settings. The authors conclude that integrated crRNA design combined with system-level optimization and standardized benchmarking will be necessary to fulfill the potential of CRISPR diagnostics for ultrasensitive point-of-care testing and precision medicine applications.

SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.

Hetta HF, Ahmed R, Haseeb A et al.. SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches.. Diagnostics (Basel, Switzerland). 2026

This review examined the landscape of point-of-care testing technologies developed for SARS-CoV-2 diagnosis, motivated by the urgent diagnostic demands exposed by the COVID-19 pandemic. The authors conducted a structured literature search across major scientific databases and evaluated multiple testing modalities, including molecular assays such as rapid RT-PCR, LAMP, and CRISPR-based methods, alongside antigen and antibody tests, biosensor platforms, and artificial intelligence-assisted diagnostic approaches. Molecular methods were found to offer high sensitivity and specificity suitable for decentralized use, while antigen tests provide rapid and affordable screening albeit with performance that varies by viral load, symptom timing, and circulating variants. Antibody-based testing proved most useful for retrospective diagnosis and seroprevalence monitoring rather than acute infection detection, and emerging biosensor and AI technologies showed analytical promise but largely remain at prototype or early-validation stages requiring further clinical evidence. The review concludes that no single modality is universally optimal, and that combining complementary approaches alongside advances in multiplex testing, digital connectivity, and variant-resilient detection will strengthen both COVID-19 management and future infectious disease preparedness.

CRISPR-Cas and Infectious Diseases: A Decade of Translational Advances in Molecular Biotechnology.

Mayuri K, Saravanan KM, Somala CS et al.. CRISPR-Cas and Infectious Diseases: A Decade of Translational Advances in Molecular Biotechnology.. Biochemical genetics. 2026

This review examines a decade of translational progress in applying CRISPR-Cas systems to infectious disease diagnostics, therapeutics, and vaccine development. The authors surveyed key platforms and research directions, including rapid pathogen detection tools, CRISPR-based strategies for targeting viral genomes and antimicrobial resistance, and the engineering of live-attenuated and personalized neoantigen vaccines. Their synthesis reveals an uneven maturity across these domains: diagnostic platforms have advanced furthest toward clinical evaluation, live-attenuated vaccine engineering has reached preclinical stages, while therapeutic genome editing and personalized neoantigen vaccines remain largely exploratory. Delivery inefficiency, off-target effects, and regulatory and ethical uncertainties are identified as the principal barriers slowing clinical translation across all application areas. The review concludes that realizing the transformative potential of CRISPR-Cas in infectious disease management will require coordinated technical innovation alongside clear ethical and regulatory frameworks to ensure safe and equitable deployment.

PCR and molecular diagnostics: Biotechnologic integration in clinical laboratories.

Saleem MA, Riaz T, Saleem F et al.. PCR and molecular diagnostics: Biotechnologic integration in clinical laboratories.. Clinica chimica acta; international journal of clinical chemistry. 2026

This review paper examines the evolution and clinical integration of polymerase chain reaction technology and related molecular diagnostic tools in laboratory medicine. The authors survey the development of PCR from conventional amplification methods to advanced platforms including quantitative, digital, reverse transcription, and multiplex variants, assessing how each contributes to nucleic acid detection, quantification, and high-throughput analysis. The paper further explores how PCR can be combined with next-generation sequencing, CRISPR-based diagnostics, microarrays, and epigenetic profiling to broaden the characterization of disease states, with particular attention to oncologically relevant biomarkers such as circulating tumor DNA and key mutation targets used in precision oncology. Applications extending to infectious disease diagnosis, antimicrobial resistance surveillance, heritable disorder detection, and minimal residual disease monitoring are also covered, alongside emerging trends in multi-omics data integration and artificial intelligence-assisted analysis. The authors conclude that while challenges related to cost, standardization, technical expertise, and data management persist, continued technological advances are progressively expanding clinician and patient access to these powerful diagnostic capabilities.

Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms

Purshottam J. Assudani, Balakrishnan . P., Anny Leema A et al.. Biosensing technologies for foodborne pathogen detection and healthcare: principles, emerging materials, and intelligent platforms. Microchimica Acta. 2026

This review examines the current state and future directions of biosensing technologies designed to detect high-priority foodborne pathogens, including Escherichia coli, Salmonella, and Listeria monocytogenes, which continue to threaten global public health. The authors survey electrochemical, optical, and microfluidic sensing platforms, analyzing how advances in functional nanomaterials, aptamers, nanozymes, and surface engineering strategies have improved sensor sensitivity, specificity, and performance in complex food matrices. A central theme is the integration of artificial intelligence and machine learning into these biosensing systems, enabling intelligent pattern recognition, real-time analytics, and high-throughput multiplexed detection that transform conventional sensors into smart diagnostic devices. The review critically identifies persistent challenges such as matrix interference, bioreceptor instability, manufacturing scalability, and regulatory standardization, while proposing mitigation strategies including ratiometric sensing, microfluidic pre-treatment, explainable AI, and printable electronics. Looking forward, the authors advocate for self-powered biosensors, modular pathogen detection panels, blockchain-enabled data integrity, and standardized validation pipelines as pathways toward regulation-ready platforms for next-generation food safety monitoring.

New approach methodologies for drug discovery

Wenqiang Liu, Paul Pang, Catherine A. Wu et al.. New approach methodologies for drug discovery. Cell. 2026

This review examines the emerging field of new approach methodologies as alternatives to traditional animal-based drug discovery, which has historically suffered from high failure rates. The authors survey recent advances in stem cell-based systems, organoid models, and computational approaches, considering how these tools apply across the full drug-discovery continuum from disease modeling through efficacy testing. The review also discusses the evolving regulatory frameworks from major health agencies that are shaping the adoption of these methods. Key biological, technical, and regulatory bottlenecks limiting broader translational uptake are identified and analyzed. The authors conclude by outlining future human-centric discovery pipelines and the societal considerations necessary for the responsible integration of these methodologies into mainstream drug development.

Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications

Deepak S. Chauhan, Rajendra Prasad, Mukesh Dhanka et al.. Nanoparticles-based phototherapy systems: molecular mechanisms and clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines nanoparticle-based phototherapy systems, focusing on how integrating nanoparticles with light-activated treatment modalities enhances therapeutic precision across a range of diseases. The authors survey the core components, classification schemes, and delivery platforms—including gold nanoparticles, liposomes, and dendrimers—that underpin photodynamic and photothermal therapies, highlighting their roles in improving light absorption, targeting, reactive oxygen species generation, and localized heating. At the molecular level, the review dissects how light-triggered interventions induce oxidative stress, activate apoptotic and autophagic cascades, and modulate immune responses through cytokine profiles and T-cell activity, with relevance not only to cancer but also to cardiovascular, neurodegenerative, metabolic, and autoimmune conditions. The authors critically evaluate regulatory-approved agents and recent clinical trial findings, addressing translational challenges such as nanoparticle optimization, effective light delivery, and regulatory barriers. Overall, the review positions nanoparticle-based phototherapy as a transformative, noninvasive strategy with broad therapeutic potential across chronic and complex diseases.

Cell death in cancer

Marcus Conrad, Andreas Strasser, Philipp J. Jost et al.. Cell death in cancer. Cell. 2026

This review paper examines the diverse programs of cell death and their roles in cancer development, progression, treatment response, and resistance. The authors synthesize knowledge spanning apoptosis and more recently characterized modalities including necroptosis, pyroptosis, and ferroptosis, presenting a unified conceptual framework for how these processes operate in malignancy. While apoptosis remains central to the cell-killing effects of conventional therapies such as radiation and chemotherapy, the review highlights that non-apoptotic programs can drive inflammation and shape interactions among tumor cells, stromal components, and the immune system, thereby influencing immunotherapy outcomes. Ferroptosis receives particular attention as a distinct modality driven by iron-dependent lipid peroxidation that arises from metabolic perturbations rather than a canonical induction signal. The authors argue that fully understanding these cell death programs is essential for overcoming evasion of cell death, a recognized hallmark of cancer, and for revealing new therapeutic opportunities.

Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions

Haojie Dong, Minghui Song, Jingfang Sun et al.. Comprehensive Review on Candidemia: Epidemiology, Diagnosis, Treatment, and Future Directions. iFungi. 2026

This review examines candidemia, a serious nosocomial bloodstream infection caused by *Candida* species, focusing on the challenges that make it difficult to manage effectively in clinical settings. The authors synthesize current knowledge on the epidemiology, species distribution, and origins of candidemia, alongside a critical appraisal of conventional diagnostic methods and their limitations in achieving timely detection. The review evaluates emerging diagnostic technologies, susceptibility-guided antifungal therapy, and therapeutic strategies encompassing empiric and targeted regimens, management of mixed infections, and host-drug interactions. Particular attention is given to antifungal resistance, the potential of combination therapy, and novel agents in development that target previously unexploited fungal pathways. The authors also explore future directions including the application of artificial intelligence in diagnostics, vaccine-based prophylaxis, and synergistic treatment approaches, with the overarching aim of guiding clinicians and researchers in addressing the evolving complexities of candidemia management.

Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications

Yuqing Liu, Kun Zhu, Weijun Peng et al.. Multi-omics and artificial intelligence for precision drug discovery and potential clinical applications. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy. 2026

This review examines how the integration of multiomics technologies and artificial intelligence is reshaping precision drug discovery and translational medicine. The authors synthesize how approaches spanning genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, and metabolomics generate deep molecular insights into disease mechanisms, while advanced deep learning architectures are applied to interpret these complex biological datasets and build predictive models. The review covers the application of this combined framework across the drug development pipeline, including target identification, drug repositioning, de novo compound design, virtual screening, pharmacokinetic modeling, and safety assessment. Case examples drawn from oncology, neurodegenerative diseases, and cardiovascular conditions are used to illustrate the practical potential of these technologies. The authors also acknowledge persistent challenges such as harmonizing disparate omics data streams, ensuring reproducibility, and addressing algorithmic biases, while arguing that the convergence of multiomics and artificial intelligence represents a new era in bench-to-clinic therapeutic development.

QGPINNs: A Physics-Informed Neural Network Framework for Nonlocal Differential Equations on Quantum Graphs

Vaibhav Mehandiratta, Saket Ramchandra. QGPINNs: A Physics-Informed Neural Network Framework for Nonlocal Differential Equations on Quantum Graphs. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces QGPINNs, a physics-informed neural network framework built in PyTorch for solving nonlocal differential equations defined on quantum graphs, which are networks of edges and vertices governed by differential equations coupled through transmission conditions. The framework assigns a separate neural network to each edge of the graph and enforces governing equations alongside boundary, initial, and vertex conditions through a unified graph-based loss function, thereby constructing a globally consistent solution. It targets two classes of nonlinear fractional problems—multi-order fractional elliptic equations and time-fractional evolution equations—and incorporates specialized learning strategies such as dynamic loss balancing, Fourier feature embeddings, and a learnable feature designed to capture the weakly singular behavior typical of fractional-order solutions. The framework is also extended to inverse problems, enabling identification of fractional operator orders and physical parameters from noisy data. Validation on benchmark graph structures and real-world networks, including an electrical power system and an agricultural drainage network, demonstrates the accuracy, computational efficiency, and physical consistency of the approach.

Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective

Akshay Pal, Andrew Lucas, Umang Mehta. Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how electron-electron interactions modify density three-point correlation functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids, focusing on whether these correlations retain their ability to probe the topology of the Fermi sea in the presence of interactions. The authors employ a coadjoint-orbit effective field theory framework, which recasts fermionic loop diagrams as tree-level diagrams in a bosonized language and provides a systematic treatment of generalized Landau interactions. They find that even at linear order in the generalized Landau interaction parameter, a contribution proportional to the band curvature at the Fermi momentum emerges that alters the nonanalytic structure of the free density three-point function. This interaction-induced nonanalytic term is distinct from those appearing in either the noninteracting case or in an interacting Galilean-invariant system, demonstrating that interaction effects can qualitatively change the topology-detecting character of the density three-point correlator.

Continuity equation on metric spaces via measure-valued derivations and BV-Wasserstein curves

Ehsan Abedi, Zhenhao Li, Timo Schultz. Continuity equation on metric spaces via measure-valued derivations and BV-Wasserstein curves. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops a theory of continuity equations on general metric spaces capable of handling curves of probability measures that are of bounded variation with respect to the 1-Wasserstein distance, going beyond the classical absolutely continuous setting. The central innovation is a notion of measure-valued derivations, whose foundational theory is established here, serving as the analytic object that replaces the classical velocity field in the continuity equation. The authors characterize bounded variation curves in the space of probability measures with the extended 1-Wasserstein distance precisely as those satisfying the continuity equation driven by a measure-valued derivation of finite mass, extending earlier work on probabilistic representations of such curves. On Euclidean space, the framework is shown to be consistent with a recently introduced continuity equation for singular fluxes, including its notion of minimal solutions, while on general geodesic metric spaces the probabilistic representations are used to construct the required measure-valued derivations and flux measures explicitly.

Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a machine-learning algorithm for generating lattice field configurations that is guided by the renormalization group. The approach combines a "perfect blocking" construction to supply equilibrated long-distance modes with a conditional normalizing flow that reconstructs short-distance details, followed by brief rethermalization to remove residual errors. By inverting a renormalization group transformation approximately, the method enables recursive cascade upscaling of lattice sizes. Applied to two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, a normalizing flow trained only on small lattices can be reused recursively to generate configurations on much larger lattices while preserving correct long-distance physics.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes and validates a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The method optimizes a blocking transformation under a "perfect blocking" condition so that the coarse-grained distribution is well described by a simple action, then uses a conditional normalizing flow to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom lost during decimation, followed by a brief rethermalization step to correct residual mismatches. A key feature is that the same blocking transformation and learned flow can be applied recursively, enabling a cascade that upscales configurations from a small initial lattice to much larger volumes without retraining. The approach is tested in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, demonstrating stable upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice size on modest computational hardware. Rethermalization tests confirm that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while long-distance (relevant-direction) mismatches relax slowly, and the authors argue the framework naturally extends toward higher-dimensional, gauge, and fermionic theories.

Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness

Lukas Müller, Lukas Woike. Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes a construction of conformal blocks organized into an open-closed modular functor with singularities, starting from a vertex operator algebra and a suitable category of its modules. The approach implements the principle of holomorphic factorization directly, using the strategy of modular extension to build mapping class group representations for surfaces with boundary components carrying specified boundary labels. A key advantage of this construction is that it operates beyond the usual assumptions of rationality, rigidity, self-contragredience, and finiteness that constrain other approaches. The authors show that when the module category is a not necessarily semisimple modular category, their spaces recover string-net spaces and the modular functor for the Drinfeld center, and they prove properties including excision, finite-dimensionality in the cofinite case, and generalizations of surface braid group representations. As a concrete application, they demonstrate that boundary conditions introduced in prior work on the triplet algebra produce well-defined correlation functions within the framework of modular functors with singularities they develop.

Ultrabright PCNs@Coumarin 6-Enhanced Wireless Lateral Flow Immunoassay Platform for Ultrasensitive Detection of Porphyromonas gingivalis.

Zhou M, Shen H, Long Z et al.. Ultrabright PCNs@Coumarin 6-Enhanced Wireless Lateral Flow Immunoassay Platform for Ultrasensitive Detection of Porphyromonas gingivalis.. ACS applied materials & interfaces. 2026

This study developed an ultrasensitive wireless lateral flow immunoassay platform for detecting *Porphyromonas gingivalis*, a key bacterial pathogen linked to periodontal and systemic diseases, addressing limitations of traditional detection methods such as poor sensitivity and procedural complexity. The platform integrated a novel fluorescent metal-organic framework nanocomposite with substantially enhanced fluorescence intensity and prolonged lifetime, combined with a quadruped DNA walker system that amplifies fluorescence signals through enzyme-dependent activity. A self-developed wireless analyzer enabled rapid and accurate interpretation of lateral flow test strips across a wide bacterial concentration range, achieving an exceptionally low detection limit. Validation with clinical saliva samples demonstrated high accuracy and strong resistance to interference, while remaining less costly and more operationally convenient than polymerase chain reaction. The authors conclude that this platform is well-suited for point-of-care testing scenarios including chair-side diagnostics, home self-testing, and large-scale community screening.

Noninvasive SARS-CoV-2 detection using a low-cost electronic nose.

Fialkovitz G, Sousa PL, Hidifira AM et al.. Noninvasive SARS-CoV-2 detection using a low-cost electronic nose.. The Brazilian journal of infectious diseases : an official publication of the Brazilian Society of Infectious Diseases. 2026

This study investigated whether a low-cost, metal-oxide-sensor-based electronic nose could noninvasively detect SARS-CoV-2 infection by analyzing volatile organic compounds in saliva and exhaled breath. The device used an array of commercially available gas sensors paired with several machine learning classification algorithms, including K-Nearest Neighbors, Support Vector Machine, Neural Networks, and Random Forest, to distinguish between infected and non-infected individuals in a real-world hospital cohort. Detection accuracy varied across algorithms and sample types, with exhaled breath generally outperforming saliva, and the K-Nearest Neighbors algorithm achieving the best overall discrimination for both sample types. The findings demonstrate proof-of-concept feasibility for a rapid, point-of-care diagnostic tool that avoids the discomfort and delays associated with conventional testing methods.

Smart wearable and implantable biosensors for continuous health monitoring: materials, biocompatibility, and AI integration

This review examines the current state of smart wearable and implantable biosensors designed for continuous, real-time monitoring of biophysical and biochemical signals in the context of personalized and preventive healthcare. The paper surveys advances in flexible, stretchable, and biocompatible materials that enable long-term comfort and seamless integration with the body, as well as multimodal and multi-analyte sensing approaches that improve measurement robustness. The role of artificial intelligence in enhancing signal processing and generating predictive health insights is also assessed. The review identifies persistent challenges including motion artifacts, energy autonomy, data privacy, and the clinical interpretability of sensor outputs. Overall, the paper synthesizes progress across materials science, device architecture, and AI-assisted strategies to outline the trajectory of biosensor development for health monitoring applications.

Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application

Marcello Mascini, Sara Palmieri, Fabiola Eugelio et al.. Analytical Performances of Polymer-Based Biosensors for Real Samples Application. Biosensors. 2026

This review examined how polymer-based biosensors perform analytically when applied to complex real-world samples across food, environmental, and clinical matrices. The authors conducted a meta-trend analysis of nearly a hundred original research articles spanning a decade, evaluating four polymer classes—conductive polymers, redox-mediator polymers, hydrogels, and molecularly imprinted polymers—across electrochemical and other detection formats. Each polymer class showed distinct strengths, with conductive polymers reducing interference at low operating potentials, hydrogels preserving enzyme function in protein-rich environments, and molecularly imprinted polymers offering robust trace detection in harsh conditions despite slow analyte diffusion. A critical finding was widespread validation deficiency, with the vast majority of studies omitting limits of quantification and substantial fractions lacking reproducibility and precision data. The authors conclude that maintaining calibration across diverse hostile matrices remains the central barrier to commercial deployment, while emerging architectures such as nanocapsulation, hierarchical nanocomposites, and microneedle-integrated systems offer promising routes toward improved fouling resistance and operational stability.

Artificial Intelligence Applications in Medical Devices for Personalized Health Care Solutions: Systematic Review

Hemanth Ponnambalath Mohanadas, A Manikandan, Ahmad Fauzi Ismail et al.. Artificial Intelligence Applications in Medical Devices for Personalized Health Care Solutions: Systematic Review. Journal of Medical Internet Research. 2026

This systematic review examined the scope and impact of artificial intelligence integration in medical devices, with a focus on enabling personalized and precision health care. The authors analyzed peer-reviewed literature, industry analyses, and regulatory advisories spanning several years to assess current applications, implementation barriers, and strategies for overcoming those barriers. The review found meaningful advances across diagnostics, treatment personalization, wearable health monitoring, and smart prosthetics, with AI-based tools demonstrating high accuracy in disease classification and clinical recognition tasks. Key challenges identified include data security vulnerabilities, algorithmic bias, regulatory constraints, and difficulties integrating AI systems into existing health care infrastructures. The authors conclude that while AI-driven personalization holds considerable promise for more adaptive and patient-centered care, realizing its full potential depends on resolving technical, ethical, and regulatory hurdles, and that emerging technologies such as quantum computing and blockchain-based data management may further strengthen AI performance and security in medical applications.

Harmonizing definitions in liquid biopsy: A terminology framework by the international society of liquid biopsy

Eloísa Jantus-Lewintre, Silvia Calabuig-Fariñas, David Gandara et al.. Harmonizing definitions in liquid biopsy: A terminology framework by the international society of liquid biopsy. The Journal of Liquid Biopsy. 2026

The paper addresses the problem of inconsistent terminology in the rapidly growing field of liquid biopsy, which has hampered clear communication, comparison of results, and clinical translation across research and practice. The International Society of Liquid Biopsy developed a comprehensive, harmonized terminology framework to standardize definitions and concepts used throughout the discipline. The framework establishes a formal definition of liquid biopsy encompassing the analysis of cells, nucleic acids, proteins, metabolites, and extracellular vesicles obtained from bodily fluids via minimally invasive methods. It provides rigorous clarification of analyte-specific terms—including circulating free and tumor-derived DNA and RNA, tumor fraction, variant allele frequency, circulating tumor cells, extracellular vesicles, and metabolomic signatures—as well as the analytical methodologies and clinical applications spanning early detection, molecular residual disease assessment, predictive biomarker evaluation, and tumor monitoring. By establishing this cohesive vocabulary, the framework aims to serve as an evolving reference standard to guide the integration of liquid biopsy into precision oncology.

Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions

Adna Hrapović, Nadia Islam, Asmaa Al Bourghli et al.. Biomaterials in personalized drug delivery: innovations, challenges, and future directions. PeerJ. 2026

This review examines the role of biomaterials in drug delivery, focusing on how engineered materials can enable personalized and controlled therapeutic applications. The authors survey a broad range of biomaterial classes—including polymers, metals, ceramics, and nanostructured carriers such as liposomes, micelles, and dendrimers—discussing how tunable properties like degradation kinetics, surface chemistry, and mechanical stability allow precise control over drug release and target-site accumulation. Polymer-based systems enable sustained or stimulus-responsive release that can extend therapeutic exposure and reduce dosing frequency relative to conventional formulations, while nanocarriers improve solubility, cellular uptake, and ligand-mediated site-specific targeting. Despite these advances, the review identifies persistent barriers to clinical translation, including immune–biomaterial interactions, manufacturing variability, unpredictable long-term biodegradation, and regulatory compliance challenges. The authors conclude that future progress requires precision fabrication methods, manufacturing-practice-compatible production pipelines, and biologically informed design strategies that accommodate patient-specific variability to realize the promise of personalized drug delivery.

QGPINNs: A Physics-Informed Neural Network Framework for Nonlocal Differential Equations on Quantum Graphs

Vaibhav Mehandiratta, Saket Ramchandra. QGPINNs: A Physics-Informed Neural Network Framework for Nonlocal Differential Equations on Quantum Graphs. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces QGPINNs, a physics-informed neural network framework built in PyTorch for solving nonlocal differential equations defined on quantum graphs, which are metric graphs equipped with differential operators on each edge. The approach assigns a separate neural network to each edge of the graph and enforces the governing equations alongside continuity, Kirchhoff-Neumann, and Dirichlet conditions through a unified graph-based loss function, coupling local edge-wise approximations into a coherent global solution. The framework targets two classes of nonlinear fractional models—multi-order fractional elliptic problems and time-fractional evolution equations—and incorporates specialized training strategies such as dynamic loss balancing, Fourier feature embeddings, and a learnable singularity-capturing feature to handle the weakly singular solutions these problems can produce. Continuity equation on metric spaces via measure-valued derivations and BV-Wasserstein curves

Ehsan Abedi, Zhenhao Li, Timo Schultz. Continuity equation on metric spaces via measure-valued derivations and BV-Wasserstein curves. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops a theory of continuity equations on general metric spaces, capable of describing curves of probability measures that are absolutely continuous or of bounded variation with respect to the 1-Wasserstein distance. The authors introduce the notion of measure-valued derivations as the central analytical tool, building their basic theory and using it to formulate the continuity equation in this abstract setting. They show that on Euclidean space their framework is consistent with a previously introduced continuity equation with singular flux, including the associated notion of minimal solutions. A key result characterizes curves of bounded variation in the space of probability measures equipped with the extended 1-Wasserstein distance as precisely those curves satisfying the continuity equation driven by a measure-valued derivation of finite mass. To establish this characterization, the authors extend their prior work on probabilistic representations of bounded-variation curves and construct explicit measure-valued derivations and flux measures on geodesic metric spaces and Euclidean space, respectively.

Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a machine-learning approach to generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting renormalization-group transformations. The method combines a "perfect blocking" construction to supply equilibrated long-distance modes with a conditional normalizing flow that reconstructs short-distance details, followed by brief rethermalization to correct residual errors. By training the normalizing flow at relatively small lattice sizes and then applying it recursively in a cascade, the method achieves scalable generation of configurations at much larger lattice sizes. Applied to two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, the approach correctly reproduces long-distance physics at lattice sizes far beyond those used in training, demonstrating that the learned flow generalizes across scales through the recursive cascade procedure.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes a lattice field theory generation algorithm that inverts a renormalization-group blocking transformation to efficiently produce configurations across multiple length scales. The approach optimizes a blocking transformation under a "perfect blocking" condition so that the resulting coarse-lattice distribution is well described by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance modes lost in decimation. A brief rethermalization step corrects residual mismatches between the generated and target distributions. The method operates recursively, reusing the same blocking transformation and learned flow to upscale configurations in a cascade from a small initial ensemble to much larger lattices. Testing in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality demonstrates stable cascade upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume on local computational resources, with rethermalization tests confirming that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while long-distance mismatches relax slowly, consistent with renormalization-group expectations.

Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness

Lukas Müller, Lukas Woike. Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes a construction of spaces of conformal blocks for a vertex operator algebra and a suitable category of its modules, organized into an open-closed modular functor with singularities. The approach is built around the principle of holomorphic factorization, using a modular extension strategy to assign mapping class group representations to surfaces with boundary components and specified boundary labels, with the construction described explicitly on generating Dehn twists. A key advantage is that the framework operates beyond the usual requirements of rationality, rigidity, self-contragredience, and finiteness that constrain other approaches. The authors prove that their construction satisfies excision, is finite-dimensional in the cofinite case, and in the special case where the module category is a modular category, the resulting spaces are equivalent to string-net spaces and the modular functor for the corresponding Drinfeld center. As a concrete application, they verify that certain boundary conditions for the triplet algebra, a logarithmic conformal field theory with non-exact fusion product, yield well-defined correlation functions within the developed framework.

Super-resolution Control of Two-dimensional Quantum Emitters

Bosai Lyu, Valeria Vento, Ludivine Fausten et al.. Super-resolution Control of Two-dimensional Quantum Emitters. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper studied localized interlayer excitons in semiconducting transition-metal dichalcogenide heterobilayers, which act as quantum emitters and nanoscale charge sensors capable of probing correlated quantum phases. The researchers combined cryogenic optical spectroscopy with scanning probe microscopy to investigate trapped interlayer excitons in WSe₂/MoSe₂ bilayers. By exploiting an AFM-based local Stark shift, they achieved super-resolution localization of emitters separated by only a few tens of nanometers and demonstrated deterministic control of individual charge states, including trion formation. Time-resolved measurements further revealed that the scanning tip modifies the electromagnetic vacuum around individual emitters, thereby controlling their radiative emission rates. The authors propose that this multi-point charge sensing platform with optical readout is well-suited for studying exotic quantum phenomena such as fractionalization and anyon dynamics in semiconducting fractional Chern insulators.

Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.

Hatem H, Mysara M, Ramadan R. Trends of nucleic acid - based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases.. Journal of biological engineering. 2026

This review paper examines the landscape of nucleic acid-based point-of-care diagnostics for infectious diseases, motivated by the need for rapid, decentralized testing solutions beyond conventional laboratory settings. The authors organized their analysis around the three core operational steps of such diagnostics: nucleic acid extraction, amplification, and detection. For extraction, they assessed approaches including magnetic bead-based, paper-based, and integrated microfluidic methods, evaluating their suitability for simplified and equipment-free deployment. Amplification strategies reviewed include both thermal cycling methods such as PCR and isothermal alternatives such as LAMP, NASBA, and RPA, all of which have been adapted for portable and low-power conditions. On the detection side, the review spans colorimetric and fluorescent lateral flow assays through to advanced biosensor platforms exploiting CRISPR/Cas systems and toehold switch principles, highlighting the trajectory toward increasingly sensitive and programmable nucleic acid recognition at the point of care.

Detection of Viral Nucleic Acid in Specimens Spotted on Commercial Filter Papers: A Review and Meta-Analysis.

Armenta-Leyva B, Munguía-Ramírez B, Kuennen B et al.. Detection of Viral Nucleic Acid in Specimens Spotted on Commercial Filter Papers: A Review and Meta-Analysis.. Viruses. 2026

This paper reviewed and statistically synthesized the published literature on using commercial filter papers to collect, transport, and store biological samples for viral nucleic acid detection across human and veterinary pathogens. Following established systematic review guidelines, the authors searched multiple databases, identified eligible studies spanning dozens of filter paper types, and applied multilevel meta-analysis to calculate and compare nucleic acid detection rates. They found that detection rates varied substantially both by filter paper type and by virus target, with cellulose-based products such as Whatman and FTA matrices dominating the literature while polyester and glass fiber substrates were also represented. Experimental conditions including storage temperature, drying time, and humidity were inconsistently reported across studies yet appeared to influence viral nucleic acid recovery. The authors concluded that filter paper-based viral nucleic acid detection is highly variable and called for more rigorously designed comparative studies conducted across a defined range of conditions.

2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Group A Streptococcal Pharyngitis: Risk Assessment Using Clinical Scoring Systems in Children and Adults.

Linder JA, Watson ME, Wessels MR et al.. 2025 Clinical Practice Guideline Update by the Infectious Diseases Society of America on Group A Streptococcal Pharyngitis: Risk Assessment Using Clinical Scoring Systems in Children and Adults.. Clinical infectious diseases : an official publication of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. 2026

This guideline update from the Infectious Diseases Society of America examined whether clinical scoring systems should be used to guide diagnostic testing decisions for group A streptococcal pharyngitis in children and adults. The authors conducted a systematic review of randomized and nonrandomized studies comparing the diagnostic accuracy of standardized clinical scoring systems — which quantify infection probability based on features such as fever, tonsillar findings, lymphadenopathy, and absence of cough — against clinician judgment alone in predicting throat culture results. The evidence suggests that clinical scoring systems perform comparably to or slightly better than clinician judgment, though the available studies are limited by small sample sizes, inconsistent outcome measures, and incomplete data. Based on this review, the guideline panel concluded that the benefits of using a clinical scoring system outweigh the harms, recommending its incorporation into patient evaluation for sore throat. The primary value of such scoring systems lies in identifying patients with a low probability of infection, thereby reducing unnecessary diagnostic testing.

The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Mahmoud M. Dahab et al.. The WHO priority list of antibiotic-resistant bacteria: challenges and opportunities for next-generation antimicrobial development. Frontiers in Pharmacology. 2026

This narrative review examines the global challenge of antimicrobial resistance with a focus on pathogens designated as priorities by the World Health Organization. The authors synthesize current understanding of the molecular mechanisms by which these bacteria evade existing antibiotics, encompassing reduced membrane permeability, efflux pumps, enzymatic inactivation, target modification, biofilm formation, and horizontal gene transfer. The review critically evaluates why conventional antibiotic discovery pipelines have failed to keep pace with resistance and assesses a range of emerging therapeutic strategies—including artificial intelligence-assisted drug discovery, phage therapy, antimicrobial peptides, CRISPR-based systems, and natural product-derived compounds—with attention to their pharmacological feasibility, toxicity, delivery challenges, and regulatory hurdles. The authors also address the clinical and economic burden imposed by resistant infections and emphasize that antimicrobial stewardship, surveillance, and a One Health framework integrating human, animal, and environmental sectors are essential complements to new drug development. The overarching conclusion is that tackling priority pathogens will require integrated, multidisciplinary approaches spanning molecular biology, pharmacology, clinical translation, and public health.

Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances

Pranay Amruth Maroju, Angad S. Sidhu, Amogh R. Motaganahalli et al.. Detection to Disruption: A Comprehensive Review of Bacterial Biofilms and Therapeutic Advances. Antibiotics. 2026

This review examines the biology, clinical significance, and treatment of bacterial biofilms, which are structured microbial communities embedded in a self-produced extracellular polymeric substance matrix. The authors synthesize current knowledge on how biofilms develop, how their matrix composition and physiological heterogeneity protect bacteria from both antimicrobials and host immune defenses, and how specific resistance mechanisms—including limited antibiotic diffusion, efflux pump upregulation, persister cell formation, and horizontal gene transfer—make these infections exceptionally difficult to eradicate. The review maps the clinical contexts in which biofilms are most problematic, spanning chronic wounds, cystic fibrosis, osteomyelitis, device-associated infections, and breast implant illness, where persistent biofilm-driven inflammation may contribute to systemic manifestations. The authors also survey conventional and emerging detection methods alongside therapeutic strategies that pair antibiotics with matrix-disrupting enzymes, quorum-sensing inhibitors, bacteriophages, metabolic reactivators, and nanotechnology-based delivery systems. They conclude that effective management of biofilm-associated disease requires integrated detection and multifaceted treatment approaches, and that advances in multi-omics and systems-level modeling will be critical for developing next-generation interventions.

Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies

Carsyn R. Travis, Jared McMaster, Fatima Rivas. Advances in Natural Product Extraction: Established and Emerging Technologies. Molecules. 2026

This review examines recent advances in the extraction and isolation of natural products, motivated by the renewed prominence of nature-derived compounds in drug discovery. The authors surveyed progress across a range of established and emerging technologies, including green and designer solvent systems, pressurized and ultrasound-assisted extraction platforms, supercritical fluid techniques, and integrated purification workflows. Particular attention is given to the application of artificial intelligence and machine learning for solvent selection, extraction optimization, and metabolite dereplication, which together with advanced phase-separation and informatic tools have expanded the range of detectable metabolites in complex biological matrices. The review concludes that combinatorial extraction strategies and next-generation solvent systems have collectively improved operational efficiency, selectivity, compound recovery, and analytical throughput over the recent period surveyed.

Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications

Emad M. Abdallah, Abdulrahman Mohammed Alhudhaibi, Ibrahim Mohammed Hussaini et al.. Harnessing biogenic nanoparticles for combating antibiotic resistance: green synthesis, mechanistic insights, and biotechnological applications. Frontiers in Bioengineering and Biotechnology. 2026

This review examines biogenic nanoparticles as a potential strategy to address the growing global crisis of antimicrobial resistance, focusing on materials synthesized through biological processes using plants, microorganisms, algae, or isolated biomolecules. The authors surveyed recent advances in green synthesis approaches, describing how biological metabolites govern metal ion reduction, nucleation, and stabilization, which in turn determine nanoparticle size, morphology, and antimicrobial properties. A range of metal and metal oxide nanoparticles were reviewed for their antibacterial activity against WHO-priority and ESKAPE pathogens, with multiple proposed mechanisms including membrane disruption, reactive oxygen species generation, biofilm suppression, and quorum sensing interference. The review also covers their experimental use as adjuncts to antibiotics or phytochemicals, where synergistic effects may lower required drug concentrations, as well as applications in wound care, drug delivery, biosensing, and food packaging. The authors conclude that while biogenic nanoparticles show considerable promise, substantial obstacles including inconsistent synthesis protocols, limited scalability, and insufficient toxicological and environmental safety data must be resolved before clinical or regulatory advancement is feasible.

Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective

Akshay Pal, Andrew Lucas, Umang Mehta. Interaction corrections to topological density three-point functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids: a coadjoint orbit perspective. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigates how electron-electron interactions affect density three-point correlation functions in two-dimensional Fermi liquids, which are known to encode topological information about the Fermi sea in the noninteracting limit. The authors employ the coadjoint-orbit effective field theory framework, which allows Landau interactions to be incorporated systematically by mapping fermionic loop diagrams onto tree-level calculations in a bosonized description. They find that even at linear order in the generalized Landau interaction parameter, a contribution proportional to the band curvature at the Fermi momentum emerges that alters the nonanalytic structure of the free density three-point function. This new nonanalytic structure is distinct from both the noninteracting case and the interacting Galilean-invariant limit, demonstrating that interactions can qualitatively modify the topology-detecting character of the density three-point correlator. The results indicate that the relationship between these correlation functions and Fermi sea topology is more subtle in the presence of interactions than previously appreciated.

Bounds for inertialess dynamo

Ali Arslan, Hezekiah Grayer. Bounds for inertialess dynamo. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper derives rigorous mathematical necessary conditions that must be satisfied for dynamo action to occur in rotating convective systems. The authors analyze a magnetohydrodynamic model in two settings: a rapidly rotating plane layer where inertia and viscosity are neglected, and a more general configuration with finite viscosity, in both cases coupling the magnetic field evolution to an inertialess force balance rather than treating the velocity field as kinematically prescribed. They establish that the buoyancy-driven component of the flow must exceed a threshold involving the magnetic Reynolds number and a norm of the flow field, expressed as an inequality over a range of Lebesgue norms, with a refined version using an anisotropic norm derived from poloidal-toroidal decomposition. For the viscous case, necessary conditions are also obtained for the growth of magnetic enstrophy and combined thermo-magnetic energy, with one branch of constraints implying a specific scaling relationship between the Rayleigh number and Ekman number that must hold for dynamo action to be possible.

Layer-Controlled Intermolecular Coupling and Many-Body Effects in C$_{60}$ Films

Hai-Lan Luo, Weitang Li, Luca Moreschini et al.. Layer-Controlled Intermolecular Coupling and Many-Body Effects in C$_{60}$ Films. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper investigated how electronic properties of crystalline C₆₀ molecular thin films evolve as a function of film thickness, from the monolayer limit to the bulk. Using angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy combined with Holstein-model simulations, the researchers systematically characterized the band structure across this thickness range. They found a non-monotonic evolution of the highest occupied molecular orbital-derived band, with intermediate-thickness films exhibiting larger bandwidth, reduced effective mass, and pronounced gap-like and sub-band features compared to both thinner and thicker films. These observations were attributed to strengthened intermolecular electronic coupling and enhanced electron-phonon-induced spectral renormalization specifically in the intermediate-thickness regime. The study identifies a dimensional crossover in C₆₀ films and establishes layer number as a tunable parameter for controlling electronic structure and many-body interactions in molecular thin films, offering a complementary approach to the twist-angle tuning used in moiré van der Waals heterostructures.

Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided cascade upscaling for lattice field generation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper introduces a machine-learning algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group transformation. The method combines a "perfect blocking" construction to supply equilibrated long-distance modes with a conditional normalizing flow that reconstructs short-distance details, followed by brief rethermalization to correct residual errors. The approach is applied recursively in a cascade scheme, where a normalizing flow trained on small lattices is reused to generate progressively larger lattices. Testing on two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality demonstrates that the method successfully reproduces correct long-distance physics at lattice sizes far exceeding those used during training, showcasing strong generalization across scales.

Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation

Anna Hasenfratz, Ethan T. Neil, Letizia Parato et al.. Renormalization-guided inverse blocking for lattice field generation: construction and validation. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper proposes and validates a new algorithm for generating lattice field configurations by approximately inverting a renormalization-group blocking transformation. The approach optimizes the blocking using a perfect blocking condition so that the coarse-grained lattice distribution is captured by a simple action, then separates the blocking into an invertible smoothing step followed by decimation, with a conditional normalizing flow used to reconstruct the short-distance degrees of freedom lost during decimation. A brief fine-action rethermalization corrects residual mismatches, and the procedure can be applied recursively, cascading upward from a small initial ensemble to much larger lattices by reusing the same blocking transformation and trained flow. The method was tested in two-dimensional scalar field theory at criticality, successfully demonstrating stable cascade upscaling across several orders of magnitude in lattice volume using only local computational resources. Rethermalization tests confirm that short-distance mismatches relax quickly while mismatches in the relevant thermal direction relax slowly, consistent with expected renormalization-group behavior, and the authors argue the framework extends naturally toward higher-dimensional, gauge, and fermionic systems.

Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness

Lukas Müller, Lukas Woike. Modular Functors with Singularities from Vertex Operator Algebras Beyond Rigidity and Finiteness. arXiv. 2026 preprint

This paper develops a construction of conformal blocks associated to a vertex operator algebra and an appropriate category of its modules, organized into a mathematical structure called an open-closed modular functor with singularities. The authors implement the principle of holomorphic factorization directly, using a modular extension strategy to assign mapping class group representations to surfaces with boundary components and labeled boundary data, with the action describable explicitly on generating Dehn twists. In the special case where the module category is a not necessarily semisimple modular category, the resulting spaces are shown to be equivalent to string-net spaces and hence to the modular functor for the associated Drinfeld center, connecting the construction to established topological and algebraic-geometric approaches. A key advantage of the framework is that it extends beyond the usual requirements of rationality, rigidity, self-contragredience, and finiteness, and the authors prove that the construction satisfies excision, is finite-dimensional in the cofinite case, and yields representations of surface braid groups. As an application, they demonstrate for a specific non-semisimple example, the triplet algebra with non-exact fusion product, that physically motivated boundary conditions give rise to well-defined correlation functions within the developed notion of a modular functor with singularities.

Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.

Brzychczy-Włoch M, Dobrut A, Drożdż K et al.. Innovative epitope-based ELISA assay for detecting Group B Streptococcus carriage in pregnant women.. Applied microbiology and biotechnology. 2026

This study investigated synthetic peptide epitopes derived from *Streptococcus agalactiae* proteins as components of an ELISA-based immunodiagnostic test for detecting Group B Streptococcus infection and carriage in pregnant women. The researchers selected three epitopes from the bacterial proteins GroEL, enolase, and EF-Tu, and evaluated their ability to distinguish between GBS-positive and GBS-negative serum samples using IgG, IgM, and IgA antibody detection. All three synthetic epitopes, both individually and in combination, significantly differentiated between positive and negative sera, with the enolase-derived and EF-Tu-derived peptides performing best as individual candidates due to their higher immunoreactivity at lower concentrations. Notably, combining the epitopes in various mixtures further improved overall immunoreactivity compared to single-peptide use. The authors propose this epitope-based ELISA prototype as a promising alternative to the current culture-based gold standard, which is slow and sometimes yields ambiguous results.

Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.

Sberna G, D'Arezzo S, Valli MB et al.. Bridging the diagnostic gap: the role of culture methods in the era of syndromic panels for central nervous system infections.. Frontiers in cellular and infection microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of a syndromic molecular panel for central nervous system infections by comparing it against conventional culture methods using cerebrospinal fluid samples collected over three years at a national reference center. The researchers analyzed concordance, sensitivity, specificity, and predictive values across a large dataset of paired molecular and culture results. They found moderate overall agreement between the two approaches, with the syndromic panel demonstrating high specificity but limited sensitivity when all culture-identified pathogens were considered; however, restricting analysis to bacterial targets actually included in the panel substantially improved both agreement and sensitivity, with a very high negative predictive value. Crucially, conventional culture identified clinically relevant organisms absent from the panel's target list, including pathogens of clear therapeutic importance, underscoring that the syndromic panel cannot serve as a standalone diagnostic tool. The authors conclude that optimal management of central nervous system infections requires systematic integration of syndromic molecular testing with conventional culture, and advocate for next-generation multiplex platforms with broader pathogen coverage guided by real-world epidemiological data.

Performance of the LIAISON PLEX gram-positive blood culture assay for identifying bacterial pathogens and resistance genes in blood cultures.

Chow S-K, Stevig C, Granato P et al.. Performance of the LIAISON PLEX gram-positive blood culture assay for identifying bacterial pathogens and resistance genes in blood cultures.. Journal of clinical microbiology. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance of the LIAISON PLEX Gram-Positive Blood Culture Assay, a multiplexed molecular test designed to rapidly identify gram-positive bacterial pathogens and antibiotic resistance markers directly from positive blood culture bottles. The assay targets thirteen gram-positive bacterial species and four antibiotic resistance markers, and its performance was assessed across a large, diverse collection of prospective clinical, pre-selected, and contrived blood culture samples at multiple sites. The assay demonstrated very high overall sensitivity and specificity for all targets, with a near-perfect valid test rate, and showed broad inclusivity across tested bacterial strains with only minimal cross-reactivity among off-panel species. Performance was maintained in the presence of potential interfering substances, across blood culture bottles from multiple manufacturers, and in polymicrobial infection scenarios. The authors conclude that the assay offers fast, accurate, and reliable detection of gram-positive organisms and their resistance genes from positive blood cultures, with potential benefits for antibiotic stewardship and patient management.

Utility and clinical relevance of multiplex PCR panels for diagnosis of central nervous system infections.

Gürler M, Karaoğlu Ç, Yurtcu EE et al.. Utility and clinical relevance of multiplex PCR panels for diagnosis of central nervous system infections.. The Indian journal of medical research. 2026

This study evaluated the diagnostic performance and real-world clinical utility of multiplex polymerase chain reaction panels designed to detect multiple pathogens simultaneously from cerebrospinal fluid in patients with suspected meningitis or encephalitis. Researchers retrospectively reviewed samples tested over a five-year period at a tertiary care hospital, examining test results alongside clinical data and subsequent management decisions. Viral pathogens dominated the positive findings, with human herpesvirus-6, enterovirus, and herpes simplex virus type-1 most frequently detected, while Streptococcus pneumoniae was the leading bacterial pathogen identified. Despite the panels' capacity for rapid, broad pathogen detection, fewer than half of positive results were associated with a documented change in diagnosis or treatment, and repeat testing after an initial negative result added little diagnostic value. The authors conclude that inappropriate test utilisation and insufficient clinician familiarity with panel contents limit the clinical impact of these tools, and that clearer testing criteria are needed to improve diagnostic efficiency.

Validation of an in-house developed rapid assay for Group B Streptococcus detection in pregnancy.

Hu S, Xie J, Liu L et al.. Validation of an in-house developed rapid assay for Group B Streptococcus detection in pregnancy.. International journal of medical microbiology : IJMM. 2026

This study addressed the need for a rapid and practical method to detect Group B Streptococcus colonization in pregnant women, a key risk factor for neonatal infections and preterm stillbirth. The researchers developed and validated an in-house assay combining CRISPR/Cas12a with recombinase polymerase amplification, an isothermal technique that eliminates the need for conventional thermal cycling. The assay was evaluated for specificity against other common female reproductive tract pathogens, and its limit of detection was established using serial dilutions of GBS-derived plasmids. Clinical performance was assessed using a large collection of vaginal-rectal swabs from pregnant women, with results compared against a commercial quantitative real-time PCR method. The assay completed testing within one hour, demonstrated high sensitivity and specificity, and showed strong concordance with the established PCR reference, supporting its potential for routine clinical use.

point-of-care nucleic acid amplification test infectious disease
